# Instellars Banking CDH Demo — Developer Handover Document

**Purpose of this document:** full context for a fresh chat/session picking up this project. Written by the assistant that just finished a long build session, for itself (or another instance) starting cold in a new conversation. Facts below were verified directly against the actual shipped files, not recalled from memory alone.

---

## 1. What This Project Is

A demo application showcasing **Pega Customer Decision Hub (CDH)** — real-time, cross-channel offer personalization — inside a fictional retail bank, "Instellars." It exists to demonstrate a customer's journey across **three different surfaces** (mobile app, website, human agent's desktop), all sharing the same underlying customer data, journey state, and live Pega API calls.

There are **two separate deliverables** that are kept in sync:

| File | What it is |
|---|---|
| `instellars-cdh-demo.html` | A **standalone, single-file** mobile app — a phone-frame mockup rendered entirely client-side. No external JS files; everything (data, CDH logic, session logic, UI) is inlined in one HTML file. |
| `instellars-banking-site.zip` | A **multi-page website** — separate HTML pages sharing common JS modules (`js/data.js`, `js/cdh.js`, `js/session.js`, `js/behavioral.js`, `js/render-shared.js`) and `css/app.css`. Includes `agent-desktop.html` (the human agent's view) and `mobile-app.html` (an **embedded copy** of the standalone mobile app, launched from the website's "Mobile Experience" panel). |

**Critical convention:** `instellars-cdh-demo.html` (standalone) and `mobile-app.html` (embedded inside the zip) must always be byte-identical. Every time the mobile app changes, both copies get updated and shipped together.

**⚠ Update (later session, working from the unzipped `instellars-banking-site` folder directly rather than the original zip/standalone pair): this convention had already drifted before that session started.** `instellars-cdh-demo.html` (found separately in the parent Downloads folder) predates the entire explainability/rule-trace feature set (§10, §29–34) — it has no `EligibilityDescription`/`ApplicabilityDescription`/`SuitabilityDescription` handling at all — and differs visually from `mobile-app.html` (different `--brass` color value, different body background, missing markup) independent of any of that session's own edits. Every fix that session made to `mobile-app.html` was mirrored there and nowhere else; `instellars-cdh-demo.html` was deliberately left alone rather than guessing at a resync. Whoever picks this up next should treat the two files as already out of sync and confirm directly before assuming otherwise — don't trust this convention line without checking.

## 2. Where the Files Currently Live

- `/mnt/user-data/outputs/instellars-cdh-demo.html`
- `/mnt/user-data/outputs/instellars-banking-site.zip`
  - Unzips to a folder `instellars-banking-site/` containing: `browse.html`, `login.html`, `dashboard.html`, `journey.html`, `new-application.html`, `emi-calculator.html`, `mobile-app.html`, `agent-desktop.html`, `index.html`, `css/app.css`, `js/data.js`, `js/cdh.js`, `js/session.js`, `js/behavioral.js`, `js/render-shared.js`

An architecture PPTX also exists at `/mnt/user-data/outputs/instellars-architecture.pptx` (not touched recently — likely stale relative to the current build).

### ⚠️ Known infrastructure issue: intermittent I/O errors on `/mnt/user-data/outputs`

Multiple times in the last session, `rm`/`cp` against this mounted directory failed with `Input/output error` or silently timed out — and once, this caused a **stale zip to get shipped** without the intended fix, undetected until directly re-checked. The reliable pattern that recovered from this every time:

1. Check health first: `timeout 15 ls /mnt/user-data/outputs/`
2. Build the final zip **entirely inside the sandbox** (e.g. `/home/claude/`), never directly in the output mount.
3. Verify the *sandbox* zip's contents are correct (`unzip -l`, `grep` for the expected code) before touching the output mount at all.
4. Ship via `cp` (not `rm` + `cp`, not `mv`) — copy the new file over the old one directly.
5. **Always verify post-ship** with `md5sum` on both the sandbox copy and the shipped copy — they must match.
6. As a final check, `unzip` the *actual shipped file* fresh into a new temp directory and run a real functional test against it (spin up `python3 -m http.server`, drive it with Playwright) — never trust that shipping succeeded just because the `cp` command returned exit 0.

## 3. Core Architecture

### 3.1 Customer data model

All customers live in a `CUSTOMERS` array (`js/data.js` on the website; inlined in the mobile app). Two customers have **centralized ID constants** specifically so their IDs can be changed in one place:

```js
const PRIYA_CUSTOMER_ID = "CUST792";   // Robert Smith (renamed from "Priya Nair" earlier in the project)
const MAYA_CUSTOMER_ID  = "CUST201";   // Maya Torres
```

Every other reference to these two customers throughout both apps uses these constants — never a hardcoded string. This was tested repeatedly by changing the ID value and confirming every dependent feature (offers, interest-rate lookup, journey eligibility, Brevo calls, Agent Desktop) picked up the new value automatically.

**Current customer reference table:**

| Name | ID | Segment | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| Aria Chen | `CUST101` | Premier | creditScore 790 |
| Sam Okafor | `CUST44` | New-to-bank | Own journey ("New Customer Acquisition"); has the Brevo Automation demo card |
| **Robert Smith** | `CUST792` | Mass Retail | `PRIYA_CUSTOMER_ID`. Was "Priya Nair" / `CUST79` / `CUST791` earlier — renamed and re-ID'd multiple times during this project |
| Diego Alvarez | `C1004` | Emerging Affluent | 3 accounts (Savings, Checking, Time Deposit). Segment was "Small Business" until §45's follow-up |
| **Maya Torres** | `CUST201` | New Account | `MAYA_CUSTOMER_ID`. Was `CUST10` earlier. `tenureYears: 1`, `relationshipLength: "1 year"` (both changed together — see §5) |

### 3.2 Journey system

Each customer is on a **journey** with **stages**, each stage having a fixed set of expected offer actions:

- **Personal Loan & Card Cross-Sell** (`loan-card-crosssell`) — Robert (always) + Maya (togglable via `MAYA_ACTIVE_JOURNEY_ID` constant). Stages: `Prospect → Consideration → Conversion → Deepening`.
- **New Customer Acquisition** — Sam's journey.
- **Digital Activation & Onboarding** — Maya's alternate journey (when the toggle is flipped away from loan-card-crosssell).

Key helpers: `isLoanCardJourneyCustomer(id)`, `journeyMatchesCustomer(journey, customerId)`.

Stage advancement rule: accepting the specific "gate" offer for the current stage (e.g. "In-Session Loan Calculator" at Prospect) advances to the next stage. This is checked via `normalizeActionTitle()` matching, not offer ID.

### 3.3 Pega CDH API integrations (verified endpoints)

All hosted at `instellars02.pegalabs.io`, except the lending-specific rate lookup:

| Endpoint | Purpose | Identity field used |
|---|---|---|
| `POST /prweb/api/PegaMKTContainer/v4/container` | Fetch NBA offers | `SubjectID` (logged-in) or `ExternalID` (anonymous) |
| `POST /prweb/api/PegaMKTContainer/v4/capture_response` | Log impressions/accept/reject | Same as above |
| `POST /prweb/api/PegaCDHIDMerge/v1/IDMerge` | Tie anonymous cookie to real customer at login | `PrimaryID` (customer id), `SecondaryID` (cookie) |
| `POST /prweb/api/BehavioralData/01.01/Insert` | Page-interest logging | `CookieID` |
| `POST /prweb/api/MessageStatus/v1/CustomerID` | Fired specifically on **Robert's** journey-stage transitions (Consideration/Conversion/Deepening, via `INST_JOURNEY_STAGE_NAMES`) — in addition to, not instead of, everything else that already happens on those transitions | `CustomerID`, `InstJourney` (stage name string, e.g. `"PersonalLoanCardCrossSellConsideration"`) |
| `POST https://cdhfslending.pegademo.com/prweb/api/PegaMKTContainer/V4/container` | Interest-rate lookup (EMI Calculator, New App wizard) | `SubjectID` — per-customer `SAMPLECON-*` values in `INTEREST_RATE_SUBJECT_IDS` |

External, non-Pega integrations:
- **Brevo** (`api.brevo.com/v3/events`) — fires `pega_nba_decision` custom event. Used for Sam's "Brevo Automation" demo card, and for Robert (see §5 — moved from Deepening to Consideration stage).
- **Twilio Segment** (`cdn.segment.com`) — real `analytics.identify(customer.id, {...})` call. Active on **`mobile-app.html`** (loaded on every page view) and on `new-application.html`, only at the wizard's final step (Confirmation/Abandoned) — disabled/commented out on every other website page (`login.html`, `dashboard.html`, `journey.html`, etc.). Not related to `BehavioralData` (a Pega endpoint) despite similar-sounding names — confirmed these are two completely separate integrations on two different domains.
- **Supabase** (`zqfbuqkhkxooeipllmhw.supabase.co`, table `events`) — powers the dashboard's "Event Store" panel, reading back Segment-tracked events (e.g. Sam's "Abandoned Application" entries). Falls back to `SAMPLE_EVENT_STORE_ENTRIES` with a "couldn't reach the event store" note when the read fails. Implemented in `js/cdh.js` → `fetchSupabaseEvents()`, rendered in `dashboard.html`. **This integration was not documented in earlier versions of this handover** — found by direct grep of every `fetch()`/network call site in the codebase, not from memory of the build session.

### 3.4 ⚠️ Known limitation: CORS blocks all live Pega calls from this sandbox

Confirmed directly, repeatedly, via browser console:

```
Access to fetch at 'https://instellars02.pegalabs.io/...' from origin 'http://localhost:XXXXX'
has been blocked by CORS policy: Response to preflight request doesn't pass access control
check: No 'Access-Control-Allow-Origin' header is present on the requested resource.
```

- Works fine from **Postman** (no CORS enforcement in non-browser HTTP clients).
- Fails from **any browser context** unless Pega's side is configured to allow that specific origin.
- This is **not fixable client-side** — the browser refuses to let JS see the response at all; the actual reason is never exposed to `catch` blocks, only visible in DevTools console.
- The **logged-in dashboard silently falls back** to a local mock offer catalog when this happens, and — this is a known, not-yet-fixed issue — its "Live" badge still says **"Live"** even while showing fallback data, because that badge is actually tied to a separate manual "Simulate outage" toggle, not to whether the real call succeeded. Flagged to the user as "arguably a real bug worth fixing on its own" — **not yet addressed**. *(Update: fixed in §14, added in a later session.)*
- **⚠ Update (later session): this is not universally true.** A later session's browser environment could reach `instellars02.pegalabs.io` directly with zero CORS errors — confirmed repeatedly, including a live `container` call for Aria that returned real data (§35 below). Whether CORS blocks a given session depends on the actual browser/origin/sandbox it's running in, not on anything about this app. **Don't assume either way — test a direct `fetch()` call first**, each new session, before deciding whether to build against live data or the mock fallback.

### 3.5 Anonymous visitor tracking

- `getOrCreateAnonymousCookieId()` (in `js/session.js`) generates `CookieID-{timestamp}{random}`, stored in `localStorage` under `instellars_anonymous_cookie_id`. Persists across all pages that load `js/session.js`.
- This same cookie ID is reused consistently across: the `container` call's `ExternalID`, `capture_response`'s `SubjectID`/`ExternalID`, `BehavioralData`'s `CookieID`, and `IDMerge`'s `SecondaryID` at login — confirmed identical across all four in live testing.

### 3.6 Cross-app / cross-tab sync

- `SHARED_SESSION_KEY` in `localStorage` holds shared session state, readable by any same-origin tab (website pages + mobile app).
- `sessionLogUpdated` custom event lets independently-rendering pages repaint after another tab writes to shared state.
- `writeSharedLoanDisbursed()`, `writeSharedCurrentStage()` — specific cross-app state writers for loan status and journey stage.

## 4. Features Implemented This Session (chronological)

1. **Agent Desktop full redesign** — matched to a user-provided reference screenshot (D365-style layout): dark navy chrome, "Active Contacts" sidebar (replacing the old icon-only nav rail), 5-metric-card row (Credit Score/DTI/Balance/Risk Flag/Loan Accounts), reordered Business Process Flow (Onboard→Qualify→Engage→Grow→Advocate), simplified tabs, "Accounts Overview" table + "Financial Snapshot" section (replacing generic Contact/Account Info), new "Compliance Flags" panel (KYC/AML/Financially Vulnerable/Days Past Due, dynamically color-coded), "Pega CDH" pill badge, section-header icons. Then a follow-up "sharper" pass (smaller fonts/icons/padding across the board). Then removed the dark "Customer Profile" banner entirely per a follow-up request. Then made "Interaction History" its own tab (previously always-visible under Summary) — added `switchFormTab()`. Then made it responsive at ≤860px width (hide contacts sidebar, stack side panel below main content) since the table was overflowing/cropping at narrow widths.

2. **Maya's ID and tenure changed**: `CUST10 → CUST201`; `tenureYears: 0.05 → 1` (also updated `relationshipLength: "3 weeks" → "1 year"` to keep both fields consistent). Side effect caught and flagged: she now also qualifies for "Flexi Loan" (requires tenure ≥ 0.5 years).

3. **Robert's ID changed twice**: `CUST79 → CUST791 → CUST792`. Each time, full journey re-verified (offers, interest rate lookup, Brevo event, Agent Desktop).

4. **Brevo call moved from Deepening to Consideration stage** ("for now" — original Deepening trigger is commented out, not deleted, for easy revert). Discovered and preserved a real discrepancy between platforms while doing this: the mobile app's original trigger was scoped to Robert only; the website's wasn't (it would've fired for any customer on that journey reaching Deepening, which now includes Maya). Fixed the log message that said "Deepening stage reached" to correctly say "Consideration stage reached."

5. **Personal Loan Offer / Loan Disbursement Confirmation now show her actual EMI Calculator loan amount** instead of static hardcoded amounts baked into their SVG illustrations (`$15,000` / `$12,000`). Generalized into one function, `augmentLoanAmountTiles()` / `augmentPreApprovedOfferWithRate()` pattern, driven by a small lookup table so future offers needing this are a one-line addition.

6. **EMI Calculator's exit button relabeled** "Back to dashboard" → "Save" (functionality identical — still navigates to dashboard, still persists loan terms).

7. **Non-logged-in homepage (`browse.html`) redesigned**, inspired by a user-provided "U+Bank" reference screenshot: bold two-line hero headline, CTA button, custom hand-built SVG hero illustration (geometric triangle-grid pattern + a flat-style phone-mockup graphic, matching the app's existing illustration style — deliberately **not** real stock photography, since none could be visually verified before use), "Discover how..." section header, 4-card feature grid with "Learn more" links. Deliberately kept the site's existing navy/blue palette rather than adopting the reference's green, to stay consistent with every other page.

8. **Mobile app offer cards restyled "ticket" style**, inspired by a user-provided "Scratch & Win" screenshot — **scoped to visual styling only** (colors/shapes/badges/buttons), not the gamification concept itself, per explicit user clarification. Added: rounded "ticket" card corners, a "New" ribbon corner badge (shown only on not-yet-responded-to offers), a dashed ticket-perforation divider (with circular "punch" notches) between description and buttons, pill-shaped buttons — **scoped specifically to buttons inside offer cards** (`.bnk-card-actions .bnk-btn-primary`), not the app's buttons globally.

9. **Anonymous "Borrow" container/capture_response flow built from scratch** (`browse.html`):
   - `fetchAnonymousBorrowContainer()` — calls `container` with `ExternalID: cookieId` instead of `SubjectID` (since no customer is logged in yet).
   - `sendAnonymousCaptureResponse(offer, cookieId, subjectId = cookieId)` — calls `capture_response` once per offer returned, with `Outcome: "Impression"`, `Behavior: "Neutral"`.
   - **Retry logic**: up to 2 attempts before giving up, each attempt logged separately.
   - **Static fallback**: when the live call returns zero offers *or* fails entirely after both attempts, still fires `capture_response` for a fixed list of 8 real offers (captured from an actual Pega response the user supplied), so the mechanism can still be demonstrated/tested even when live personalization isn't available.
   - **Guard against duplicate fetching**: `borrowContainerFetched` flag — the real bug this fixed: repeat "Borrow" clicks were firing a fresh `container` call every time.
   - **"API debug log" panel** added to `browse.html`: shows every `container`/`capture_response` call's status (Pending → Success/Failed), the actual payload sent (pretty-printed JSON), and — critically — the actual live offer *count* and Pega's own `Message` field when present (e.g. `"No offers available"`), so it's immediately clear *why* no captures followed a "successful" call. Implemented via an optional `window.__apiDebugLog` hook that shared files call into safely (no-op on every other page that doesn't define it). **`BehavioralData` was deliberately excluded** from this panel per explicit user request — it still fires normally, just isn't logged here.
   - **"Download cookie ID" button** — lets a visitor actually see their own anonymous cookie value (otherwise only visible in `localStorage`/network payloads) by triggering a real browser file download.

10. **Robert-specific fallback burst on his own (logged-in) dashboard**: once his real `container` call succeeds and returns a real `InteractionID`, the app also fires the same 8-offer static fallback `capture_response` burst used on the anonymous Borrow screen — but with `InteractionID` swapped for his real one, and (per a follow-up request) `SubjectID` swapped for his actual customer ID (`ExternalID` stays the anonymous cookie, since that's what `IDMerge` already tied to him). **Fires exactly once per journey**, not once per stage — implemented via a dedicated persistent flag `session.robertFallbackBurstLogged` (not a log-entry-based check, since `session.log` is capped at 20 entries and could eventually trim a marker entry back out over a long journey).

11. **Mobile app "sharper" density pass** (most recent): reduced font sizes and tightened padding across the header, hero balance card, section panel titles, offer card text, Activity ledger rows, greeting headline, and bottom nav — mirroring the same approach used for the Agent Desktop's density pass. Scoped to the most visually prominent, highest-impact elements rather than a blanket global font-size change (to keep the result predictable and avoid unintended side effects elsewhere in a very large single file).

## 5. Bugs Found and Fixed This Session

| # | Bug | Root cause | Fix |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Offer card illustrations cropped at narrow/collapsed viewports | `.bnk-card-image` used `object-fit: cover` with a **fixed** `height`; as the card's width shrank the container's aspect ratio drifted further from the image's native 500:260 ratio, so `cover` cropped more and more | Replaced fixed `height` with `aspect-ratio: 500 / 260`, so the container always matches the image's shape and nothing needs cropping |
| 2 | "Instellars" logo invisible on `browse.html` | Reused the shared `.bnk-brand` class (light color, designed for dark backgrounds like the login screen) on a page with a light header background | Overrode the logo's color to a dark value specifically on `browse.html`, without touching the shared class other pages depend on |
| 3 | Dynamic loan-amount text injection silently failing | `buildSceneIllustration()` returns a **URL-encoded** SVG data URI — a literal string search for `"$15,000"` never matches the encoded `%2415%2C000` | Decode the data URI, do the string replace on the decoded SVG, then re-encode |
| 4 | Dynamic loan amount not updating after visiting EMI Calculator | The augmentation ran inside `fetchOffers()`, which completes *before* `lastLoanTerms` is set (that only happens when she exits the EMI Calculator screen) | Re-run the same augmentation function directly inside `handleEmiCalculatorExit()`, at the point the value actually becomes known |
| 5 | Repeated "Borrow" clicks re-fired the `container` call every time | No guard existed — `showLoanOffers()` called `fetchAnonymousBorrowContainer()` unconditionally on every click | Added `borrowContainerFetched` boolean guard |
| 6 | Debug log's "success" message didn't reflect the real offer count | `window.__apiDebugLog("container","success",...)` was called *before* the offer count was even computed, using a hardcoded message | Moved the log call to after computing the count; message now states the actual number and surfaces Pega's own `Message` field |
| 7 | Robert's fallback capture burst fired once **per stage** instead of once **per journey** | Reused the existing `alreadyLogged` gate, which is intentionally stage-scoped (checks for a stage-prefixed log entry) — correct for its original purpose, wrong for this new one | Built a dedicated `session.robertFallbackBurstLogged` flag, set once and persisted directly on the session object (not inferred from the log, which is capped at 20 entries and could eventually trim a marker entry back out) |
| 8 | Shipped a stale zip without the latest fix, undetected initially | `rm` against `/mnt/user-data/outputs` failed with `Input/output error` mid-operation; the subsequent `cp`/`zip` steps still "succeeded" but operated on stale/partial state | Recovery pattern now standard practice — see §2's shipping checklist. Caught by directly `grep`-checking the shipped file's actual contents rather than trusting command exit codes |

## 6. Verification Methodology (worth preserving as a practice, not just a record)

Every feature above was verified **live**, not just read back from the code:
- Spin up `python3 -m http.server` against the actual working directory (or the actual shipped-and-re-extracted zip).
- Drive it with Playwright, intercepting/stubbing network calls (`page.route(...)`) where the real Pega endpoints are CORS-blocked, to test success/failure/retry/fallback paths that can't be reached live from this sandbox.
- Assert on **actual captured request payloads** (`req.post_data`), not just "no errors were thrown."
- Explicitly test the "no regression" cases too — other customers (Sam, Maya, Diego) unaffected by a change scoped to Robert; wide-viewport layout unaffected by a narrow-viewport fix; etc.
- Final check before presenting to the user: re-extract the *actual shipped file* fresh and re-run the test against that specific copy — not the working directory.

## 7. Immediate Next Steps / Open Items

These are things that were surfaced, discussed, or flagged during this session but **not yet acted on** — worth raising early in the next session rather than assuming they're resolved:

1. **The static 8-offer fallback (for the anonymous Borrow flow and Robert's dashboard burst) is explicitly marked "for now" / temporary** in code comments. It exists because Pega's live `container` call currently returns `"No offers available"` for anonymous/unrecognized visitors in this environment. Once Pega's strategy reliably returns live offers for anonymous visitors (or once this is confirmed as intentional, permanent behavior), this fallback should be reconsidered or removed — it currently makes every anonymous visitor's impression data look identical (same 8 offers, same static field values) regardless of who they actually are.

2. **The anonymous `container` call doesn't check `data.Status !== "OK"`** the way the logged-in path does (`simulateCDHRequest()` explicitly validates this and throws if it isn't `"OK"`). The anonymous path only checks the HTTP status. This was identified and explicitly offered as a fix — **the user has not yet confirmed whether they want it added.**

3. **The logged-in dashboard's "Live" badge is misleading** — it reflects a manual "Simulate outage" toggle, not whether the real Pega call actually succeeded. It currently says "Live" even while silently showing fallback mock data. Flagged as "arguably a real bug worth fixing on its own" but not yet requested/actioned. *(Update: fixed in §14, added in a later session.)*

4. **The CORS issue itself is unresolved and cannot be fixed from this codebase.** `instellars02.pegalabs.io` needs `Access-Control-Allow-Origin` configured to permit whatever origin the site is actually deployed/tested from. This blocks *all* live Pega verification from this sandbox — every test in this session for real API behavior relied on either Playwright route-stubbing or user-supplied real response payloads pasted into chat.

5. **Mobile app "sharper" pass covered the Dashboard and Journeys tabs** specifically (the most visible/highest-traffic screens). Other tabs (Accounts, Transfers, Bills, Messages, More) were not audited for the same density pass — worth checking if the user wants full coverage.

6. **The Agent Desktop's "Customer Details" tab remains a non-functional label** (only "Summary" and "Interaction History" were wired up to actually switch content, per the specific scope of that request). *(Update: removed entirely in a later session — see §43 below.)*

## 10. Explainability Engine (added this session)

A new capability, not present in §1–9: turns the raw arbitration inputs that were already sitting on every offer (`eligibility` function, `propensity`/`businessValue`/`urgency`) into an actual explainability layer, modeled on what real Pega CDH deployments provide (reason codes, an Eligibility/Applicability/Suitability rule trace, and arbitration transparency) — see the research summarized earlier in this conversation on Pega's Explainability Engine, Value Finder, and GenAI "AI Insight" feature for the real-product references this was modeled after.

**New file: `js/explainability.js`** (plus an inlined, manually-kept-in-sync copy inside `mobile-app.html`, per the project's existing convention for that file). Nothing in it is hand-authored per offer — everything is derived at render time from data already in the catalog:

- **Reason code** — a plain-language sentence built from whichever checks actually passed, so it can never say something the trace below it disagrees with
- **Eligibility** — parses each offer's `eligibility` arrow function source (`extractArrowBody()`, `splitTopLevelAnd()`), maps known clause shapes to human-readable labels (`describeClause()`), and evaluates each clause independently against the actual customer (`evaluateClause()`, via a scoped `new Function(...)` — safe here since these are first-party clauses from this same codebase, not user input) — so a multi-condition eligibility check like `!c.riskFlag && c.tenureYears >= 1` shows as two separate pass/fail rows, not one opaque boolean
- **Applicability** — generic, not hand-coded per offer: "not already responded to," and, for journey-stage offers, "has the customer actually reached that stage yet" (cross-checked against `session.currentStageIndex`)
- **Suitability** — generic soft/compliance-style checks applied by customer attribute and offer category: not financially vulnerable, debt-to-income ratio under 45%, and (for lending-related offers only) credit score above a 620 responsible-lending floor
- **Arbitration** — the same Propensity × Business Value × Urgency shape CDH uses conceptually, normalized to 0–1 scales so the displayed formula and the resulting score actually agree with each other (this was a real bug caught and fixed during verification — the first version showed `0.50 × 5/10 × 8/10` next to a score of `20.00`, which don't match), ranked against sibling offers

**Wired into all three surfaces:**

- **Website** — `js/render-shared.js`'s `renderOfferCard()`, used by `dashboard.html` and `journey.html`. Both pages now pass `session` and `allOffers` (the sibling list currently on screen) into the render call so arbitration ranking has something real to rank against.
- **Mobile app** — same engine, ported inline into `mobile-app.html`'s own copy of `renderOfferCard()`, using its `state.responses`/`state.offers`/`loadSession()` equivalents.
- **Agent Desktop** — a heavier build, since `agent-desktop.html` doesn't share `render-shared.js` and had no receipt/expand mechanism at all before this. Added a "Why this recommendation" toggle + panel to `renderOffersHtml()`, with one deliberate difference from the other two surfaces: Agent Desktop only ever displays the single top-ranked recommendation (`.slice(0, 1)`), so ranking it against "siblings on screen" would trivially always read "#1 of 1." Instead, arbitration there ranks against every offer in `OFFER_CATALOG` the customer is currently eligible for (`OFFER_CATALOG.filter(o => o.eligibility(agentCustomer))`), which is the more useful question for an agent: where does this recommendation sit among everything it beat, not just the one other thing that happened to be shown alongside it.

**Bug found and fixed during this build**, worth recording alongside the others in §5's style:

| # | Bug | Root cause | Fix |
|---|---|---|---|
| 9 | Agent Desktop's "Why this recommendation" panel wouldn't stay expanded after being clicked | `renderOffersHtml()` generated a random fallback `interactionId` (`Date.now() + random`) whenever `decision.interactionID` was falsy — which is the case for mock-scored offers. Toggling the panel re-renders from the same cached `decisions` array (to avoid re-firing a live CDH call), but each re-render generated a *new* random id, so the just-clicked toggle's id could never match on the next render | The generated id is now written back onto the `decision` object (`decision.interactionID = interactionId`) the first time it's computed, so re-rendering the same cached array reuses the same id every time |

Not yet done, worth flagging for whoever picks this up next: explainability was intentionally **not** extended to `browse.html`'s anonymous Borrow-screen tiles, since there's no logged-in customer or existing receipt mechanism there to attach it to — extending it would mean designing a lighter-weight, no-PII version of the same trace for anonymous visitors, which wasn't in scope for this pass.

---

## 11. Strategy Governance Layer (added this session)

Prompted by a direct question worth recording verbatim in spirit: *"Isn't the Activity Ledger an audit trail?"* — the answer is: related, but not the same thing, and the distinction is exactly what this section adds.

**The Activity Ledger (`session.log`, `pushSessionLog()`) is a customer-interaction log** — what offers were shown/accepted/rejected, journey-stage transitions. It has three real limitations that keep it from being a compliance-grade audit trail: it's **capped at 20 entries** and silently drops older ones (this is *why* `session.robertFallbackBurstLogged` exists as a separate persistent flag rather than a log-derived check — see §5, bug #7); it's **ephemeral, per-browser localStorage**, not centrally queryable; and it records **what happened to the customer**, not **why the system decided it** — no ruleset version, no record of who approved the decisioning logic itself.

**New file: `js/governance.js`** answers that second question — a registry of strategy version, approval, and change-history metadata **per proposition group** (the same grouping Pega's NBA Designer uses — i.e. `offer.group`, matching each journey), not per individual offer and not per customer:

- `STRATEGY_GOVERNANCE` — one record per group (`Personal Loan & Card Cross-Sell`, `New Customer Acquisition`, `Digital Activation & Onboarding`, `Wealth Management & Advisory`, `Consumer Credit Line`, `Financial Education`, `Scratch & Win — Gamification Campaign`), each with `strategyVersion`, `status` (`Live` / `In review`), `owner`, `approvedBy`, `approvedDate`, `modelVersion`, and a `changeHistory` array
- **Grounded in real project history where it exists** — e.g. the Personal Loan & Card Cross-Sell group's change history literally lists the real changes made to it across this project (Lost Card Replacement added, Brevo trigger moved from Deepening to Consideration, Loan Top-Up/Refinance added, Maya's eligibility extended), each flagged `real: true`. Groups without a documented real history get a plausible-but-fictional single-entry history, flagged `real: false` — this is a demo, not an actual approval system, but it's structured the way a real 1:1 Operations Manager change record is: versioned, attributed, dated, with a status.
- **Deliberately fails honest, not silent** — any offer whose `group` isn't in the registry (e.g. a future offer added without a reviewed strategy) renders as `Unregistered` with a visible warning, rather than fabricating a plausible-looking record for something that was never actually reviewed.

**Wired into the same three surfaces as the explainability engine**, appended as a final "Governance" section inside the same receipt/panel (`js/render-shared.js` for website, inlined copy in `mobile-app.html`, and `agent-desktop.html`'s own D365-styled panel — which now sits logically alongside the existing "Compliance Flags" panel on that screen, reinforcing the same risk/compliance story). `explainOffer()` (`js/explainability.js`) now also returns the `offer` object itself in its result, since the governance lookup needs it and previously only the derived checks were returned.

---

## 12. Program Performance / ROI Dashboard (added this session)

Prompted directly by the earlier RFP-criteria research in this session — banks weight demonstrated ROI, NPS impact, and volume-processed metrics highly when evaluating a CDH vendor — and none of that lived anywhere in this app before now — every existing screen is scoped to a single customer or a single decision. This is the first genuinely program-level, portfolio-wide view.

**New page: `program-performance.html`**, linked from a new card in `dashboard.html`'s sidebar rail (alongside the existing Mobile Experience and Agent Desktop cards), opened in a new tab, not gated behind a specific customer login — same access pattern as Agent Desktop.

**New data file: `js/program-performance.js`** — there's no real backend to aggregate actual interaction data from (the six demo customers' localStorage sessions aren't a meaningful sample size for a "program" view), so this generates **deterministic, seeded synthetic data** via a small `mulberry32()` PRNG — same numbers on every reload, not fresh random noise per visit, which matters for a dashboard meant to be screenshotted or demoed repeatedly. Produces:

- A 12-week time series of offers served / accepted / rejected, with a CDH-driven conversion rate that drifts upward (models improving over time — the actual pitch) against a roughly-flat non-personalized baseline
- Per-proposition-group breakdown (reuses the same group names as `js/governance.js`'s `STRATEGY_GOVERNANCE` registry, so the two stay consistent with each other)
- Per-channel breakdown (Web / Mobile / Agent-assisted) — Agent-assisted is deliberately modeled as converting highest, reflecting a commonly-cited real pattern (human + AI recommendation outperforming either alone), not an arbitrary number
- A "Top performing offers" table pulling **real offer titles and treatment IDs straight from `OFFER_CATALOG`**, not invented names, so it can't drift out of sync if offers are added or renamed later

**Charts are hand-rolled inline SVG** (`ppLineChart()`, `ppBarChart()`) rather than pulling in an external charting library — consistent with the rest of this app's existing pattern of hand-built SVG illustrations (`buildSceneIllustration()`), and avoids adding a new dependency for two fairly simple chart types.

**Headline KPIs shown**: total offers served, overall CDH conversion rate vs. baseline, relative lift %, and an estimated incremental-value figure (offer accepts × an illustrative average-deal-value-by-group constant — clearly labeled as illustrative, not derived from any real pricing model).

**Explicit methodology disclosure on the page itself**, matching this app's established convention of never letting mock data pass as real (same spirit as the dashboard's "Showing static fallback offers" note): states plainly that the data is simulated and seeded, not aggregated from the app's actual demo customers, and that the baseline rate / deal values / channel-mix assumptions are placeholders.

Verified live: KPI values confirmed identical across a page reload (determinism check), the dashboard's new "View Program Performance" card link confirmed present and pointing at the right URL, and the layout confirmed to collapse cleanly to a single column at narrow (700px) viewport widths.

## 13. Core Banking Integration Layer (added this session)

Prompted by a direct architecture question: *"How to solve the issue of integrating with a core banking endpoint for transactions, account info, etc."* The honest answer is that this app never actually solved that — `CUSTOMERS` (`js/data.js`) has always been a static in-memory array with no indication of where any field actually came from or how fresh it is. This section makes that gap visible rather than pretending it doesn't exist, modeling the real integration pattern rather than building a real integration (there's no actual core banking system to connect to in this sandbox).

**The real pattern this models**: real CDH deployments essentially never call a core banking system directly — there's always a middleware/integration layer in between (an ESB, API gateway, or a bank's own "digital core" abstraction), because core banking data models need translation and because auth/security posture can't reasonably be handled by the decisioning layer itself. Within that, Pega's own architecture splits into three tiers, each modeled here:

1. **Batch ETL** (most account/profile fields — credit score, holdings, DTI, segment) — nightly-refreshed, matching how most banks actually move slow-changing profile data
2. **Real-time cached Data Page** (balance, active loan balance) — short TTL, refetched frequently but not on every single request
3. **Event-driven push** (risk flag, days past due) — updates the moment the source system changes, not on a batch cycle

**New file: `js/core-banking.js`** — per-field metadata (`CORE_BANKING_FIELD_META`) mapping each `CUSTOMERS` field to a plausible real source system (Temenos T24, Credit Bureau Feed, Compliance/AML System, Loan Servicing System, Customer Data Platform, CRM/Segmentation Engine) and sync mechanism. `cbBuildProvenance(customerId)` generates deterministic, seeded per-field "last synced" timestamps and staleness flags (stable across reloads, like the governance registry — provenance metadata shouldn't change every time you look at it). `fetchCoreBankingProfile(customer)` simulates an actual round-trip: realistic latency (220–700ms) and an honest ~8% simulated failure rate that falls back to last-known-good data — the deliberately honest counterpart to the "Live" badge bug in §3.4, which shows "Live" even while silently serving fallback data.

**Bug found and fixed during this build**: the first version of `cbBuildProvenance()` computed `elapsedFraction = rand() * 0.85`, capping the value strictly below the `> 0.85` staleness threshold — mathematically making the "stale" UI state unreachable for any customer. Caught by explicitly testing all demo customers for at least one stale field before shipping (none showed any), not just eyeballing one screenshot. Fixed by using the full `0..1` range for `elapsedFraction`.

**Wired into two surfaces:**

- **Agent Desktop** — each Financial Snapshot field (Credit Score, Debt-to-Income, Customer Segment, Digital Engagement, Active Loan Accounts, Risk Flag) now shows a small caption underneath: source system, sync mechanism, and relative freshness, with a green/amber dot for fresh vs. stale. `applyCoreBankingProvenance()`, called from the existing `applyCustomerData()`.
- **Website dashboard** — new "Data Sources" panel (`renderCoreBankingPanel()`) below the Activity Ledger, showing the same provenance for all ten tracked fields in a two-column grid, plus the simulated sync latency and an honest fallback note if the simulated call "failed." Loaded via `loadCoreBankingProfile()`, following the same async-load-then-repaint pattern as the existing Supabase Event Store panel.

**A second real layout bug found and fixed**: the first version of the dashboard panel reused `bnk-receipt-row`'s two-column `justify-content: space-between` layout, which was designed for short label:value pairs (e.g. "Business value: 5/10") — the provenance strings here ("Core Banking (Temenos T24) · Real-time Data Page · 12s ago") are much longer and overflowed the card edge. Fixed by switching to a stacked grid layout (`bnk-datasource-grid`/`bnk-datasource-field`/`bnk-datasource-detail`), matching the pattern that already worked cleanly on Agent Desktop.

**Not wired into mobile app or Agent Desktop's** — deliberately scoped to the two surfaces where this data actually gets *read* directly (the customer-facing dashboard, and the agent's own account snapshot); `mobile-app.html` doesn't currently surface these underlying fields directly the way the dashboard's Financial Snapshot does, so extending this there wasn't a natural fit for this pass.

## 14. "Live" Badge Bug — Actually Fixed This Session

This was flagged as a known issue as far back as §3.4 in the very first version of this document and never actually fixed until now — worth recording properly since it was explicitly called out as "arguably a real bug worth fixing on its own."

**The bug**: the dashboard's "Live"/"Fallback mode" badge (`dashboard.html`, and three separate instances inside `mobile-app.html`) was wired directly to `session.outage` — the **manual** "Simulate outage" toggle — rather than to whether the last real `container` call **actually succeeded**. Since `instellars02.pegalabs.io` is CORS-blocked from any browser context in this sandbox (§3.4), the real call fails on essentially every single page load regardless of the toggle, silently falling back to `simulateCDHRequestMock()` — and the badge kept saying "Live" throughout, because it was never actually looking at the outcome of that call.

**Root cause, one level deeper**: `dashState.meta.fallback` (or `state.meta.fallback` in the mobile app) was already the correct signal to use — every *explicit* fallback code path (manual outage, journey-specific catch blocks) already set `fallback: true` on the meta object. But `simulateCDHRequestMock()` itself — the actual function invoked by the organic CORS-failure catch blocks, in both `js/cdh.js` and `mobile-app.html`'s own inlined copy — never set that flag on its returned `meta`. So the signal existed and was used correctly everywhere except the single most common path.

**The fix**:

1. Added `fallback: true` to `simulateCDHRequestMock()`'s returned `meta` in both `js/cdh.js` and `mobile-app.html`'s inlined copy.
2. Replaced the badge's condition everywhere it appears — `dashboard.html` (new `renderLiveBadge(dashState)` helper) and all three instances in `mobile-app.html` (new shared `renderLiveBadge(state)` helper) — from `session.outage ? ... : ...` to actually reading `state.meta?.fallback`.
3. Added a third, honest **"Checking…"** neutral state for while a request is still in flight, rather than defaulting to "Live" during the loading window.
4. The "Simulate outage" toggle button itself is intentionally left reading `session.outage` — that's correct, since the button's own state should reflect the user's intent, not the outcome. Only the status badge needed to change.

**Verified live** — the critical test: with `session.outage` left at its default `false` (the toggle never touched), the badge correctly shows **"Fallback mode"** on both the dashboard and mobile app, because the real Pega call genuinely fails from this sandbox. This is the exact scenario that was broken before — previously this same setup would have shown "Live" throughout. Also verified: the manual "Simulate outage" toggle still works and produces the same (correct) "Fallback mode" result, and a full regression pass (Agent Desktop toggle, Borrow screen, governance panels on all three surfaces, Program Performance, Data Sources panel across 5 other customers) came back clean.

## 15. Website "Sharper" Density Pass (added this session)

Prompted by a direct question: *"did we make the website more crisp and sharp the way we did the mobile app?"* Checked rather than assumed — grepped the actual CSS values, not relied on memory. The honest answer was **no**: the "sharper" density pass (smaller fonts, tighter padding) from earlier in the project (§4 item 1, item 11) had only ever been applied to **Agent Desktop** and to the **mobile app's Dashboard/Journeys tabs** — the main website (`css/app.css`, shared by `dashboard.html`, `journey.html`, `browse.html`, `login.html`, `new-application.html`, `emi-calculator.html`) was still at its original, larger sizing throughout. Confirmed by direct comparison, e.g. `.bnk-greeting` was `1.3rem` on the website vs. `1.15rem` on mobile; `.bnk-sidebar-panel-title` was `1rem` vs. `0.92rem`.

**What was done**: the same disciplined, scoped approach used for the earlier passes — high-traffic, visually prominent elements only, not a blanket global font-size reduction — applied to `css/app.css`. Proportionally similar reductions to what mobile/Agent Desktop received, adapted for a wider desktop layout rather than copying the mobile app's phone-frame values verbatim (a 1100px-wide desktop dashboard has different density needs than a 375px phone frame).

**Elements tightened**: header padding, nav tabs, greeting headline, hero account card (padding, balance value, label, masked account number, quick-action buttons), sidebar panel padding/header/title, engine badge, offer card title/description, Activity Ledger section (padding, header, row spacing, name/meta/amount text), and the journey/decision log rows (`.bnk-log-row`/`.bnk-log-time`).

**Verified live**: screenshotted `dashboard.html`, `browse.html`, `journey.html`, and a narrow (480px) viewport — all render cleanly with no overflow, clipping, or misalignment. Full regression suite (Agent Desktop, Borrow screen, governance panels on all three surfaces, Program Performance, Data Sources panel, Live badge fix) re-run clean afterward, since `css/app.css` is shared by every page that could plausibly be affected.

**Not touched**: `new-application.html` and `emi-calculator.html` weren't specifically screenshotted/verified in this pass (they share the same stylesheet and same class names, so the same tightened values apply automatically, but worth a visual spot-check next session if not already done). Agent Desktop and the mobile app were left as-is, since they already received their own density passes earlier and use separate stylesheets (`agent-desktop.html` has its own inline `<style>` block; `mobile-app.html` has its own inlined copy of these same class names, deliberately not touched here since it already went through this exercise).

## 16. Mobile App Nav-Tab Cropping — Found and Fixed

Prompted by a user screenshot showing "Bil…" abruptly chopped off at the right edge of the mobile app's top nav bar, with a raw horizontal scrollbar visible underneath — reported as "some code got stale for mobile, cropping is happening."

**Root cause, confirmed by actually reproducing it** (not guessed from reading code): `mobile-app.html`'s `.bnk-phone-frame` mockup is a fixed-width (390px) decorative "phone" illustration meant primarily for previewing the mobile app inside a desktop browser. Tested at a real phone viewport (Playwright's iPhone 13 device profile, 390px), the frame correctly shrinks via `max-width: 100vw`, but the inner usable screen area (`.bnk-phone-screen`, ~358–362px after the frame's own decorative padding) is genuinely too narrow to fit all **7 top nav tabs** (Dashboard / Accounts / Transfers / Bills / Cards / Loans / Investments) at a readable size. `.bnk-nav-tabs` already had `overflow-x: auto` — the horizontal scroll was actually working correctly — but there was **no visual cue that more tabs existed**, so a tab getting cut mid-word at the container edge reads as a bug rather than an intentional "swipe for more" pattern.

This is architecturally distinct from — and unrelated to — the CSS **container queries** (`@container phone (max-width: ...)`) that already make the rest of this page responsive to the phone-frame's own width regardless of actual browser viewport size; those were working correctly. This was specifically a missing scroll affordance on one element, not a broken breakpoint.

**The fix** (`mobile-app.html` only — this decorative phone-frame mockup doesn't exist on the main website):

1. Added a right-edge fade-out gradient overlay (`.bnk-nav-tabs-fade`, wrapped in a new `.bnk-nav-tabs-wrap`) that fades from transparent to the header's exact background color (`#0E1230`), so a partially-visible tab now reads as "scroll for more" instead of looking clipped/broken.
2. Slightly tightened `.bnk-nav-tab` padding and font size at the existing `@container phone (max-width: 480px)` breakpoint (`0.7rem 0.9rem` → `0.6rem 0.6rem`, `0.85rem` → `0.78rem` font), so one additional full tab fits before the fade/scroll point — this alone doesn't solve the underlying space constraint (7 tabs genuinely can't all fit legibly on a ~360px screen), which is why the fade was the primary fix, not just tighter padding.

**Verified live**: reproduced the exact bug first (Playwright, real iPhone 13 viewport, screenshot matched the user's report almost exactly — "Bil" cut off, no scroll cue), then confirmed the fix resolves it (Bills now fully visible, Cards fades cleanly at the edge instead of being clipped). Confirmed no regression at wide desktop-preview widths (1280px) — tabs still scroll correctly, nothing broken. Full regression suite (Agent Desktop, Borrow screen, governance on all three surfaces, Program Performance, Data Sources, Live badge) re-run clean afterward, and the standalone `instellars-cdh-demo.html` re-synced to stay byte-identical with `mobile-app.html` per the project's core convention.

### 16.1 Follow-up: the real remaining cause (this fix was necessary but incomplete)

The user reported the issue persisting after §16's fix shipped, described as "loads correct, then a refresh happens resulting in the same issue" — but confirmed via follow-up questions that this was **not** a real page reload (no loss of the selected customer, stayed on the dashboard) and **not** a caching issue (freshly re-downloaded the zip). This ruled out both of the obvious explanations and meant §16's fix, while correct as far as it went, wasn't the complete picture.

A user-supplied screenshot of the actual "odd" state revealed the real second cause: a **native OS scrollbar** — the classic Windows style, with visible directional arrow buttons at each end of the track — rendering directly underneath `.bnk-nav-tabs`. This is why it was unreproducible in this sandbox: Playwright's headless Chromium uses invisible overlay scrollbars by default, so `.bnk-nav-tabs`' pre-existing (and entirely legitimate) `overflow-x: auto` never visibly painted a scrollbar in any automated test here, no matter how many times the loading→settled transition or long-idle states were checked. A Windows browser with classic (non-overlay) scrollbar rendering enabled shows this scrollbar constantly and prominently once there's overflow content — which reads exactly like "it loaded fine, then something changed" if the scrollbar's paint is deferred slightly relative to the rest of the layout.

**The actual fix**: hide the native scrollbar entirely (`scrollbar-width: none` for Firefox, `-ms-overflow-style: none` for legacy Edge/IE, `::-webkit-scrollbar { display: none; }` for Chrome/Safari/Edge), while leaving `overflow-x: auto` itself untouched — confirmed via computed style and a direct `scrollLeft` manipulation test that the element is still genuinely scrollable, just without ever painting OS scrollbar chrome. Applied to `.bnk-nav-tabs` in both `mobile-app.html` and `css/app.css` (the website has the identical class and was equally exposed, just not yet reported), plus `.bnk-stepper-row` (the journey progress stepper) in both files, found via a full grep for every other `overflow-x: auto` element in the codebase rather than fixing only the one that was reported.

**Verified live**: confirmed `scrollbar-width: none` applies via computed style, confirmed the element's `scrollWidth` still genuinely exceeds `clientWidth` (real overflow, not accidentally removed), confirmed `scrollLeft` can still be set and read back correctly (functionally still scrollable), and re-screenshotted — no scrollbar bar renders, Bills fully visible, Cards fades cleanly. Full regression suite re-run clean.

### 16.2 Second follow-up: a third, distinct cause (frame overflow, not nav-tabs at all)

The user reported the issue *still* persisting after §16.1 shipped, with another screenshot. This one showed a different signature: **multiple unrelated elements** — the nav tabs, the "Active" badge on the savings card, the "Statement" quick-action button, the "Instellars Engine" badge — all cropped at the exact same vertical line on the right edge, with the left edge rendering perfectly fine. That pattern (uniform right-edge crop across many unrelated elements, left edge intact) doesn't match a single component's internal scroll — it matches the **entire phone-frame mockup overflowing the browser's viewport**.

**Root cause**: `body { padding: 2.5rem 1rem; }` gives 16px of horizontal padding on each side (32px total), but `.bnk-phone-frame { max-width: 100vw; }` never accounted for that padding — it only capped the frame at the full viewport width, not the viewport width *minus the padding around it*. On any real browser window between 390px (the frame's natural width) and 422px (390 + 32px padding), the frame could render wider than the space actually available inside the padded body, pushing its right portion past the visible browser window edge. Since the frame is horizontally centered (`justify-content: center`) but overflowing content in a centered flex item is asymmetric in practice — the portion left of the initial viewport's x=0 stays inaccessible either way, while the right-side overflow is what a page-level horizontal scrollbar would normally reveal — and nothing in this file explicitly suppressed or exposed that scroll, so it just looked like a hard crop with no way to reach the missing content.

This is architecturally the third distinct cause found across three rounds of this same user-reported symptom: §16 was a missing scroll affordance on one element, §16.1 was a visible native scrollbar on that same element, and this is a genuine layout overflow of the entire mockup frame, unrelated to either.

**The fix** (`mobile-app.html` only):

1. `.bnk-phone-frame`'s `max-width: 100vw` → `max-width: calc(100vw - 2rem)`, matching body's `1rem`-per-side horizontal padding exactly, so the frame can never render wider than the space actually available inside the padded body.
2. Added `overflow-x: hidden` to `body` as a defensive safety net, so even an unanticipated edge case (box-shadow bleed, sub-pixel rounding) can't reproduce a page-level horizontal crop again.

**Verified live**: tested every viewport width across the specific vulnerable range (400px, 405px, 410px, 415px, 420px — all between the frame's natural 390px and the un-padded 422px danger threshold) and confirmed `document.documentElement.scrollWidth` no longer exceeds `window.innerWidth` at any of them, screenshotted 400px specifically (the worst case) and confirmed the Active badge, Statement button, and Instellars Engine badge are all now fully visible with clean margin on both sides. Also re-verified both extremes (1280px wide desktop, and the exact real iPhone 13 391px profile) show zero overflow. Full regression suite re-run clean.

### 16.3 Third follow-up: the actual root cause (a classic CSS Grid pitfall)

The user reported the issue *still* persisting after §16.2 shipped, with a third screenshot (657×784px, implying a real CSS viewport in the ~320–330px range once display scaling is accounted for) showing the same signature as §16.2 — but this time only *some* elements were cropped (the "Active" pill and "Instellars Engine" badge) while others (the nav tabs, the four quick-action buttons) rendered correctly. That partial pattern was the clue that §16.2's fix was necessary but still not the full story — a genuinely different, narrower-viewport-only cause remained underneath it.

**Root cause, found by direct measurement, not guesswork**: tested every width from 320px to 375px and measured exact bounding-box coordinates of the affected badges against their containing `.bnk-phone-screen`. At 320px, `.bnk-hero-active-pill`'s right edge was at `x=322`, while `.bnk-phone-screen`'s own right edge was at `x=290` — the badge was rendering **32px past its own container's boundary**, silently clipped by the screen's `overflow: hidden`. Traced up the ancestor chain: `.bnk-main-col`'s bounding rect reported `right: 0` — expected, since it uses `display: contents` at this breakpoint (§4's original mobile redesign), meaning its former children (`.bnk-hero-account`, offer cards, etc.) become direct items of `.bnk-dashboard-grid` for layout purposes. That grid's narrow-breakpoint column was defined as `grid-template-columns: 1fr`.

This is a well-known CSS Grid pitfall: a bare `1fr` track has an **implicit `min-width: auto`**, not `0` — meaning the track (and everything placed in it) refuses to shrink below the *natural minimum content width* of whatever's inside it, no matter how narrow the viewport gets. Every other fix in §16–§16.2 (the nav-tabs fade, the scrollbar hiding, the frame's own `max-width` cap, even the `flex-wrap` added to the badge rows earlier in this same section) was correct and necessary, but all of them were operating *inside* a grid track that had already silently refused to shrink past a certain point — so past that point, no child-level fix could matter.

**The actual fix**: `grid-template-columns: 1fr` → `grid-template-columns: minmax(0, 1fr)` at both the narrow single-column breakpoint and the base two-column definition, in both `mobile-app.html` and `css/app.css` (both share the identical grid). `minmax(0, 1fr)` explicitly tells the track it's allowed to shrink to zero if needed, overriding the implicit `auto` floor — the standard, idiomatic fix for this exact class of bug. Combined with the `flex-wrap`/`flex-shrink`/`min-width: 0` additions made to the badge rows in the same pass (`.bnk-hero-top-row`, `.bnk-txn-header`, `.bnk-sidebar-panel-header`, plus `flex-shrink: 0` on `.bnk-hero-active-pill` and `.bnk-engine-badge` so they stay legible rather than being squashed once the track *can* shrink), badges now correctly wrap onto their own line instead of overflowing once genuinely out of room.

**Verified live**: re-measured the exact same coordinates that proved the bug (badge-right vs. screen-right) across 320px–375px and confirmed the badge now stays within bounds at every width tested (e.g. 320px: badge right `259.6` vs. screen right `290` — comfortably inside, vs. `322` vs. `290` before the fix). Screenshotted 320px and visually confirmed both the "Active" badge and "Instellars Engine" badge now render fully, with "Instellars Engine" correctly dropping to its own line rather than being clipped mid-word. Re-verified the website's `dashboard.html` at 340px shows zero page overflow and the same badges fully visible, since `css/app.css` shares the identical grid and got the identical fix. Full regression suite re-run clean, both extremes (1280px, 390px) re-confirmed zero overflow, and the standalone file re-synced.

**Retrospective note for whoever reads this next**: this three-round saga (§16, §16.1, §16.2, §16.3) is a good illustration of why "verified live" claims need to specify *at what viewport width* — each of the four fixes was independently correct and independently verified, but each was verified at widths that didn't happen to expose the next layer of the problem underneath it. The lesson applied going forward: when fixing a responsive layout bug, test a *sweep* of widths spanning the full range where the layout meaningfully changes (every breakpoint boundary, plus the narrowest realistic width), not just one or two representative sizes.

## 17. Home Page (`browse.html`) Redesign — Layout Pattern from a Real Bank Reference

Prompted by two user-supplied screenshots of the real CIMB Bank Singapore website's Contact Us page, asking to bring that "look and feel" to the app's home page.

**Handled deliberately, not just executed as asked**: the reference screenshots showed a real, named bank's actual branding (their logo, trademark red color identity, real Singapore address, real phone numbers, real SDIC deposit-insurance disclosure, real registration number) — and were of their *Contact Us* page specifically, while the request was for the *home page* (`browse.html`, distinct from a contact page in this app). Both were flagged directly before building anything: the real bank's exact branding, colors, and regulatory claims weren't reproduced (copying a real institution's trademark identity onto a fictional one would also just be factually wrong — Instellars isn't actually protected by SDIC), and the layout *pattern* was confirmed as the actual intent via a clarifying question before starting.

**What was adapted from the reference, restyled entirely in Instellars' existing brand** (`--ink` navy, `--brass` blue, `--star` purple, `Fraunces`/`IBM Plex Sans`, existing icon set — no new palette introduced):

- **Richer header** — added Personal Banking / Business Banking / Help & Support nav links (smooth-scroll to in-page sections, not dead links) and a decorative search icon, alongside the existing Log in button and demo-only "Download cookie ID" button.
- **Boxed gradient hero** — the existing hand-built hero illustration (triangle-grid + phone mockup SVG, kept as-is — already a distinctive signature element, not worth discarding) now sits inside a bold rounded gradient card with an eyebrow pill label, matching the reference's punchier "boxed hero" presentation instead of the previous plain-background two-column layout.
- **24/7 Lost Card & Fraud Reporting hotline banner** — a genuinely realistic bank-homepage pattern (not contact-page-specific), styled with the app's existing `--danger` token, fictional phone number.
- **New "How do you bank with us?" segment-picker section** — three cards (Personal / Business / Preferred Wealth), a common real-bank-homepage pattern distinct from the existing product grid ("what you can get" vs. "which relationship fits you"). New icons (`briefcase`) needed for this were added to the shared `ICON_PATHS` in `js/data.js` (`search`, `phone`, `mail`, `briefcase`), available site-wide now.
- **New rich footer** (`renderFooter()`, shared between `renderBrowse()` and `renderLoanOffers()`, which previously had no footer at all) — a trust/deposit-protection banner (fictional "National Deposit Guarantee Scheme," explicitly not any real insurer), a 4-column link footer (brand blurb + fictional contact details, Products & Services, Banking Segments, Help & Support), and a bottom bar that explicitly states *"This is a demo application built to showcase Pega Customer Decision Hub — not a real bank"* — matching this project's established convention of never letting fictional content quietly pass as real.

**Bug found and fixed during this build**: the header's actions cluster (search icon + "Download cookie ID" + Log in button) overflowed the viewport by 42px at narrow widths — found by sweeping 14 widths from 320px to 1280px rather than checking only one or two sizes (directly applying the lesson from §16.3's retrospective note above). Fixed by hiding the demo-only cookie-ID button and decorative search icon at ≤480px, where a real bank's mobile header would similarly simplify down to its essential actions.

**Verified live**: confirmed zero page overflow across the full 320px–1280px width sweep, confirmed the nav links' smooth-scroll works, confirmed the existing Borrow flow (`showLoanOffers()`) and all 10 loan tiles (including Lost Card Replacement and Lifestyle Card Recommendation) still work correctly and now also get the new footer, and re-ran the full regression suite (Agent Desktop, governance on all three surfaces, Program Performance, Data Sources, Live badge, scrollbar fix, both mobile-app frame-size extremes) clean — since the new icons were added to the shared `js/data.js`, used by every page in the app.

### 17.1 Follow-up: moved the product grid into a Personal Banking menu

Immediate follow-up request: move the "Discover how Instellars can help you reach your goals" 4-card grid (Borrow / Credit Cards / Savings Accounts / Time Deposits) off the home page body entirely, and surface those same four items as a dropdown menu under the header's "Personal Banking" nav link instead.

**Implementation**: `renderBrowseHeader()`'s "Personal Banking" nav link became a click-to-toggle dropdown (`.bnk-nav-dropdown`), not hover-based — hover doesn't work well on touch devices, and this app is meant to demo responsibly across both. Each of the four `PRODUCTS` entries renders as a menu item with its icon; clicking "Borrow" still calls the existing `showLoanOffers()` (unchanged behavior, same guarded fetch-once pattern as before), while the other three (which never had a real destination — they were static, non-functional cards even before this change) now link to `login.html`, consistent with the "log in to access" pattern used everywhere else in the app for actions that need an authenticated customer. Closes on an outside click via a single `document`-level listener, or automatically when a menu item is clicked and its `onclick` fires. `togglePersonalBankingMenu()` toggles a plain `.open` CSS class rather than triggering a full `render()` re-render, so opening/closing the menu doesn't reset scroll position or repaint the rest of the page.

**Removed from the home page body**: the "Discover how Instellars can help you reach your goals" heading and its 4-card grid — those cards' content now lives entirely in the dropdown. The footer's "Products & Services" column (previously duplicating the "Banking Segments" column by linking Personal/Business/Preferred Wealth to the same `#segments` anchor) was updated to list the four actual products instead (Borrow, Credit Cards, Savings Accounts, Time Deposits), removing that redundancy and fixing a dangling link to the now-deleted `#products` anchor.

**Verified live**: confirmed the product grid section and its heading are fully absent from the shipped `browse.html`, confirmed the dropdown opens on click and shows all four items with icons, confirmed it closes on an outside click, confirmed clicking "Borrow" from inside the dropdown still correctly navigates to the Loan Offers page, and re-swept the full 320px–1280px width range for overflow (clean) since the dropdown is absolutely positioned and a common source of small-screen layout bugs. Full regression suite re-run clean.

## 18. Card Density Pass — "Every Card on the Website Looks Too Big"

Prompted by direct feedback that every card across the home screen and other website screens looked oversized. Scoped to `css/app.css` only (shared by `dashboard.html`, `journey.html`, `browse.html`, `login.html`, etc.) — Agent Desktop and the mobile app were deliberately left untouched, since they already went through their own separate density passes earlier in this project (§4 item 1, §15) and use entirely separate stylesheets.

**What was tightened, and why the offer-card image aspect ratio specifically was NOT touched**: the shared `.bnk-card` system (offer cards on the dashboard, journey page, and Loan Offers grid) got reduced padding (`1.3rem → 1.05rem`), a smaller icon badge (`34px → 30px`), smaller title/description text, tighter button padding, and a tighter grid gap. The card image's `aspect-ratio: 500 / 260` was deliberately left unchanged — that exact ratio was a previous bug fix (§5, bug #1: "Offer card illustrations cropped at narrow/collapsed viewports"), matched precisely to the offer illustrations' native SVG viewBox dimensions. Changing it now would silently reintroduce that same cropping bug. The card still reads meaningfully smaller overall because the padding around that image shrank, even though the image's own proportions didn't change.

Also tightened: `.bnk-icon-btn` (Simulate outage / Refresh buttons), and on `browse.html` specifically, the "How do you bank with us?" segment cards (padding, icon size, title/description text) and the hotline alert banner's icon.

**Verified live**: screenshotted the dashboard's offer rail, the journey page's offer cards, and the browse.html segment cards side-by-side against the pre-change versions — all read noticeably tighter while remaining legible. Re-swept the full 320px–1280px viewport width range for overflow (clean, applying the same lesson from §16.3/§17's retrospective notes rather than checking only one size), and re-ran the full regression suite (Agent Desktop, governance on all three surfaces, Program Performance, Data Sources, Live badge, Personal Banking dropdown menu) clean.

### 18.1 Follow-up: the home page hero specifically, halved

Direct follow-up with a screenshot showing the home page's boxed gradient hero (added in §17) was still much too large relative to everything else on the page — asked for its size to be roughly halved.

**What changed, all in `browse.html`** (this hero doesn't exist anywhere else): `.bnk-hero-box` padding roughly halved (`3rem 3rem 2.5rem` → `1.5rem 1.7rem`), border-radius reduced to match (`20px` → `14px`), the headline font size actually halved (`3rem` → `1.6rem`), the illustration's grid column width nearly halved (`460px` → `260px`, which scales the whole hand-built SVG illustration down proportionally since it's set to `width: 100%` of its column), the eyebrow pill and CTA button both shrunk to match, and the mobile breakpoint override updated the same way (illustration max-width `380px` → `220px`).

**Verified live**: confirmed the hero box's rendered height dropped substantially at desktop width, screenshotted and visually confirmed the illustration is still fully legible (not just shrunk into illegibility) at both desktop and mobile (400px) widths, re-swept the full 320px–1280px range for overflow (clean), and re-confirmed the Personal Banking dropdown menu (§17.1) still works correctly alongside the resized hero.

### 18.2 Follow-up: replaced the CTA banner with a real "Current offers" section

Direct follow-up, with a screenshot of the "See what's personalized for you" CTA banner (the dark banner sitting below the segment cards) asking to remove it, and add a "current offers" section instead — one card each for Borrow, Credit Card, Savings Account, and Deposits.

The new entry has its own hand-built badge illustration (a padlock, matching the "guaranteed/locked-in rate" framing) in a blue palette, deliberately distinct from the amber tones already used by the loan offer sitting next to it on the page.

Added a new "Current offers" section (`HOME_CURRENT_OFFER_IDS`) directly below the existing "How do you bank with us?" segment cards, picking exactly one real catalog entry per product line: `pre-approved-loan-offer` (Borrow), `lifestyle-card-recommendation` (Credit Card), `hy-savings` (Savings Account), and the new `time-deposit-standalone` (Deposits) — reusing the same card markup/styling already used on the Loan Offers page (`.bnk-card` + `.bnk-browse-loan-image`), so these read as genuine offer cards (real illustration, real description, each offer's own actual `cta` label) rather than the old generic static product tiles.

**Verified live**: confirmed the CTA banner text is completely absent from the shipped file, confirmed all four new offer cards render with correct images/titles/descriptions/category labels/CTAs, re-swept the full 320px–1280px width range for overflow (clean), and re-ran the full regression suite (Agent Desktop, governance on all three surfaces, Program Performance, Data Sources, Live badge, Personal Banking dropdown, other customers' dashboards — since the new catalog entry, with `eligibility: c => true`, is now eligible for every customer, not just anonymous visitors) — all clean.

Two more direct follow-ups: add a "few latest advisories" section after Current Offers, and move the "Download cookie ID" debug button off the header — the request described it as belonging "at the bottom of the screen," where it wasn't yet present/visible, so it was added there rather than just deleted.

**Latest Advisories section** (`browse.html`): a new `ADVISORIES` array — four static institutional notices (Security, Product Update, two Notice items), deliberately modeled as **general bank communications, not CDH offers or decisions** — the same separation a real bank homepage draws between "things we're personally recommending you" (the Current Offers section, driven by the actual offer catalog) and "things everyone should know" (these advisories, which don't come from `OFFER_CATALOG` or any decisioning call at all). Each renders with a colored category tag, title, date, and short description. Placed directly below Current Offers, above the footer.

**Verified live**: confirmed the header no longer contains the cookie-ID button, confirmed the footer button exists, is visible (`isVisible()` check, not just present in the DOM), and actually triggers a real file download when clicked (`instellars-cookie-id.txt`). Confirmed all four advisory items render with correct tags/dates/copy, and that the title/date row wraps cleanly on narrow screens (400px) instead of overflowing. Re-swept the full 320px–1280px width range for overflow (clean) and re-ran the full regression suite — all clean.

## 19. Aria's Wealth Management Journey — Stage-Scoped Like Robert's

Prompted directly: move Aria to the Awareness stage of her Wealth Management & Advisory journey the moment she logs in, and advance her stage as she responds to offers — the same behavior Robert already had for his Personal Loan & Card Cross-Sell journey, just never extended to her.

**Investigated before building**, in direct response to two prior questions in this session about whether the real `container` API call happens for Aria and whether her offers get cross-validated against her journey: confirmed both were already true (the real call fires and fails via CORS same as any customer; her offers were already filtered to her journeys' action titles via `getJourneyActionTitleSet`). What was missing specifically was the **stage-scoped experience** — she was landing on a generic top-3-scored-then-filtered offer rail instead of being positioned at a specific stage, and nothing advanced her through stages on accept, because that whole mechanism was hardcoded to check for Robert's journey id specifically, in three separate places.

**Three small, precisely-scoped changes** (all in already-shared, already-generic code — nothing about the underlying mechanism needed to be rebuilt, since Robert's stage-scoped machinery was already journey-agnostic internally, just gated by a single hardcoded id check in each spot):

1. **`login.html`'s `selectCustomer()`** — now also sets `session.currentJourneyId = "wealth-management"` and `currentStageIndex = 0` when the selected customer is Aria (`id === "CUST101"`), alongside the existing Robert-specific branch.
2. **`js/session.js`'s `maybeAdvanceJourneyStage()`** — the guard `if (session.currentJourneyId !== "loan-card-crosssell") return;` widened to also allow `"wealth-management"`. The existing "Prospect is a special case" branch only applies when `stage.stage === "Prospect"` — the Wealth journey has no stage by that name, so Aria automatically gets the intended generic behavior ("any matching action advances") without needing a special case of her own.
3. **`dashboard.html`'s `loadOffers()`** — the top-level condition gating the stage-scoped fetch path widened from `session.currentJourneyId === "loan-card-crosssell"` to include `"wealth-management"` too. Everything inside that block was already driven generically by `session.currentJourneyId`/`session.currentStageIndex`, not hardcoded to Robert — the one piece of logic that *is* Robert-specific (the fallback `capture_response` burst) stays correctly gated behind a separate `customer.id === PRIYA_CUSTOMER_ID` check inside the block, so it doesn't fire for Aria.

**`journey.html` needed no changes at all** — it was already fully journey-agnostic (accepts any `journey.id` via URL param, already had Wealth-Management-aware subject-ID handling for the Awareness stage from earlier work), which is how her Wealth Management journey tracker was already rendering correctly on her dashboard even before this change.

**Verified live**, driving the actual UI rather than pre-seeding session state: logged in as Aria via the real customer picker, confirmed she lands with `currentJourneyId: "wealth-management"`, `currentStageIndex: 0`, confirmed her Personalized Offers panel shows **only** "FD Maturity Reinvestment" (the Awareness stage's one offer, labeled "AWARENESS STAGE"), clicked "Reinvest now," confirmed `currentStageIndex` advanced to `1`, and confirmed her offers panel then showed **only** "SIP / Regular Investment" (the Consideration stage's offer) — the stage-based offer swap working end to end, not just the state flag changing. Re-confirmed Robert's own flow is completely unaffected (still lands on Prospect stage, still shows his three Prospect-stage offers) since this touched shared code his flow also depends on. Full regression suite re-run clean.

**Scoped to the website only** (`login.html`, `dashboard.html`, `journey.html`) — `mobile-app.html` has its own separate, similarly-structured Robert-specific stage-scoped logic that was not extended to Aria in this pass; worth doing as a follow-up if the mobile app's experience for her should match.
