## 38. Interest Rate — Now Read From the Main Container Call, Separate Lending Endpoint Removed

Started from a direct report: "Robert's Pre-Approved Loan Offer used to show an interest rate, I don't see it now." Investigated live and found this wasn't a regression from anything changed this session — the rate came from a *second*, separate call (`fetchPriyaInterestRate()`, hitting `cdhfslending.pegademo.com`, a different domain than the main container endpoint) which was failing outright with `Failed to fetch` (confirmed directly: the main endpoint returned 200 in the same browser session where this one failed with zero network request even logged) — a live external-dependency issue, not a code bug.

Then, on request: **removed that second call entirely.** The interest rate now comes back as an `OfferedRate` field directly on the offer from the *main* container call — confirmed live (Robert's real container response does include it). `mapPegaOffer()` (`js/cdh.js`, mirrored in `mobile-app.html`) now maps `o.OfferedRate` onto `offer.offeredRate`. Removed `PRIYA_LENDING_CONTAINER_ENDPOINT`, `INTEREST_RATE_SUBJECT_IDS`, and the async `fetchPriyaInterestRate()` entirely. The old async `augmentPreApprovedOfferWithRate(offers, onUpdate, customerId)` became a synchronous `applyOfferedRateToOffers(offers, session)` — no network call, and it persists the rate to `session.lastOfferedRate` so screens reached later in the same session (EMI Calculator, New Application) can read it without a call of their own, the same pattern `session.lastLoanTerms` already used for carrying the loan amount across pages.

Every call site touched: `dashboard.html`/`journey.html` (now call the synchronous helper *before* the first paint, so there's no flash-then-update), `emi-calculator.html` and `js/session.js`'s `resetApplication()` (both now default from `session.lastOfferedRate` instead of fetching, with the old "Looking up your rate…" placeholder removed since there's no longer an async wait to show it during). `mobile-app.html` has its own fully separate copy of all of this and got the identical treatment, using `state.lastOfferedRate` instead of `session.lastOfferedRate`.

**A real bug caught mid-edit**: after deleting the old `if (interestRateSubjectId) { ... }` block in `mobile-app.html`'s `startNewApplication()`, a stray closing `}` was left behind — would have been a hard syntax error breaking the entire file. Caught by re-reading the edited region immediately, not by a test failure.

**Verified live** end-to-end for Robert on both website and mobile: zero requests to the old lending endpoint anywhere anymore; the Pre-Approved Loan Offer tile shows the real rate on first paint (no flash); `session.lastOfferedRate`/`state.lastOfferedRate` persist correctly; EMI Calculator and New Application both pick up the real rate with zero network calls and no console errors.

## 40. SIP / Regular Investment — Aria's Own "Apply Now" Flow

Robert's Personal Loan Offer has had an "Apply Now" flow (→ `new-application.html`) since early in the project. On request, built the equivalent for Aria's "SIP / Regular Investment" offer (Consideration stage, Wealth Management & Advisory) — **a new dedicated wizard**, not a reuse of the loan one, since that wizard's Additional Info step is loan-shaped (Loan Amount / Tenure / Interest Rate) and wouldn't fit an investment context. Confirmed as the intended scope via a clarifying question before building.

**New file: `sip-application.html`** — same 3-step pattern as `new-application.html` (Basic Info → Investment Details → Confirmation), but Investment Details swaps in Monthly SIP Amount, Investment Tenure, and a Fund Type selector (Equity/Debt/Hybrid/Index) in place of the loan fields. Uses its own session bucket, `session.sipApplication` (not `session.application`, which stays loan-specific), via a new `resetSipApplication()` in `js/session.js`.

**Wiring**: `js/render-shared.js`'s `renderOfferCard()` gained a second title-matched Apply Now check (`showsApplySip`, alongside the existing `showsApplyNow` for Personal Loan Offer), rendering a button that calls a new `handleApplySip()` in `dashboard.html`/`journey.html`.

**A real bug found and fixed, reported directly by the user**: the journey stage was advancing the moment "Apply Now" was clicked — before the wizard even loaded — because `handleApplySip()` originally called `respondToOffer(..., "accepted", ...)` immediately on click (mirroring the loan flow's own long-standing pattern, which has the identical issue but was never reported for Robert). Since Aria's Consideration stage has only this one offer in it, that single "accepted" response was enough to satisfy her journey's "every offer in this stage must be handled" rule (§25) and advance her right away, regardless of whether she ever finished the wizard. Fixed by deferring: `handleApplySip()` now just stashes `{ offer, journeyId, meta }` in `session.pendingSipOffer` and navigates — no response recorded yet. The real `respondToOffer()` call (and whatever stage advancement it triggers) now happens in `sip-application.html`'s `handleFinalize()`, only once she genuinely completes the wizard. Abandoning (`handleExit()`) records nothing at all — the offer stays unresponded, so it reappears on the dashboard and can be retried, and the stage never moves.

**Segment Analytics added to this flow too**, on a direct follow-up asking whether it fired here the way it does on the loan flow (it didn't — the first version of `sip-application.html` had none). Ported `new-application.html`'s `enableSegmentAnalytics()` verbatim (same snippet, same `identify()`-before-`page()` ordering), firing once at stage 2 — which covers both the completed and abandoned outcomes, since both land on stage 2.

**Brevo event added on abandonment**, on a direct follow-up: `handleExit()` now also fires a Brevo Events API call with `event_name: "pega_nba_decision_sip"` (distinct from the existing Robert-only `"pega_nba_decision"`). `triggerBrevoDecisionEvent(customer)` (`js/cdh.js`) gained an optional `eventName` parameter, defaulting to `"pega_nba_decision"` so every existing caller is unaffected.

**Verified live, both paths, for Aria**: **Abandon** — clicked Apply Now → Continue → Exit; confirmed `currentStageIndex` stayed put, confirmed the SIP offer never entered `responses`/`journeyActionResponses` (reappeared on the dashboard, retriable), confirmed the Activity Ledger logged the Brevo event by name. **Complete** — retried Apply Now → Continue → Finalize; confirmed the stage genuinely advanced, the offer was recorded `"accepted"`, and `session.pendingSipOffer` was cleaned up.

**Not done**: the equivalent flow on `mobile-app.html` — it has its own separate `showsApplyNow`/offer-response mechanism that was never extended to SIP, since this wasn't requested there.

## 44. IDMerge — `keepalive` Fix Applied; the Remaining 401 Is a Pega-Side Auth Gap

Prompted by a direct question: "is the IDMerge API not being called when Robert logs in?" Investigated and found **two separate, real problems**, only one of which is fixable from this codebase.

**Problem 1 (fixed): the request was being cancelled by the browser before it ever left.** `sendIdMerge()` (`js/cdh.js`) is `async` but was never `await`ed by `login.html`'s `selectCustomer()`, which fires it from an `<a href="dashboard.html?...">` link's `onclick` — the browser processes the click synchronously, then immediately follows the link, tearing down the page (and any in-flight, non-`keepalive` fetch on it) before the request could actually be sent. Confirmed directly: instrumenting `fetch()` showed the call starting but never resolving or rejecting before navigation, and it never appeared in the network log at all — then, after redirecting the same call to a local logging server and repeating the exact login click-through, the request **did** arrive, with the correct payload, once `keepalive: true` was added to the fetch options.

**Problem 2 (not fixable here): the real endpoint demands OAuth2 auth this app has never sent, and it's currently rejecting every call.** Calling the real endpoint directly with the app's own exact payload —
```json
{"PrimaryID":"CUST792","SecondaryID":"CookieID-...","ContextName":"Customer","Application":"IREA"}
```
— returns `401 Unauthorized` with a `www-authenticate: Bearer realm="https://instellars02.pegalabs.io/prweb", error="invalid_authorization_header"` header. Compared directly against its sibling endpoints on the same Pega instance to confirm this is unique to IDMerge: `container` returns 200 with no auth at all; `capture_response` returns a real logical 500 ("Outcome is missing") rather than an auth error, meaning it processed the request past the auth layer. Only `IDMerge` demands a Bearer token. The user confirmed this endpoint is *expected* to be unauthenticated in the real environment — so the 401 is a configuration gap on this specific Pega instance, not something to add client-side credentials for. No code change was made for this half; `keepalive: true` is still correct and necessary on its own, and the call should work as-is once the endpoint's auth requirement is resolved on the Pega side.

**A methodology note worth keeping**: the first pass at "verifying" the `keepalive` fix redirected the call to a local echo server that trivially returns 200 for anything — proving delivery, but nothing about whether the *real* endpoint would accept the payload. That's a meaningfully weaker claim than "confirmed working," and it took a direct follow-up ("let me see the payload for which you're getting a positive response") to catch the gap. Worth remembering for future verification passes: proving a request *arrives* is not the same as proving the *real* endpoint accepts it — check the actual response, not a stand-in's.

## 45. Diego Alvarez — New "Priority Banking Upgrade" Gamification Journey (7 Stages)

Direct request: update Diego's profile to reflect that he wants to upgrade to Priority Banking, and build him a customer journey matching a supplied journey-map image — 7 stages (**Discover → Register → Engage → Earn → Scratch → Win → Fulfil**) with specific named actions per stage.

**Profile update** (`js/data.js`, `CUSTOMERS`): Diego (`C1004`) gained `wantsPriorityBankingUpgrade: true`. This isn't just cosmetic — it's the actual eligibility gate on every offer in the new campaign (`eligibility: c => c.id === "C1004" && c.wantsPriorityBankingUpgrade === true`), the same pattern every other single-customer journey in this app already uses for its own flag/id checks.

**New journey** (`js/data.js`): `PRIORITY_BANKING_JOURNEY`, 7 stages, registered in `JOURNEYS` as `{ id: "priority-banking-upgrade", eligibleCustomerId: "C1004" }`:

| Stage | Actions |
|---|---|
| Discover | Register to Win Exciting Gifts |
| Register | Bank More with Us · Spread the Word |
| Engage | Upgrade to Priority Banking · Refer Your Buddy |
| Earn | Track Your Progress · Mission Completed |
| Scratch | 3 Scratch Cards |
| Win | Points Earned Which Can Be Redeemed |
| Fulfil | More Info on How Points Can Be Redeemed |

Ten new `OFFER_CATALOG` entries (one per action) back this journey, each using `buildBadgeIllustration()` with a hand-drawn glyph (gift, bank, share/megaphone, star, two-person referral, progress bar, checkmark, stacked scratch cards, trophy, info) rather than the `buildSceneIllustration()` style used by the older journeys — gives this campaign its own distinct visual identity. `journey.html` and `dashboard.html` needed **zero changes for the journey itself** — both already drive entirely off the `JOURNEYS` registry generically (`getJourneysForCustomer`, `filterOffersForStage`, `renderJourneyMap`), the same mechanism every other journey already uses. Not mirrored into `mobile-app.html` / `agent-desktop.html` — not requested there, same scoping precedent as §40's SIP flow.

**A real gap found while verifying live, and a targeted fix for it.** Calling the real Pega container for Diego succeeds in this environment (`Live` badge, 200 response) but — unsurprisingly, since these action titles are demo-invented, not configured in the actual Pega instance — returns none of them. Before assuming this was specific to the new work, checked whether it's actually a *pre-existing* characteristic: navigated to Aria's existing Scratch & Win journey (already shipped, §-unnumbered/pre-40) and got the exact same `[Registration] CDH responded, but none of the returned actions matched this stage.` — confirming this gap already existed for every "speculative" journey (Scratch & Win, Digital Activation & Onboarding, New Customer Acquisition), not something this session broke. The reason it stays hidden day-to-day: `journey.html`'s `loadStage()` only ever fell back to the local mock catalog on a **hard CDCH call failure** (the `catch` block) — a live call that *succeeds* but matches zero titles fell straight through to "No eligible actions", and unlike `dashboard.html`, `journey.html` never checked `session.outage` at all, so the dashboard's "Simulate outage" toggle couldn't force it either.

Flagged this to the user with three scoping options (fix all four speculative journeys / fix Diego's only / leave as-is) — **fix Diego's journey only** was chosen, to avoid an unrequested behavior change rippling into Scratch & Win, Digital Activation, or New Customer Acquisition. Implemented as a narrowly-scoped branch inside `loadStage()`'s live-call success path: when `journey.id === "priority-banking-upgrade"` and the live response matches zero titles, it now falls back to `filterOffersForStage(scoreOffers(OFFER_CATALOG, customer), stage)` — same computation the catch block already used — sets `source: "mock"` with `error: null` (distinguishing "no live match" from "call failed"), and logs `"...— showing local mock strategy instead."` rather than the older bare message. The status line under the stage header was also split on `data.error` so it reads *"Local mock strategy (live CDH had no match for this campaign)"* for this new path versus *"Local mock strategy (CDH call failed)"* for a genuine outage — an inaccurate label would otherwise make a presenter think CDH itself was down. Every other journey's `loadStage()` branch (the `else` at the bottom of the live-success handler) is untouched byte-for-byte.

**Verified live**: logged in as Diego, confirmed the dashboard's journey map renders all 7 stages with the exact action titles above and no console errors; walked `journey.html` through the Discover stage before and after the fix (before: "No eligible actions…"; after: the "Register to Win Exciting Gifts" card renders with correct title/description/CTA and a correctly-labeled mock-strategy status line); confirmed all 10 new catalog offers pass `eligibility()` for Diego and render distinct, valid badge illustrations (rendered all 10 side-by-side to inspect each glyph directly); confirmed `filterOffersForStage` matches the mock catalog against all 7 stages 1:1 with zero missing/extra titles; re-checked Aria's Scratch & Win journey afterward and confirmed its behavior (and log message wording) is byte-identical to before this fix — the scoped branch did not leak into other journeys.

**A second instance of the same gap, found after the user reported the dashboard still showed nothing.** The fix above only covers `journey.html`'s per-stage pages. `dashboard.html` has its own, entirely separate offer-fetching function (`loadOffers()`) for the "Personalized Offers" sidebar, and it has the identical "live call succeeds, zero title matches, silently returns empty, never triggers a fallback" gap — confirmed live: Diego's Personalized Offers panel said "No personalized offers available right now" even with the `journey.html` fix already in place and after a full `localStorage` clear (ruling out stale cache as the cause).

Fixed with the same scoping choice (Diego only) inside `loadOffers()`'s try block: when `liveOffers` (already filtered down to this customer's journey action titles) comes back empty **and** `customer.id === "C1004"`, it now re-derives the offer list from `scoreOffers(OFFER_CATALOG, customer)` filtered by the same journey-title set, rather than leaving it empty. **Deliberately does not reuse `simulateCDHRequestMock()`** (the function the pre-existing catch-block fallback uses) — that function calls `runNBAStrategy()`, which slices to the top 3 scored offers *before* any journey-title filtering happens. Diego is also generically eligible for a few unrelated lending offers (`personal-loan-offer` at priority 16, `flexi-loan` at 8.96, `easy-credit` at 8.4 — he has `riskFlag: false`) whose priority scores land between his own campaign actions' scores (ranging 3.0–37.8). Slicing to top-3 first, then filtering, would have silently dropped stages like Discover (priority 13.2, ranks 5th overall) entirely. Filtering the *full* scored catalog by journey title first — the same order of operations `journey.html`'s `filterOffersForStage(scoreOffers(...), stage)` already uses — avoids that.

**Verified live**: cleared `localStorage` and logged in as Diego fresh; Personalized Offers now shows his top 3 highest-priority campaign offers (Upgrade to Priority Banking · 3 Scratch Cards · Points Earned Which Can Be Redeemed — priorities 37.8/20.88/19.2, matching a hand-computed check against every one of his 10 offers' `propensity × businessValue × urgency`), correctly labeled "Fallback mode" / "Showing static fallback offers" (the same generic wording the existing outage and catch-block paths already use for any non-live result — left as-is rather than introduced as a new label, since `journey.html`'s more specific wording was a deliberate choice for that page only). No console errors. Re-checked Sam Okafor (New Customer Acquisition, also outside the stage-scoped allowlist at line 316) immediately after — his dashboard rail is untouched, since the new branch is gated on `customer.id === "C1004"` specifically.

**Follow-up: Diego's segment corrected to "Emerging Affluent".** He was originally given `segment: "Small Business"` when this journey was built, which read oddly against a *personal* Priority Banking upgrade campaign. Changed in both `js/data.js` (the eligibility-relevant copy) and `mobile-app.html`'s own inlined `CUSTOMERS` array (kept in sync as a basic profile fact, independent of whether the journey itself is mirrored there). Verified live: dashboard header pill now reads "Emerging Affluent", no console errors.

**Follow-up: the single "3 Scratch Cards" offer was split into three sequential offers** — "This Is Your First Scratch Card", "Your Second Scratch Card, Keep It Up", "Your Third Scratch Card, Looks Like You're a Pro" (`first-scratch-card` / `second-scratch-card` / `third-scratch-card` in `OFFER_CATALOG`, all still `category: "Scratch"`) — replacing the old `three-scratch-cards` entry entirely. `PRIORITY_BANKING_JOURNEY`'s Scratch stage now lists all three titles instead of one; no other file needed touching, since `journey.html`/`dashboard.html` already render however many offers a stage's `actions` array matches (Register and Engage already had two actions each, so this wasn't a new code path). Each card gets a progressively-filled scratch-card-stack glyph (1 card → 2 → 3, same shapes the old single card used) and a slightly richer amber-to-gold palette per card, building toward the "Pro" framing on the third. `points-earned-redeemable`'s and `points-redemption-info`'s `treatmentId`s were renumbered (`T-PBU-WIN-09`→`T-PBU-WIN-11`, `T-PBU-FUL-10`→`T-PBU-FUL-12`) purely to keep the campaign's treatment IDs sequential; nothing reads or matches on that string's numeric suffix. Verified live: all three cards render with correct titles/descriptions/CTAs on the Scratch stage (via the pre-existing catch-block mock fallback, which fired this run instead of the live-empty-match branch — confirming both fallback paths pick up the new offers correctly), and the dashboard journey map's Scratch chip list shows all three new titles. No console errors.

**Follow-up: same split applied to the Win stage** — the single `points-earned-redeemable` offer became three: "500 Points Earned", "2000 Points Earned", "You Have a 3% Cashback Voucher on Travel Spends" (`points-500-earned` / `points-2000-earned` / `cashback-voucher-travel`), all `category: "Win"`. The two points offers reuse the original trophy glyph at two sizes of reward (bronze for 500, the original gold for 2000); the cashback voucher gets a new coupon-shaped glyph (a perforated divider + a "%" made of two circles and a diagonal line) in a travel-blue palette, distinct from the points cards. `treatmentId`s renumbered again to stay sequential (`T-PBU-WIN-11/12/13`, `T-PBU-FUL-12`→`T-PBU-FUL-14`). Verified live: all three cards render correctly on the Win stage with correct titles/descriptions/CTAs, and the dashboard journey map's Win chip list shows all three new titles. No console errors.

**Follow-up: a second Fulfil-stage offer added (additive this time, not a replace)** — "Travel Expense Categories That Qualify for Your Cashback Voucher" (`travel-expense-categories-cashback`), alongside the existing "More Info on How Points Can Be Redeemed" rather than replacing it. Uses a new checklist-style glyph (three bullet-and-line rows) in a travel-blue palette related to, but visually distinct from, the Win stage's cashback voucher card. `treatmentId: T-PBU-FUL-15`. Verified live: both Fulfil cards render correctly with no console errors, and the dashboard journey map's Fulfil chip list shows both titles.

**Follow-up: Diego's dashboard made properly stage-scoped, matching Robert/Aria/Maya's existing pattern.** Three things were asked together: (1) place Diego at the Discover stage the moment he logs in, (2) show only the current stage's offers on his dashboard — not a cross-stage top-3 pull, and (3) only advance him to the next stage once every offer in the current one has been accepted or dismissed. All three already existed in this codebase for three other customers — Robert (`loan-card-crosssell`), Aria (`wealth-management`), and Maya (`digital-activation-onboarding`) — so this was wiring Diego into the same three existing mechanisms rather than building anything new:

1. **Auto-placement** (`login.html`'s `selectCustomer()`): added an `else if (id === "C1004")` branch, same shape as Aria's/Maya's, setting `session.currentJourneyId = "priority-banking-upgrade"` and `session.currentStageIndex = 0` at login.
2. **Stage-only offers** (`dashboard.html`'s `loadOffers()`): added `"priority-banking-upgrade"` to the stage-scoped allowlist at the top of the function (previously `loan-card-crosssell` / `wealth-management` / `digital-activation-onboarding` only). This block is already journey-agnostic — it reads whichever stage `session.currentStageIndex` points to and calls `filterOffersForStage`, so Diego's dashboard now shows exactly one stage's offers at a time, the same way the other three already do.
3. **Gated advancement** (`js/session.js`'s `maybeAdvanceJourneyStage()`): added `"priority-banking-upgrade"` to the function's guard clause. Doing that alone was sufficient — the function's existing `else` branch (previously "Aria's and Maya's journeys... wait until EVERY offer... has been responded to") already implements exactly the rule asked for here, keyed generically off `session.currentJourneyId` and `session.journeyActionResponses`, not hardcoded to specific customers. No new branch needed; Diego's journey simply falls into the same "wait for every offer" bucket Aria and Maya are already in (as opposed to Robert's different "any one accept" rule, which stays untouched and customer-specific to him).

Since Diego now always takes the stage-scoped path, the Diego-specific fallback carve-out added to `loadOffers()`'s *generic* (non-stage-scoped) branch two follow-ups ago became dead code — removed, and the equivalent live-succeeds-zero-match → mock fallback was added inside the *stage-scoped* branch instead, scoped to `journey.id === "priority-banking-upgrade"`, mirroring the same fix already in `journey.html`'s `loadStage()`.

**A scope boundary worth flagging explicitly**: this gate controls what `session.currentStageIndex` points to (and therefore what the dashboard's Personalized Offers panel shows) — it does **not** restrict `journey.html`'s own stepper/Back/Next navigation, which has never been gated for Robert, Aria, or Maya either. A user can still click ahead to any stage number directly from `journey.html`'s stepper regardless of whether the current stage's offers are fully handled; doing so overwrites `session.currentStageIndex` to wherever they clicked. This is pre-existing behavior across all four stage-scoped journeys now, not something introduced or left half-done here — flagging it in case "progression" was meant to also lock down that manual navigation, which would be a different, additional change.

Also left as-is: Diego's journey map on the dashboard was **not** hidden, unlike Robert/Aria/Maya's (which are hidden via a `display:none` gate on `["CUST101", PRIYA_CUSTOMER_ID, MAYA_CUSTOMER_ID]`, precisely because their Personalized Offers panel already shows the same stage-scoped content and the map would be redundant). The same redundancy argument now technically applies to Diego too, but hiding his map wasn't part of what was asked, and his is a longer 7-stage journey where an overview map arguably has more standalone value — left visible; easy to fold into that same hidden list later if wanted.

**Verified live** (had to work around a same-origin static-file-server sub-resource caching quirk during testing — see note below): logged in as Diego fresh — dashboard immediately showed only "Register to Win Exciting Gifts" tagged "DISCOVER STAGE"; accepted it, confirmed `currentStageIndex` advanced to Register (2 offers: "Bank More with Us", "Spread the Word") and the panel updated to show both; dismissed one, confirmed the stage correctly did **not** advance (`currentStageIndex` stayed put) with the other still pending; accepted the second, confirmed it then advanced to Engage and the panel updated accordingly. No console errors at any step.

*(Testing note, not a shipped-code issue: `python -m http.server`, used only for local verification, sends no cache-control headers, so a browser tab that already had an older `js/session.js` cached in this same testing session kept using the stale copy across `force`-reload navigations of the top-level HTML — sub-resource `<script src>` caching isn't bypassed by navigating the parent document. Worked around by re-fetching each shared JS file with `cache: "no-store"` and `eval`-ing it into the page, and later by simply serving from a fresh port for a genuinely empty cache partition. Not a real-user-facing issue — an actual visitor's first load has no stale cache to begin with.)*

---

## 46. Diego's Engage Stage — Third Offer, Mission-Progress Tracking, "Register" CTA

A four-part request, all about Diego's `priority-banking-upgrade` campaign specifically:

1. **New Engage-stage offer**: "Open a New Investment Account" (`open-new-investment-account` in `OFFER_CATALOG`, a green upward-trend glyph via `buildBadgeIllustration`). Engage now has three actions: `["Upgrade to Priority Banking", "Refer Your Buddy", "Open a New Investment Account"]`. All downstream `treatmentId`s from Engage onward were renumbered to stay sequential (`T-PBU-ENG-06` through `T-PBU-FUL-16`) — same purely-cosmetic renumbering done in every earlier follow-up.
2. **Named the mission structure explicitly**: "Bank More with Us" and "Spread the Word" (Register stage) are the two missions the customer registers for. "Upgrade to Priority Banking" + "Open a New Investment Account" (Engage) both count toward the "Bank More with Us" mission; "Refer Your Buddy" (Engage) is what completes the "Spread the Word" mission.
3. **"Track Your Progress" (Earn) becomes dynamic**: its description is rewritten at display time to "0% / 50% / 100% Complete — N of 2 'Bank More with Us' missions done" based on how many of the two tracked Engage actions were *accepted* (not just responded to — a dismiss doesn't count toward progress, only toward the existing "every offer handled" stage-advancement gate, which is a separate check).
4. **"Mission Completed" (Earn) becomes conditional**: only shown if "Refer Your Buddy" was accepted; otherwise filtered out entirely.
5. **"Register" CTA**: "Bank More with Us" and "Spread the Word" always show a "Register" button, regardless of whether the offer object came from the mock catalog or a live CDH match (`mapPegaOffer`, `js/cdh.js`, always hardcodes `cta: "View offer"` on a live-mapped offer, having no notion these two are campaign enrollments rather than generic offers).

**Implementation**: a single new function, `applyPriorityBankingCampaignAdjustments(offers, session, stage)` in `js/cdh.js`, called from the same choke point as the existing `applyOfferedRateToOffers` in both `dashboard.html`'s stage-scoped block and `journey.html`'s `loadStage()` — i.e. once, after whichever try/catch branch populated that stage's offers, regardless of which of the three branches (live match, live-zero-match fallback, catch fallback) supplied them. It: (a) unconditionally maps a "Register" cta onto matching titles wherever found — harmless as a no-op on any stage that doesn't have them; (b) when `stage.stage === "Earn"`, rewrites "Track Your Progress"'s description from the accepted-count of the two tracked Engage titles, and filters "Mission Completed" out unless "Refer Your Buddy" was accepted, silently auto-recording `journeyActionResponses["mission completed"] = "dismissed"` in that case so it doesn't block Earn-stage advancement despite never being shown (mirrors the same "auto-resolve so a hidden action doesn't deadlock the stage gate" reasoning used for other conditional actions elsewhere in this app).

**A real bug found and fixed mid-implementation, not just a testing artifact this time**: the first version of `applyPriorityBankingCampaignAdjustments` didn't take a `stage` parameter and ran its Mission-Completed auto-resolve logic unconditionally whenever `journey.id === "priority-banking-upgrade"` — which is *every* stage load, not just Earn. Caught by testing the full flow in order (Discover → Register → Engage) rather than jumping straight to Earn: the very first time Diego's *Engage* stage loaded — before he had ever gotten the chance to respond to "Refer Your Buddy" — the function ran, saw `referBuddyAccepted === false` (nothing had been answered yet) and `journeyActionResponses["mission completed"] === undefined`, and permanently wrote `"dismissed"` right there. By the time the customer actually reached Earn stage and had genuinely accepted "Refer Your Buddy", "Mission Completed" was already locked out and would never show again. Fixed by passing `stage` into the function and gating the entire auto-resolve/filter block on `stage.stage === "Earn"`; the CTA-override half stays unconditional since it's a pure per-title map with no side effect to guard.

**Verified live** (a fresh origin/port was used per test run to sidestep the `http.server` sub-resource caching issue noted in §45, since this feature specifically needed to test stage-to-stage transitions in their real order rather than jumping directly to Earn): confirmed `journeyActionResponses` stays empty through Discover and Register; confirmed it's still clean arriving at Engage (catching the bug before the fix would have shown "mission completed" already present at this point); confirmed both "Bank More with Us" and "Spread the Word" render a "Register" button, including against a live-matched Pega offer (not just the mock fallback); confirmed Engage shows all three offers including the new "Open a New Investment Account"; ran the full accepted-count matrix directly against the pure function (0/1/2 accepted × Refer Your Buddy accepted/dismissed, six combinations) and confirmed every description/filter/auto-resolve output was correct; then, with the fix applied, walked a real accept-two-of-three-plus-refer-accepted scenario end to end and confirmed Earn stage showed both "Mission Completed" and "Track your Progress" — "50% Complete — 1 of 2 'Bank More with Us' missions done toward your Priority Banking upgrade" — exactly matching the accepted count. No console errors at any step.

---

## 47. Diego's Campaign — Full Mission-Gated Cascade Through Engage → Earn → Scratch → Win, Plus a Visual Progress Bar

A large follow-up to §46 that turns "Bank More with Us" and "Spread the Word" (Register stage) into genuine mission gates controlling visibility all the way through the rest of the journey, given a screenshot of an unrelated "Grow your balances" card as a style reference for a progress bar. Five rules, all implemented in one place:

1. **"Refer Your Buddy" (Engage) shows only if "Spread the Word" (Register) was accepted.** Otherwise hidden.
2. **"Upgrade to Priority Banking" and "Open a New Investment Account" (Engage) show only if "Bank More with Us" (Register) was accepted.** Otherwise hidden.
3. **"Mission Completed" (Earn) gets dynamic text naming whichever mission(s) actually reached 100%** — "Bank More with Us" (both its Engage actions accepted), "Spread the Word" (Refer Your Buddy accepted), or both. Hidden if neither is complete.
4. **"Track Your Progress" (Earn) tracks combined progress across whichever mission(s) were registered for**, as a percentage (accepted actions ÷ total applicable actions) — plus a new visual progress bar, styled after the reference screenshot's rounded track + circular step markers + filled portion.
5. **Scratch and Win stages cascade the same gating one level further**: completing "Bank More with Us" (both Engage actions accepted) unlocks the first two Scratch cards; completing "Spread the Word" unlocks the third. Then each Win-stage reward is gated on the matching Scratch card having actually been accepted — first card → "500 Points Earned", second → "2000 Points Earned", third → the cashback voucher.

**Implementation**: `applyPriorityBankingCampaignAdjustments` (`js/cdh.js`) was substantially rewritten (still the same function, same call sites, same `(offers, session, stage)` signature from §46) to branch on `stage.stage` across four stages now instead of one:
- **Engage**: filters to only the actions whose registering mission was accepted; auto-resolves ("dismissed") whichever of the three titles got filtered out, so a customer who registers for only one mission doesn't get stuck unable to advance past Engage.
- **Earn**: computes `totalSteps`/`acceptedSteps` across whichever mission(s) were registered (Bank More with Us contributes up to 2 steps, Spread the Word up to 1), derives a percent, and attaches `progressPercent`/`progressSteps` to the "Track Your Progress" offer object alongside its rewritten description; separately computes which mission(s) are fully complete for "Mission Completed"'s dynamic text and visibility.
- **Scratch**: filters the three cards on `bankMoreComplete`/`spreadWordComplete` (not just "registered" — actually complete).
- **Win**: filters the three rewards on whether the *matching* scratch card was individually accepted.

Every filtered-out title gets the same "auto-resolve to dismissed if not already recorded" treatment established in §46, extended to all four stages now — this is what keeps a customer who, say, registers for only "Spread the Word" from ever getting stuck on Engage (2 offers auto-resolved), Scratch (2 cards auto-resolved), or Win (2 rewards auto-resolved) despite never seeing those cards. Tested the extreme edge case directly too — dismissing *both* Register-stage missions cascades all the way through: Engage, Scratch, and Win each end up with zero visible offers and everything auto-resolved, so the journey just glides through to Fulfil with nothing to interact with in between. Not prevented — a demo presenter showing "customer isn't interested" is a legitimate scenario, not a bug.

**Progress bar**: a new `renderProgressBar(percent, steps)` helper in `js/render-shared.js`, called from `renderOfferCard()` whenever `offer.progressPercent != null` (currently only ever true for "Track Your Progress"). Renders a rounded track, a filled portion sized to `percent`, and `steps + 1` evenly-spaced circular dot markers (filled once "reached") — new CSS added to `css/app.css` right after `.bnk-card-desc` (`.bnk-progress-bar`/`-track`/`-fill`/`-dot`/`-dot-done`/`-percent-label`), using the existing `--brass` token for the fill/filled-dot color so it matches the rest of the app's blue accent rather than introducing a new color. This is a generic, reusable card feature now — any future offer that sets `progressPercent`/`progressSteps` gets the same bar for free.

**Verified live**, matrix-first then a full walkthrough: ran all 14 meaningfully-distinct scenarios directly against the pure function first (both Register combinations × Engage; five Earn combinations covering partial/complete/neither-registered mixes; three Scratch combinations; two Win combinations) — every title list, description, progress percent, and auto-resolved `journeyActionResponses` entry matched expectations exactly, including the 33%/3-step case when both missions are registered but neither individually complete. Then walked a real "Bank More with Us only" run stage-by-stage in order (Register → dismiss Spread the Word → Engage shows only the two Bank-More actions, live-returned "Refer your Buddy" correctly absent from the visible list despite CDH returning it → accept both → Earn shows "Mission Completed" with the correct dynamic text and "Track your Progress" at 100%, screenshotted to confirm the progress bar itself renders correctly (three dots, all filled, blue track) → Scratch shows only the first two cards → accept both → Win shows only 500 and 2000 points, cashback voucher correctly absent). No console errors at any point across the full run.

---

## 48. Earn Stage Split Into Per-Mission Pairs — "Track Your Progress"/"Mission Completed" for Each of the Two Missions

Direct follow-up to §47's combined-with-dynamic-text design: rather than one "Track Your Progress" and one "Mission Completed" that switch their wording depending on which mission(s) applied, Earn stage now has **two independent pairs**, one per mission — "Track Your Progress" / "Mission Completed" for Bank More with Us (the original two, `OFFER_CATALOG` ids `track-your-progress` / `mission-completed`, both now given a static, mission-specific description instead of dynamically-combined text), and two new ones for Spread the Word: **"Track Your Progress for Spread the Word"** (`track-progress-spread-word`) and **"Mission Completed for Spread the Word"** (`mission-completed-spread-word`). Visually distinguished with a purple palette (matching Refer Your Buddy's own color) rather than the original's gold/green, reusing the same progress-bar/checkmark glyph shapes. `PRIORITY_BANKING_JOURNEY`'s Earn stage now lists all four action titles; every downstream `treatmentId` from Earn onward was renumbered to stay sequential again (`T-PBU-EARN-07..10`, `T-PBU-SCR-11..13`, `T-PBU-WIN-14..16`, `T-PBU-FUL-17..18`).

This actually **simplified** `applyPriorityBankingCampaignAdjustments`'s Earn-stage branch (`js/cdh.js`) versus §47's version — no more combined step-counting across missions, no more "and"/"&" text-joining logic for a possible two-mission case. Each of the four offers is now independently gated on its own single mission: `Track Your Progress` shows (0/50/100%) only if Bank More with Us was registered; `Mission Completed` shows only once it's complete; the two `*Spread the Word` counterparts mirror that against `spreadWordAccepted`/`spreadWordComplete` (Spread the Word only has one underlying action, so its progress is inherently binary — 0% or 100%, never 50%). Same auto-resolve-to-"dismissed"-if-hidden treatment as every other gate in §46/§47, now covering all four Earn titles individually instead of two.

**Verified live**: ran all 7 meaningfully-distinct scenarios (Bank More complete/partial/not-registered × Spread the Word complete/registered-not-complete/not-registered, plus both-registered-neither-complete, plus neither-registered) directly against the function — every title list, percentage, and auto-resolved response matched expectations, including the "neither registered" case correctly auto-resolving all four titles with zero cards shown (no deadlock). Rendered "Track Your Progress for Spread the Word" and "Mission Completed for Spread the Word" side-by-side directly via `renderOfferCard` and screenshotted them — the new purple badge and a correctly-filled 2-dot (0%/100%) progress bar render cleanly, matching the visual language established in §47. No console errors.

---

## 49. Register Auto-Advance, Available Missions/Milestones, Investment Account Wizard, More CTA Overrides — Shipped Without Live Verification

**Explicitly not live-tested this round** — the user asked for these changes to be made and shipped without regression testing, planning one consolidated regression pass once everything in this stretch (§46–§49) is done. Everything below was implemented by careful mirroring of already-proven patterns elsewhere in this codebase and a full manual read-through of every changed region for syntax/logic correctness, but none of it has been exercised in a real browser yet. Whoever picks this up next should treat §49 specifically as unverified until that regression pass happens.

**1. Register stage now advances the moment EITHER mission is accepted.** New branch in `maybeAdvanceJourneyStage` (`js/session.js`): `session.currentJourneyId === "priority-banking-upgrade" && stage.stage === "Register"` → `shouldAdvance = status === "accepted"`, ahead of the generic "wait for every offer" `else` branch that still governs every other stage of Diego's journey (and Aria's/Maya's journeys entirely). Whichever mission he didn't touch is left with no recorded response — not auto-resolved, since it's still meant to be genuinely offered later.

**2 & 3. Two new Diego-only dashboard sections** surface exactly what "not auto-resolved" in point 1 leaves behind: **Available Missions** (Register-stage missions with no recorded response yet) and **Available Milestones** (Engage-stage actions already unlocked by an accepted mission, not yet accepted or dismissed). Two new pure functions in `js/cdh.js`, `getDiegoAvailableMissions(session, customer)` and `getDiegoAvailableMilestones(session, customer)`, both filtering `scoreOffers(OFFER_CATALOG, customer)` by title against `session.journeyActionResponses` — deliberately NOT routed through `applyPriorityBankingCampaignAdjustments` (that function's auto-resolve side effects only make sense while genuinely viewing that specific stage). Rendered in `dashboard.html` as two more `.bnk-sidebar-panel` blocks below Personalized Offers, gated on `customer.id === "C1004"`, computed once on initial page load and recomputed inside `handleRespond` after every response (accepting a mission can newly unlock a milestone the very next line). Since these offers live outside `dashState.offers`, `handleRespond` and the new `handleApplyInvestmentAccount` (point 4) now look offers up via a new `findAnyDashOffer(offerId)` helper that checks `dashState.offers` → `dashState.availableMissions` → `dashState.availableMilestones` in order, instead of `dashState.offers.find()` alone.

**4. "Open a New Investment Account" gets its own dedicated wizard**, `investment-application.html` — a near-exact structural mirror of Aria's `sip-application.html` (same 3-step shape: Basic Info → Account Details → Confirmation/Abandoned, same deferred-response pattern via `session.pendingInvestmentOffer` + `resetInvestmentApplication()` (new, `js/session.js`, mirrors `resetSipApplication`), same Segment Analytics gating to stage 2 only, same `handleExit()`-records-nothing / `handleFinalize()`-records-the-real-response split, same `triggerBrevoDecisionEvent` call on abandon but with its own event name `"pega_nba_decision_investment"`). Fields were renamed for an account-opening context rather than a recurring SIP: "Initial deposit amount" (was "Monthly SIP amount"), "Investment horizon" (was "Investment tenure"), "Account type" (was "Fund type", options: Mutual Fund Portfolio / Direct Equity Account / Fixed Income Fund / Balanced Advantage Fund). `renderOfferCard` (`js/render-shared.js`) gained a `showsApplyInvestment` check (title-matched to "Open a New Investment Account", same pattern as `showsApplySip`) rendering an Apply Now button that calls a new `handleApplyInvestmentAccount(offerId)` — added to both `dashboard.html` and `journey.html`, mirroring `handleApplySip` in each exactly.

**5 & 6. Two more forced CTAs**, extending the override mechanism from §46/§47: "Upgrade to Priority Banking" → **"Upgrade"**, "Refer Your Buddy" → **"Refer"**. The single-title-list check in `applyPriorityBankingCampaignAdjustments` was replaced with a `ctaOverrides` lookup object covering all four forced titles now (Register/Register/Upgrade/Refer), still applied unconditionally as a harmless no-op on stages without these titles. The same `ctaOverride` mapping is duplicated (small, two-entry) inside `getDiegoAvailableMilestones` so milestones shown in that new dashboard section carry the same button labels as when shown on the Engage stage itself.

**Scope discipline carried through as asked**: every change above is gated on `customer.id === "C1004"` or `session.currentJourneyId === "priority-banking-upgrade"` specifically — the Register/Engage/Earn/Scratch/Win generic branches for Robert/Aria/Maya are untouched, and `sip-application.html` itself was not modified (a new, separate file was created instead of parameterizing the existing one).

**Follow-up, same "no regression test, just ship" instruction**: Earn stage now advances the moment either "Mission Completed" or "Mission Completed for Spread the Word" is accepted — a fourth `else if` branch in `maybeAdvanceJourneyStage`, same shape as Register's (point 1 above): `shouldAdvance = status === "accepted" && missionCompletedTitles.includes(...)`, checked ahead of the generic "wait for every offer" fallback that still governs every other journey. Track Your Progress (either mission's) no longer needs a response to leave Earn stage — only an actual Mission Completed accept does.

Noted but deliberately not hardened against: if *neither* mission ever reaches completion, no "Mission Completed" card is ever shown (per §46–48's gating), so nothing can trigger this new rule via a click. This was already true of the *old* generic rule too, though — `maybeAdvanceJourneyStage` only ever runs inside `respondToOffer`, so a stage with zero visible offers could never auto-advance under either rule without a click to fire from. Not a new regression, and not a true deadlock either: `journey.html`'s own Back/Next navigation has never been gated on stage completion (see §45/§49's scope-boundary notes) — a demo presenter can always click through manually. Only the dashboard's auto-tracked `currentStageIndex` would linger on an empty Earn panel in that specific combination.

**Follow-up, same instruction again**: Scratch-stage unlocking was re-keyed off *accepting* the Mission Completed card, not merely off the underlying mission being complete. Previously `bankMoreComplete`/`spreadWordComplete` (both derived purely from Engage-stage responses) gated the first-two/third Scratch cards; now two new booleans, `missionCompletedAccepted` (`jar[norm("Mission Completed")] === "accepted"`) and `missionCompletedSpreadAccepted` (same for the Spread the Word variant), do — the reward reveal now follows the explicit "claim" click rather than happening silently the instant the last Engage action is accepted. `bankMoreComplete`/`spreadWordComplete` are still used exactly as before for the Mission Completed *cards'* own Earn-stage eligibility (still gated on the mission actually being complete) — only the Scratch-stage gate changed which signal it reads.

**A real interaction worth flagging, not fixed**: combined with the previous follow-up (accepting *either* Mission Completed variant now advances straight to Scratch), if a customer completes *both* missions before claiming either, whichever Mission Completed card they accept first fires the stage advance immediately — leaving the *other* mission's Mission Completed card behind on Earn, unaccepted, with no route back to it (Earn's own stage-scoped dashboard panel no longer shows once `currentStageIndex` has moved on). Under the new Scratch gate, that mission's cards then never unlock, even though the mission itself is genuinely complete. This wasn't asked about and hasn't been fixed — flagging it since it's a real gap in the two-missions-simultaneously path specifically (the single-mission path, almost certainly the primary demo scenario, works exactly as intended). A fix, if wanted later, would likely mirror the existing "Available Missions" pattern — e.g. an "Available Rewards" dashboard section surfacing any Mission Completed card left behind the same way.

**Follow-up, same instruction again**: registering for a mission via Available Missions now also merges that mission's Engage-stage action(s) directly into the Personalized Offers panel, not just Available Milestones. New function `getDiegoEngageOffersForMission(missionTitle, session, customer)` (`js/cdh.js`, shares its title/cta mapping with `getDiegoAvailableMilestones` — same data, just scoped to one mission instead of both) returns the mission's own unresponded Engage action(s); `dashboard.html`'s `handleRespond` captures whether the just-responded offer came from `dashState.availableMissions` (before the response overwrites that array), and if so, pushes the newly-unlocked offer(s) straight into `dashState.offers` (deduped by title), then persists `session.lastFetchedOfferIds`.

This surfaced a real, pre-existing display bug while implementing it: the Personalized Offers panel's render logic caps to `dashState.offers.slice(0, 3)` for every journey except Priya's (`loan-card-crosssell`, which has its own carve-out for exactly this reason — a stage can legitimately have more than 3 actions). Diego's journey was never given that same carve-out, so his Earn stage — which can already show up to 4 cards at once (both missions' Track Your Progress *and* Mission Completed, once both are complete and neither claimed yet) — was silently dropping the 4th card off the bottom of the panel even before this change, and the newly-merged Engage offer(s) from this fix would have been silently dropped the same way whenever the current stage already had 3 shown. Fixed by extending the existing `isPriyaJourney` exception to also cover `isDiegoJourney` (`session.currentJourneyId === "priority-banking-upgrade"`) — his Personalized Offers panel now shows however many offers `dashState.offers` actually holds, uncapped, same as Priya's.

**A second, more fundamental bug — this one user-reported ("I don't think its working"), actually investigated live rather than shipped blind.** Registering a mission late via Available Missions did nothing: the newly-unlocked Engage action never appeared anywhere, not even in Available Milestones. Root cause: `applyPriorityBankingCampaignAdjustments`'s own Engage-stage auto-resolve (§46) had already run once — the very first time Diego viewed the Engage stage, while the *other* mission was still unregistered — and permanently marked that mission's Engage action(s) `"dismissed"` in `journeyActionResponses`, purely as a deadlock-avoidance side effect (so Engage stage's generic "wait for every offer" advancement rule wouldn't hang forever on a title that was never going to be shown). That auto-resolve had no way to know Available Missions would let the customer register the mission *afterward* — so by the time he did, every downstream check (`getDiegoAvailableMissions`, `getDiegoAvailableMilestones`, `getDiegoEngageOffersForMission`) correctly-but-unhelpfully saw an already-"dismissed" entry and treated it as handled for good.

Fixed with a new function, `clearStaleMissionEngageResolve(missionTitle, session)` (`js/cdh.js`, alongside a new shared `DIEGO_ENGAGE_TITLES_BY_MISSION` map both this and `getDiegoEngageOffersForMission` now read from instead of each keeping its own copy) — deletes a mission's Engage title(s) from `journeyActionResponses` if and only if their current value is `"dismissed"` (never touches a genuine `"accepted"`, which couldn't exist yet for an unregistered mission's actions anyway, since the visibility filter would have hidden them from ever being clicked). Called from `dashboard.html`'s `handleRespond`, right after `respondToOffer()` records the mission's own acceptance and *before* `getDiegoAvailableMissions`/`getDiegoAvailableMilestones`/`getDiegoEngageOffersForMission` are computed — otherwise those three would keep reading the stale value even after the clear.

**Verified live this time** (the bug report justified breaking from the "ship without testing" pattern for just this fix): reproduced the exact failure first — registered Bank More with Us, let Engage stage auto-resolve "Refer Your Buddy" to "dismissed" by viewing the dashboard, confirmed via `loadSession().journeyActionResponses` that it really was stuck at `"dismissed"` — then registered Spread the Word via Available Missions and confirmed the same lookup now showed it correctly cleared, with "Refer Your Buddy" (labeled "Refer", per §49) rendering in Personalized Offers immediately, no console errors, no separate reload needed.

---

## 50. Engage → Earn Advances on Mission Completion; Two More Instances of the Same Staleness Bug Found and Fixed

Direct question, then a direct request: does Engage advance to Earn once a mission is complete? It didn't — Engage still used the generic "every offer in the stage has a response" rule. Added a fourth journey-specific branch to `maybeAdvanceJourneyStage` (`js/session.js`), same shape as Register's and Earn's: `shouldAdvance = bankMoreComplete || spreadWordComplete` (Bank More with Us needs both its Engage actions accepted; Spread the Word needs just Refer Your Buddy). If both missions are registered and only one completes first, the other's still-unresponded action stays reachable afterward via Available Milestones rather than being stranded — confirmed this holds via live testing below.

**This surfaced the same staleness bug class twice more while verifying it**, both caught live before shipping (this section, unlike most of §49, was fully tested — a stage-advancement rule is exactly the kind of change worth verifying before calling done):

1. **Registering both missions, then completing Bank More first**, correctly advanced Engage → Earn — but left "Refer Your Buddy" needing to be completed later via Available Milestones, same as any Engage leftover. Accepting it there worked correctly (already covered by §49's fix), *except* that Earn stage's own auto-resolve had, by then, already stashed a stale `"dismissed"` on **"Mission Completed for Spread the Word"** — the exact same staleness class as §49's Engage-stage bug, one stage further along. Fixed with a new pair of functions in `js/cdh.js`: `clearStaleMissionCompletedResolve(session)` (clears a mission's Mission Completed title if it's carrying a stale `"dismissed"` and the mission is now genuinely complete) and `getDiegoNewlyCompletedMissionOffers(session, customer)` (returns any Mission Completed variant that just became eligible and unresponded, for merging into Personalized Offers — mirrors `getDiegoEngageOffersForMission` one stage later). Both called unconditionally on every response for Diego in `dashboard.html`'s `handleRespond`, right alongside the existing Engage-level clearing. This also closes the "known gap, not fixed" flagged at the end of the previous follow-up in §49 (completing both missions before claiming either no longer strands the second one).

2. **A related but distinct staleness**: even after the fix above made "Mission Completed for Spread the Word" reappear correctly, "Track Your Progress for Spread the Word" — already sitting in `dashState.offers` from Earn stage's original fetch — kept showing its old "0% Complete" text, since accepting Refer Your Buddy via Available Milestones changes *that* card's progress without it being the card actually clicked. Fixed by refreshing both Track Your Progress cards' `description`/`progressPercent` in place from current `journeyActionResponses` on every response, rather than only ever setting them once at initial fetch time.

**Verified live**: ran the Engage-advancement rule directly against four scenarios first (accept one of two Bank More actions → no advance; accept both → advances; accept Spread the Word's one action → advances; dismiss → no advance) — all four correct. Then walked the full real scenario end to end: registered both missions, completed Bank More's two Engage actions (confirmed stage stayed on Engage after the first, advanced to Earn after the second), confirmed "Refer Your Buddy" reachable via Available Milestones, accepted it there, and confirmed — all in the live-rendered Personalized Offers panel, no console errors — "Mission Completed for Spread the Word" now appears with its correct description and "Claim reward" button, and both Track Your Progress cards show accurate, non-stale percentages (100%/100%).

## 51. Scratch and Win Merged Into One "Scratch & Win" Stage

Direct request. `PRIORITY_BANKING_JOURNEY`'s two separate 3-action stages (`Scratch`, `Win`) became one 6-action stage, `"Scratch & Win"` (`js/data.js`) — Diego's journey is now 6 stages instead of 7 (Discover, Register, Engage, Earn, Scratch & Win, Fulfil). No other file needed a code change for the stage-count reduction itself — the stepper, journey map, and dashboard's stage-scoped fetch are all already driven generically off `journey.stages`, and a grep across the codebase turned up no other hardcoded `"Scratch"` / `"Win"` stage-name checks outside `applyPriorityBankingCampaignAdjustments` itself.

**That function's Scratch and Win branches were merged into one** (`js/cdh.js`, keyed on `stage.stage === "Scratch & Win"`) — combining the filter conditions is the easy part; the auto-resolve logic needed rethinking, not just concatenating. Previously, Win's auto-resolve fired on `!firstScratchAccepted`/`!secondScratchAccepted`/`!thirdScratchAccepted` — safe back when Win was its own stage, reachable only after Scratch had already fully resolved, so "not yet scratched" and "will never be scratched" were the same thing by the time Win's own auto-resolve ever ran. Merged into one stage, the *first* view of it now happens *before* anything's been scratched — reusing that same condition unmodified would have auto-marked every reward "dismissed" the instant the stage first loaded, reintroducing the exact §49/§50 premature-lock bug one stage further along, before the customer ever got a chance to scratch anything.

Fixed by rethinking rather than patching: each reward's auto-resolve is now keyed off the same *permanent* condition that gates its matching scratch card — `missionCompletedAccepted` / `missionCompletedSpreadAccepted` — not off whether that specific card has been scratched yet. A reward only gets auto-resolved if its card was never going to be reachable *at all* (mission never completed); if the mission is complete but the card just hasn't been scratched yet, the reward simply stays hidden and unresolved (visibility still correctly checks the immediate `firstScratchAccepted`/etc.), naturally becoming visible the moment its card actually gets scratched — no stale-clearing helper needed this time, because the bug was avoided at the source instead of patched after the fact.

**Verified live before shipping** (a design choice with real deadlock risk if gotten wrong, same bar as §50): ran four scenarios directly against the merged function — mission complete with nothing scratched yet (the critical first-view case: both scratch cards showed, and critically neither `journeyActionResponses` entry for their rewards was written at all, not even "dismissed" — confirming no premature lock); mission complete with the first card scratched (500 Points Earned correctly newly appeared); both missions complete with everything scratched (all six titles present); neither mission complete (everything correctly auto-resolved, zero cards, no deadlock). Rendered the "first card scratched" scenario directly via `renderOfferCard` and confirmed the second scratch card, first scratch card, and the newly-unlocked 500 Points reward all render together with their own distinct SCRATCH/WIN category labels. No console errors.

---

## 52. Container API Called Exactly Once Per Session for Diego — Every Stage After That Filters the Same Cached Response

Direct instruction, refined mid-turn: call the real Container API only once, the first time Diego's journey loads (in practice, the moment his Discover stage first renders, right after login) — never again for the rest of his session, and **not** falling back to the local mock catalog when a stage's titles aren't in that one response (an explicit correction to my first-draft plan, which would have quietly reused the existing mock-fallback pattern). Instead: cache the one-time response, and filter that *same* cached list through the existing title-based journey mapping (`filterOffersForStage`) for every stage, every time.

**Implementation**: two new functions in `js/cdh.js`. `ensureDiegoContainerOffers(session, customer)` — async, no-ops immediately if `session.diegoContainerOffers` is already set (the actual "only once" gate), otherwise calls `simulateCDHRequest(customer)` a single time and caches both `offers` and `meta` onto the session; on failure, still caches an empty array (not `null`) so a failed attempt doesn't retry on every subsequent stage — it just means no personalized offers show for the rest of that session, logged plainly either way. `getDiegoContainerOfferByTitle(session, title)` — a single-title lookup against that same cache, normalized the same way `filterOffersForStage` already matches.

Both `dashboard.html`'s stage-scoped block and `journey.html`'s `loadStage()` got a new `if (journey.id === "priority-banking-upgrade") { ... } else { <existing try/catch, unchanged> }` split — Diego's branch calls `ensureDiegoContainerOffers` then filters the cache directly, no try/catch, no live call, no mock fallback. This let two now-obsolete pieces get deleted outright rather than left as dead code: the "live-succeeds-zero-match → fall back to mock" special case built for Diego in §45/§46 (impossible to hit now — he never makes a per-stage live call to begin with) and its accompanying commentary.

**The four dashboard-only helper functions from §49/§50** (`getDiegoAvailableMissions`, `getDiegoAvailableMilestones`, `getDiegoEngageOffersForMission`, `getDiegoNewlyCompletedMissionOffers`) were switched from scoring the local mock catalog (`scoreOffers(OFFER_CATALOG, customer)`) to `getDiegoContainerOfferByTitle` — so Available Missions and Available Milestones now source from the exact same single cached response as every stage view, never the mock catalog, keeping every surface on Diego's dashboard consistent with the one-and-only container call.

**A real, material risk, stated plainly rather than quietly absorbed**: this campaign's action titles are demo-invented, not reliably configured in the real Pega container (documented extensively since §45). If the one login-time call doesn't happen to return a given stage's titles, that stage will show zero personalized offers for the *entire rest of that session* — there is no retry, no fallback, by design. Whether this is a problem depends entirely on what the live container actually returns at the moment Diego logs in, which has varied across this whole project. Implemented exactly as instructed rather than second-guessed, since the explicit "don't use fallback" correction made the intent unambiguous — but flagging it here since a session where the initial call returns few or no matching titles will visibly look broken (empty stages, nothing to click) despite the code behaving exactly as designed.

**Verified live**: logged in as Diego fresh, confirmed `session.diegoContainerOffers` populated (39 offers) after the very first Discover-stage load, and confirmed via the session log's one-time marker string that it appeared exactly once — then walked forward through Discover → Register → Engage (registering Bank More with Us along the way), re-checking that marker count after every single step: stayed at 1 throughout. Confirmed Register stage, Engage stage, and Available Missions all rendered correct, consistent offers pulled from that same cached response (including Available Missions correctly narrowing to just "Spread the Word" once Bank More with Us was registered), with the "Strategy NextBestAction · 7341ms" meta line staying fixed at the original call's latency throughout — proof it's genuinely reused, not refetched. No console errors at any step. (The browser tool's network-request log didn't capture the cross-origin POST to the CDH endpoint itself, so call-count verification relied on the session log marker instead, which is authoritative here since `ensureDiegoContainerOffers` writes it exactly once per real call.)

---

## 53. §52's "Missing" Title Wasn't Missing — It Was a One-Word Naming Mismatch With the Real Pega Container

Direct follow-up to §52's stated risk, which materialized almost immediately: the user reported that after completing the Spread the Word mission (having already completed Bank More with Us), neither "Track Your Progress for Spread the Word" nor "Mission Completed for Spread the Word" showed in Personalized Offers. Reproduced live first rather than guessing — walked the exact scenario (register both missions, complete Bank More's two Engage actions, complete Refer Your Buddy via Available Milestones) and confirmed: "Track Your Progress for Spread the Word" rendered correctly and updated to 100% as expected, but "Mission Completed for Spread the Word" never appeared at all, at any point.

Checked `session.diegoContainerOffers` directly against all 15 of Diego's campaign titles: 14 present, exactly one absent — "Mission Completed for Spread the Word". Under §52's now-strict "cache only, no fallback" rule, a title genuinely missing from the one-time response can never be found, by design — so before assuming that was simply this session's bad luck (a real possibility given how "sometimes matches, sometimes doesn't" this container's real-time arbitration had been throughout the project), the user asked for a direct live check: call the real Container API right now and see what's actually configured.

**Called the live endpoint directly** (`curl` against `https://instellars02.pegalabs.io/prweb/api/PegaMKTContainer/v4/container`, the exact same request shape `simulateCDHRequest` sends, for `SubjectID: "C1004"`) and inspected the raw JSON. All 39 offers came back, including one with `"Name": "MissionCompletedSpreadTheWord"` — so the action genuinely *is* configured in Pega, contradicting the "just missing from this one response" theory. Checked that entry's actual `Label` field (the value that becomes `offer.title` in this app, via `mapPegaOffer`) and found the real mismatch: Pega's configured Label is **"Mission Completed Spread the Word"** — no "for" — while this app's own catalog title was **"Mission Completed for Spread the Word"**. One word, and the exact-normalized-title matching every part of this campaign relies on (`filterOffersForStage`, `getDiegoContainerOfferByTitle`, `DIEGO_ENGAGE_TITLES_BY_MISSION`, etc.) had no tolerance for the difference.

**Fixed by renaming the app's side to match Pega's real Label exactly** — "Mission Completed for Spread the Word" → "Mission Completed Spread the Word" — rather than loosening the matching logic (which risked false-positive matches elsewhere) or reinstating a fallback (which the user had just explicitly asked to remove in §52). Updated everywhere the literal string appeared: the `OFFER_CATALOG` entry's `title` (`js/data.js`), `PRIORITY_BANKING_JOURNEY`'s Earn-stage `actions` array (`js/data.js`), the `MISSION_COMPLETED_SPREAD` constant and two further literal uses inside `clearStaleMissionCompletedResolve`/`getDiegoNewlyCompletedMissionOffers` (`js/cdh.js`), the `missionCompletedTitles` array inside the Engage-stage advancement rule (`js/session.js`), and the doc comment above `applyPriorityBankingCampaignAdjustments` that names all the per-mission title pairs. The `mission-completed-spread-word` catalog `id` was left unchanged (ids aren't matched against Pega at all, only titles are).

**Verified live, twice**: first confirmed the fix against a fresh Diego login — checked all 15 (well, all 17 including Fulfil's two) titles against the newly-cached container response and got zero missing. Then re-ran the exact original failing scenario end to end (register both missions, complete Bank More's two Engage actions, accept Refer Your Buddy via Available Milestones) and confirmed "Mission Completed Spread the Word" now renders correctly in Personalized Offers alongside "Mission Completed", both Track Your Progress cards at 100%, no console errors. §52's stated risk (a title silently missing from the one-time response, staying unavailable all session) remains real and by design for any *genuinely* unconfigured title — this just wasn't one of those; it was a naming bug, now fixed at the source.

**A follow-up question surfaced no bug**: asked whether the Earn-stage filter logic restricts rendering to only one Mission Completed card at a time. It doesn't — `bankMoreComplete` and `spreadWordComplete` are checked independently, so both cards can render together if both missions happen to be complete in the same fetch. Asked to verify this live specifically because it "didn't seem to be happening" — re-ran the completion sequence in **both directions** (Bank More with Us finished first then Spread the Word; and the reverse) fresh against the current code, and both cards correctly appeared together in Personalized Offers each time, no console errors either way. No fix was needed here; the behavior was already correct, most likely a stale browser tab from before §52/§53 landed.

---

## 54. Track Your Progress Now Hides Once Its Mission Is Complete

Direct request: once a mission is complete, stop showing that mission's "Track Your Progress" card (previously it stayed visible, frozen at 100%, alongside the newly-revealed "Mission Completed" card). Small, surgical change to `applyPriorityBankingCampaignAdjustments`'s Earn-stage branch (`js/cdh.js`): the visibility filter for `TRACK_PROGRESS`/`TRACK_PROGRESS_SPREAD` now requires `!bankMoreComplete`/`!spreadWordComplete` in addition to the existing "mission registered" check, and the corresponding `autoResolve` calls fire once the mission completes too (not just when it was never registered), so each Track Your Progress card ends up with a definitive `"dismissed"` in `journeyActionResponses` once it steps aside rather than lingering `undefined` forever. Confirmed this is safe: Earn stage's own advancement rule (§50) only cares whether a Mission Completed card gets accepted, never whether Track Your Progress has a response at all, so nothing depends on that jar entry either way.

**Verified live**: ran five scenarios directly against the function — Bank More partial progress (only Track Your Progress shows), Bank More complete (only Mission Completed shows, Track Your Progress correctly auto-resolved and gone), Spread the Word registered-but-incomplete (only its Track Your Progress shows), Spread the Word complete (only its Mission Completed shows), and both missions complete (only the two Mission Completed cards show, neither Track Your Progress card present). All five matched exactly.
