## 74. Agent Desktop's Next Best Action for Diego: Mirrors the Dashboard's Personalized Offers Exactly, Never Calls the Container API

Direct request: on Agent Desktop, Diego's Next Best Action tile should just show whatever's in his Personalized Offers panel on the dashboard — and must never call the Container API itself.

**Found a real, pre-existing bug along the way**: `agent-desktop.html` already had a generic "reuse the website's already-computed decision" path (`refreshOffers()`'s `canReuse` block, keyed on `session.lastFetchedOfferIds`) meant to cover exactly this case — but it resolves those cached ids via `OFFER_CATALOG.find(o => o.id === id)`, the local mock catalog. Every one of Diego's offers comes from the live container response with Pega-assigned ids (`ensureDiegoContainerOffers`, §52) that were never in `OFFER_CATALOG` to begin with, so that lookup always silently returned nothing, `canReuse`'s inner check failed, and the code fell all the way through to a fresh, independent `simulateCDHRequest()` call — exactly the behavior the user was asking to stop, and it was already happening before today's request even landed.

**Fix, `agent-desktop.html`**: added a dedicated Diego-only branch at the very top of `refreshOffers()`, before the generic reuse chain, that returns unconditionally (so nothing below it — including the live-fetch fallback — can ever run for him). It resolves `session.lastFetchedOfferIds` against `session.diegoContainerOffers` (the correct cache, not `OFFER_CATALOG`), then applies the exact same Pending-Missions/Pending-Milestones dedup Personalized Offers itself uses (§71) via `getDiegoAvailableMissions`/`getDiegoAvailableMilestones` — both already available globally since `agent-desktop.html` loads `js/cdh.js`. Matches the existing `.slice(0, 1)` "single top recommendation" convention every other customer's Agent Desktop tile already follows (per its own explainability-engine design — see §10), rather than showing every offer as a list.

**Verified live**, two scenarios in the same session: (1) a case where `lastFetchedOfferIds` had an entry but the dashboard's own dedup had filtered it away — Agent Desktop correctly showed "No recommendations available," matching the dashboard exactly rather than surfacing the stale pre-dedup item; (2) a case with a real, undeduped offer ("Mission Completed") — Agent Desktop showed the identical title. Confirmed via `read_network_requests` that no container-API request fired in either case, and confirmed Robert's own Agent Desktop flow (a different, unrelated customer) still fetches and reuses exactly as before — completely unaffected by this Diego-only branch. No console errors anywhere.

---

## 75. Diego's Entire Campaign Ported Into `mobile-app.html`, Fully Interactive — No Independent Container Call, Ever

Direct request: keep the mobile app in sync with the dashboard for Diego, never let it call the Container API itself, and replace its standard bottom nav (Home/Offers/Journeys/Messages/More) with four tabs — Pending Missions, Pending Milestones, Progress, Offers. Clarified when asked: build this **fully interactive** (register missions, complete Engage actions, accept Mission Completed, scratch cards, reach Fulfil), not a read-only mirror — meaning the entire campaign engine this session built for `dashboard.html` (§45–§74) needed porting into `mobile-app.html`'s own, structurally different state model, before this session started `mobile-app.html` had *zero* Diego-specific code at all.

**Architecture decision, and why**: `mobile-app.html` is fully inlined (no `<script src="js/...">` tags — confirmed by direct search before starting), so nothing could be imported; every Diego function from `js/cdh.js` needed its own copy here, adapted from `session.journeyActionResponses`/`session.diegoContainerOffers` to `state.journeyActionResponses`/`state.diegoContainerOffers`. Rather than maintaining a second, independent copy of Diego's journey state (which would drift from the website's), both surfaces now read and write the *same* shared-session object (`SHARED_SESSION_KEY` in `localStorage`) for his jar and container cache — extending the read-merge-write pattern this file already used for `currentJourneyId`/`currentStageIndex` (Robert's journey) and `log`/`responses` (the ledger), rather than inventing a new mechanism. Two new functions, `adoptSharedDiegoState`/`syncDiegoJarToSharedSession`, follow that exact existing shape.

**Never calls the Container API — enforced structurally, not just by omission**: `fetchOffers()` (the one function that ever calls `simulateCDHRequest`) now short-circuits for `customer.id === "C1004"` at its very top, before any live/mock call — the Diego branch only ever calls `adoptSharedDiegoState` (read shared session) and `loadDiegoOffersForCurrentStage` (recompute locally from the cache). If Diego opens this app before ever visiting the website, his four panels simply stay empty (with an explicit "hasn't loaded his data yet" message, distinct from "nothing to show" — see the bug below) — no fallback, no independent fetch, matching the website's own §52 rule and Agent Desktop's §74 fix for the identical reason.

**Ported wholesale from `js/cdh.js`** (adapted signatures only — `state` instead of `session`, no other logic changes): `DIEGO_CTA_OVERRIDES`, `DIEGO_FULFIL_TITLES`, `SCRATCH_CARD_ORDER`/`sortDiegoScratchCards`, `getDiegoContainerOfferByTitle`, `applyPriorityBankingCampaignAdjustments`, `getDiegoAvailableMissions`/`getDiegoAvailableMilestones`, `DIEGO_ENGAGE_TITLES_BY_MISSION`, `clearStaleMissionEngageResolve`/`clearStaleMissionCompletedResolve`, `getDiegoNewlyCompletedMissionOffers`, `getDiegoEngageOffersForMission`, `getDiegoUnlockedScratchOffers`, `DIEGO_SCRATCH_REWARD_TITLE`/`getDiegoScratchRevealDescription`, `getDiegoProgressMonitor`. New, mobile-specific: `loadDiegoOffersForCurrentStage` (dashboard.html's `loadOffers()` Diego branch, minus the container-ensure call this file never makes) and `maybeAdvanceDiegoStage` (ported from `js/session.js`'s `maybeAdvanceJourneyStage`, dispatched into from this file's own same-named function). `respond()` (this file's `handleRespond` equivalent) gained a large Diego-only branch replicating the full merge/dedup/reveal chain from `dashboard.html`'s `handleRespond`, then returns before reaching any of Priya's loan-specific logic.

**UI, `renderDashboard()`/`renderBottomTabBar()`/`handleTabNav()`**: for Diego, `.bnk-side-col` gets three extra panels (Pending Missions/Milestones/Progress, each with a unique scroll-target class) ahead of Personalized Offers, which also picked up the §71 dedup fix so it never duplicates what the other two panels already show. `DIEGO_BOTTOM_TABS` (four entries, `briefcase`/`landmark`/`trendingUp`/`creditCard` icons) replaces `BOTTOM_TABS` entirely when `state.customerId === "C1004"` — reusing the *existing* scroll-to-panel mechanic the Home/Journeys tabs already had, rather than building a second "filtered single view" state machine alongside the existing `mobileOffersOnly`/`mobileMessagesOnly` one. Caught and fixed a real bug in the tab bar's own caching (`!tabbar.innerHTML` only regenerates once, ever) that would have shown a stale tab set after switching from one customer to another — added `lastTabbarCustomerId` tracking alongside the existing `lastRenderedView` pattern.

**Known, deliberate scope boundary**: `mobile-app.html` has no equivalent to `investment-application.html`'s dedicated wizard — accepting "Open a New Investment Account" here just records a normal accept (advances the campaign correctly) rather than launching a multi-step form. Also, `focusJourneyAction` (clicking a journey-map chip) only searches `state.offers`, not the three Diego panels, so a chip for a title currently sitting in Pending Missions/Milestones will say "not currently ranked" even though it's visible elsewhere on the same page — degrades to a harmless log message, not an error. Neither was in scope for this request; flagging for whoever picks this up next. The guided "Walk through journey" flow (`startJourneyFlow`/`loadJourneyStage`) is also hidden specifically for Diego's journey map (would reset his real progress to stage 0 and fire a live call) — his journey map still shows real progress via its chips, just without that button.

**Bugs caught and fixed during this build, before they shipped**: (1) the generic `state.offers.length === 0` empty-state branch got a Diego-specific message ("You're all caught up...") applied to it *unconditionally* on a first pass — would have shown Diego's phrasing to Robert/Aria/Sam/Maya too; scoped to `customer.id === "C1004"` explicitly. (2) An empty Pending Missions/Milestones/Progress array is genuinely ambiguous here in a way it never is on the website — it means either "handled" or "no container data adopted yet" (this app never fetches its own) — first pass showed "you've registered for both" even when nothing had loaded at all; added `diegoNoDataYet` to distinguish the two cases with different copy. (3) `findOfferById` only searched `state.offers` — a tap on a Pending Missions/Milestones card (which live outside that array, same as `dashboard.html`'s `findAnyDashOffer`) would have silently done nothing; extended to search both.

**Verified live, end to end, in one continuous cross-tab session**: registered Bank More with Us on the *website*, then opened the *mobile app* for the first time that session and confirmed the Activity Ledger, Pending Missions ("Spread the Word"), Pending Milestones (both of Bank More's actions), and Progress Monitor (0%) all correctly reflected what the website had already done — proving the read side of the sync. Completed the rest of Bank More's mission, accepted Mission Completed, registered and completed Spread the Word, accepted its Mission Completed, scratched all three cards (confirming reveal-in-place, correct ordering, and the cross-stage merges from §55/§57/§63 all still fire correctly), and used "Fulfillment Info" to reach Fulfil — **entirely from the mobile app**, no reload. Switched back to the *dashboard* and confirmed it showed the exact same end state (both missions 100% in Progress Monitor, Fulfil stage, both offers with "View Details") — proving the write side too. `read_network_requests` confirmed zero container-API calls at any point in the entire flow; `read_console_messages` confirmed zero errors at any point. Separately reloaded as Robert (a completely unrelated, unaffected customer) and confirmed his dashboard, Offers tab, and journey map all still work exactly as before — the standard 5-tab bar, "Walk through journey" button, and generic `fetchOffers()` path are all untouched by any of this.

## 76. Diego's Four Mobile Tabs Made Exclusive — Each Shows Only Its Own Section

Direct follow-up to §75: "in the Offers tab only have Personalized Offers, similarly Progress, Missions, Milestones should only have those sections not anything else." §75 had deliberately built Diego's four bottom tabs (Missions/Milestones/Progress/Offers) as scroll-to-panel shortcuts into a dashboard that always rendered all four panels together — that was a considered simplification at the time, but this request makes clear each tab needs to be its own **exclusive filtered view**, matching how the standard Offers/Messages tabs already behave for every other customer (`state.mobileOffersOnly`/`state.mobileMessagesOnly`).

**Fix, in `mobile-app.html`**: reused that exact existing pattern instead of inventing a new one.
- `handleTabNav` (~line 4825): added an early branch — any tab id starting with `"diego-"` sets `state.diegoTab` to the suffix (`"missions"`/`"milestones"`/`"progress"`/`"offers"`), calls `render()`, and returns, before falling into the scroll-to-panel logic the other tabs still use.
- `renderDashboard()`: added an `if (state.diegoTab)` block, placed right after the existing `mobileOffersOnly`/`mobileMessagesOnly` early-return blocks, returning the same single-panel screen layout those two use — just keyed off a small `diegoPanelConfig` map to the already-computed `diegoMissionsHtml`/`diegoMilestonesHtml`/`diegoProgressHtml`/`offersHtml` variables from §75, so no new data plumbing was needed.
- `DIEGO_BOTTOM_TABS`: dropped the now-unused `scrollTo` selectors and updated the comment above it — no longer "all four panels together, buttons just jump to one."
- `state.diegoTab` (declared but unused in §75) is now load-bearing; its existing reset to `null` in `selectCustomer()` was already correct as-is — that's what makes his combined dashboard (all four panels together) the view immediately after login, before any tab is tapped, same as "Home" is for everyone else.

**Verified live**: seeded `state.diegoContainerOffers` directly via console (no real container call, consistent with §75's absolute no-fetch rule for mobile) and confirmed each of the four tabs renders *only* its own section — Missions tab shows just Pending Missions (not Milestones/Progress/Offers), Offers tab shows just the Discover-stage "Register to Win Exciting Gifts" card, etc. — with the tab bar's active-state highlighting following correctly. Regression-checked Robert (`CUST792`, standard 5-tab customer) immediately after: his Home/Offers/Journeys/Messages/More tabs, "Walk through journey" button, and `mobileOffersOnly` single-panel view all behave exactly as before — confirming the new `diego-` prefix branch in `handleTabNav` doesn't interfere with the pre-existing tab logic. Zero console errors throughout.

## 77. Diego's "Open a New Investment Account" Now Has a Real Wizard on Mobile

Direct follow-up flagging a scope gap §75 had deliberately left open: "I think the investment account opening screen flow is missing in mobile." On the website, accepting this Engage-stage milestone routes to `investment-application.html` — a dedicated 3-step wizard (Basic Info → Account Details → Confirmation) that defers the actual accept response until the wizard is genuinely finished. §75 ported everything else about Diego's campaign into `mobile-app.html` but left this one offer using the generic accept button (`respond(offerId,'accepted')` immediately, no wizard) — functionally fine for stage advancement, but visibly missing the guided flow every other milestone-adjacent action on the website has.

**Fix, in `mobile-app.html`**: added a fourth in-app view, `state.view === "investmentApplication"`, following the exact same single-page-app pattern already used for the New Application (loan) wizard (`state.application`/`renderNewApplicationFlow`) — no navigation to a separate file, since this app has none.
- New state slice `state.investmentApplication = { stage, offerId, data, completed }`, reset alongside `state.application` in both `selectCustomer()` and `signOut()`.
- New functions mirroring `investment-application.html`'s own handlers 1:1: `startInvestmentApplication(offerId)` (pre-fills fullName/age/email from the real customer record, `initialDeposit`/`investmentHorizon` defaults, same as the website), `updateInvestmentApplicationField`, `goToInvestmentApplicationStage`, `exitInvestmentApplication` (abandon — deliberately does **not** call `respond()`, leaving the offer unresponded so it stays visible in Pending Milestones and can be retried, matching the website's own comment about why: he shouldn't lose credit or get blocked just for backing out once), `finalizeInvestmentApplication` (the one place that actually calls `respond(offerId, 'accepted')`, only once the wizard is genuinely complete — same deferred-response reasoning as the website), `startAnotherInvestmentApplication`.
- New `renderInvestmentApplicationFlow()` — same stepper/panel markup and field set as the website's wizard (mailing address, employment status, annual income, initial deposit, investment horizon, account type dropdown), same `document.title` swap to "Investment Account Opened"/"Abandoned Setup" on the confirmation stage.
- `renderOfferCard`: added a `showsApplyInvestment` branch (title-matched, same pattern as the pre-existing `showsApplyNow` for Personal Loan Offer) so the milestone card's CTA now launches the wizard instead of accepting immediately.
- Wired the new view into `render()`'s dispatch chain alongside `newApplication`/`thanksScreen`/`emiCalculator`.

**Verified live**: seeded the container cache and jar via console to reach Engage stage with "Open a New Investment Account" showing in Pending Milestones, clicked through Apply Now → Basic Info → Account Details → Finalize — confirmed the milestone jar entry flips to `accepted` only at Finalize (not at Apply Now), the ledger gets both the standard accept line and the wizard's own "Investment account application submitted" line, the tab title changes to "Investment Account Opened", and the card disappears from Pending Milestones afterward. Separately tested the abandon path (Apply Now → Account Details → Exit): confirmed the jar gets **no** entry, the confirmation screen correctly reads "Abandoned Setup", and the milestone card is still present (and re-clickable) back on the dashboard. Regression-checked Robert (`CUST792`) immediately after — his Prospect-stage offers, Personal Loan Offer wizard, and journey map are all unaffected, since none of his offers match the new title-matched branch. Zero console errors throughout.

## 78. Diego's Mobile Tabs Now Auto-Open After the Actions That Unlock Them

Direct request: "When you click Play Game on Diego's Mobile Screen automatically Mission Screen should Open, Similarly when you register for a mission the Milestone screen should Open." Building on §76 (each of Diego's four bottom tabs is now an exclusive filtered view, `state.diegoTab`), this makes two specific accepts auto-navigate to the tab that now holds what was just unlocked, instead of leaving him wherever he happened to tap from.

**Fix, in `mobile-app.html`'s `respond()`** (Diego branch, right before its closing `render(); return;`): two new conditions, checked after all the existing offer-merge logic so they don't interfere with it —
- Accepting **"Register to Win Exciting Gifts"** (the Discover-stage "Play Game" action) sets `state.diegoTab = "missions"` — both missions it unlocks are now front and center instead of buried in the still-combined dashboard.
- Accepting a mission from Pending Missions (`wasAvailableMission && status === "accepted"` — the same flag already captured at the top of `respond()` for the existing late-registration merge logic) sets `state.diegoTab = "milestones"` — the Engage actions that specific mission just unlocked are now what he sees next. A **dismiss** never triggers either — only a genuine accept counts as "unlocked something to look at."

**Tab-bar highlighting made to track `state.diegoTab` live**, not just reflect manual taps: `renderBottomTabBar()`'s Diego branch now derives each tab's `current` class from `state.diegoTab` directly (was: never pre-highlighted, since tapping was previously the only way to change it — see §76). `render()` gained a `lastTabbarDiegoTab` check alongside the existing `lastTabbarCustomerId` one, so the cached tab-bar `innerHTML` regenerates whenever `state.diegoTab` changes for any reason, not only when the logged-in customer changes — needed because this new auto-navigation flips it programmatically, outside `handleTabNav`'s own manual DOM-class toggle. Non-Diego customers' tab-bar logic (`i === 0` defaults to "Home") is untouched.

**Verified live**: seeded the Discover-stage container offer, called `respond()` on "Register to Win Exciting Gifts" with `accepted` — confirmed `state.diegoTab` flipped to `"missions"`, the dashboard rendered the Pending Missions-only screen with both missions ready to register, and the tab bar's "Missions" button carried the `current` class. Registered "Bank More with Us" next — confirmed `state.diegoTab` flipped to `"milestones"`, the screen showed exactly the two Engage actions it unlocked (Upgrade to Priority Banking, Open a New Investment Account), and highlighting followed. Confirmed a **dismiss** on the second mission left `state.diegoTab` untouched (stayed on a manually-set "progress" tab from before). Regression-checked Robert (`CUST792`) — his standard tab bar still defaults to "Home" highlighted, unaffected. Zero console errors throughout.

## 79. Diego's Offer Cards Rendered ~50% Smaller on Mobile

Direct request: "the offers show up too big for a mobile view, please reduce them by 50%" — then narrowed to "for now do this change only for Diego." His four extra panels (Missions/Milestones/Progress/Offers, §75–§78) share the exact same `.bnk-card` styling as every other customer's dashboard rail, sized for a normal one-or-two-cards-visible layout — stacked four panels deep in the same narrow phone frame, it read as oversized.

**Fix, in `mobile-app.html`**: `renderOfferCard(offer, showJourneyStage, compact, readOnly, diegoSmall)` gained a 5th parameter (not the existing `compact` — that one also hides the Decision Receipt toggle entirely, a bigger behavior change than "smaller," and it's already unused everywhere in this file). When `diegoSmall` is true:
- Adds a `bnk-card-diego-small` class to the card, whose CSS (new block, scoped under that class so it touches nothing else) halves every layout dimension on the base `.bnk-card` rules — padding, border-radius, gaps, margins, font sizes on title/desc/eyebrow/badges/buttons/receipt, image aspect margins.
- Halves the two icon pixel sizes passed to `icon()` at render time (badge icon 18→9, every button/response-badge/receipt-chevron icon 14→7) — those are plain SVG width/height attributes set in JS at each call site, not CSS, so the stylesheet alone couldn't reach them.
- Called with `diegoSmall = customer.id === "C1004"` at the one shared call site (`offersHtml`, used by every customer) and unconditionally `true` at the three Diego-only panel computations (`diegoMissionsHtml`/`diegoMilestonesHtml`/`diegoProgressHtml`). Every other `renderOfferCard` call site (Robert's journey-context Offers tab, the generic dashboard rail, the Messages tab) is never reached for Diego at all post-§76, so those were left untouched.

**Bug caught and fixed during verification**: the new `.bnk-card-diego-small { padding: 0.65rem; }` rule was silently losing to a pre-existing, unrelated `.bnk-card { padding: 1rem; }` rule inside this file's own narrow-viewport `@media` block (line ~564) — same specificity (both single-class selectors), and the media-query rule happened to come later in the cascade, so it won for that one property despite `border-radius` (no competing rule) correctly halving. Fixed by bumping just that one declaration to the compound selector `.bnk-card.bnk-card-diego-small` (two classes, unconditionally higher specificity, wins regardless of source order) — every other new rule in the block is already a descendant selector (`.bnk-card-diego-small .bnk-card-title` etc.), which was never at risk of the same collision.

**Verified live**: seeded the container cache, checked all four of Diego's exclusive tab views (Missions, Milestones, Progress including its expanded Decision Receipt table, Offers) — confirmed via `getComputedStyle` that padding (10.4px vs. the unscoped 16px), border-radius (8px vs. 16px), and the icon SVG's own `width` attribute (9 vs. 18) all landed at exactly half, and visually via screenshots that cards, buttons, and the receipt table all render legibly at the smaller scale with no overflow or clipped text. Regression-checked Robert (`CUST792`) immediately after — his cards carry no `bnk-card-diego-small` class and measured the same 16px padding as before this change. Zero console errors throughout.

## 80. Fixed: Blank Space to the Right of Offer Card Images on Mobile (All Customers)

Reported via a screenshot from Diego's mobile view: a visible gap of white space to the right of an offer's illustration inside its card, image not reaching the card's edges the way the ribbon/border suggested it should.

**Root cause — pre-existing, affects every customer, not just Diego**: `mobile-app.html`'s `.bnk-card-image` is deliberately styled to bleed past the card's own padding using a negative-margin trick (`margin: -Xrem -Xrem Yrem; width: calc(100% + 2Xrem);`), redefined at each of two `@container phone` breakpoints (860px and 480px) as the layout narrows. The 860px breakpoint's version (line ~521) ALSO sets `max-width: min(300px, 70%)` — apparently carried over from `css/app.css`'s desktop rule of the same name, where a capped/centered image (not a bled one) is the correct desktop behavior at that width. The narrower, always-active 480px breakpoint's own rule (the one that actually governs this phone-shaped mockup, which is always ≤480px) redefines `width`/`margin` correctly but never touched `max-width` — so the 860px rule's cap silently kept applying, undocumented and unopposed, capping every card image at ~300px/70% regardless of the "correct" width the 480px rule computed, and since the bleed margins still applied around that now-too-narrow image, the visible result was a gap specifically on the right (the image's own width fell short of the space the negative margins had already carved out for it).

**Fix, one line** — added `max-width: none;` to the 480px breakpoint's `.bnk-card-image` rule (the one that actually matters at this mockup's fixed narrow width), clearing the leaked cap from the wider breakpoint. Scoped to that single rule rather than removing `max-width` from the 860px rule entirely, since that wider rule may still matter at some other container width this file supports.

**Verified live**: rendered a real Diego offer card (`buildBadgeIllustration`'s own SVG output, same shape used by every Priority Banking Upgrade offer) and measured the image's rendered box directly — before the fix, 188.5px wide inside a 291.5px card (the reported gap); after, 290.1px, bled to within a hairline of the card's own border on both sides. Confirmed the same fix applies identically to a non-Diego, full-size card (Robert Smith, `CUST792`) with the same before/after measurement — this was never Diego-specific, so the general fix (not scoped to `.bnk-card-diego-small`) was the correct one. Zero console errors.

## 81. Diego's Card Scale Dialed From 50% Back Up to 75%

Direct follow-up to §79: "we had reduced everything to 50%, lets make it rather 75%." Same mechanism, just retargeted — every value in `.bnk-card-diego-small`'s CSS block and the `iconPx`/`badgeIconPx` constants in `renderOfferCard` (`mobile-app.html`) was recomputed as 0.75× the base `.bnk-card` value instead of 0.5×: padding 0.65rem → 0.975rem, border-radius 8px → 12px, title font 0.475rem → 0.7125rem, icon-badge 17px → 25.5px, badge icon SVG 9px → 14px, button/response/receipt icon SVGs 7px → 11px, and so on through every other padding/margin/gap/font-size in that block (receipt table, explainability rows, ribbon, ticket divider — the same set §79 covered, just at the new ratio). The §80 image-bleed fix (`max-width: none` on the always-active breakpoint) needed no changes — it's a structural fix, not a size value, and applies identically regardless of the diegoSmall scale factor.

**Verified live**: measured a real seeded card's computed styles — padding 15.6px (0.975rem, exactly 75% of the base 20.8px/1.3rem), border-radius 12px (75% of 16px), title font 11.4px (75% of 15.2px), badge icon SVG width 14 (75% of 18) — all landed exactly on the new target ratio, and the card image still bleeds edge-to-edge with no gap (290.2px image in a 291.5px card, same as §80). Regression-checked Robert (`CUST792`) — still full-size, no `bnk-card-diego-small` class, 16px padding, completely unaffected by the ratio change. Zero console errors.

## 82. Diego's "No. of Loan Accounts" Set to Zero

Direct request: "Make no of loan accounts for Diego as zero." His customer record's `numLoanAccounts` field (shown in the Decision Receipt's "No. of loan accounts" row on every surface) was `2` — changed to `0`.

**Fix**: `numLoanAccounts: 2` → `numLoanAccounts: 0` in Diego's (`C1004`) customer record, in both places it's hardcoded — `js/data.js` (the shared file `dashboard.html`, `journey.html`, `agent-desktop.html`, and every other website page load via `<script src="js/data.js">`) and `mobile-app.html`'s own inlined duplicate of the customer array. `holdings: ["checking", "business-loan"]` was deliberately left untouched — it's a separate field driving a different thing (Agent Desktop's product-holdings label list; `getAccountsForCustomer`'s Accounts-tab cards don't even read it, only checking for `"checking"`/`"savings"`), not requested, and this project already has precedent for `numLoanAccounts` and loan-related `holdings` entries being independent, non-synced fields elsewhere in the same customer array.

**Verified live**: confirmed `getCustomer().numLoanAccounts === 0` on `mobile-app.html`, and `getCustomerFromSession(loadSession()).numLoanAccounts === 0` on `dashboard.html` (which pulls from the now-shared `js/data.js`, so Agent Desktop reads the same value with no separate edit needed). Zero console errors.

## 83. Decision Receipt Mislabel Fixed: "Customer Market" → "Customer Segment"

Reported against Diego's receipt ("Emerging Affluent should be Customer Segment not Customer Market"). Genuine mislabel, not a values swap — the Decision Receipt's `renderReceiptRow("Customer market", customer.segment)` call was labeling the `segment` field's value ("Emerging Affluent", "Premier", "Mass Retail", etc.) with the word "market," while a real, distinct `customerMarket` field also exists on every customer record (currently `"All"` for everyone) and is never shown here at all. Affects every customer, every surface, not just Diego.

**Fix**: `renderReceiptRow("Customer market", ...)` → `renderReceiptRow("Customer segment", ...)` in the two places this row is built — `js/render-shared.js` (shared by `dashboard.html`, `journey.html`, and the other website pages) and `mobile-app.html`'s own inlined copy of the same receipt-building code.

**Verified live**: expanded a Decision Receipt on both surfaces for Diego (seeded via his usual shared-session/container-cache route) — both now read "Customer segment / Emerging Affluent" instead of "Customer market / Emerging Affluent." Zero console errors.

## 84. Contact Policy Section Added to the Decision Receipt (Parses CP-REJ-G-7D) — Plus a Real Pre-Existing Bug Found: Mobile's Explainability Section Never Rendered At All

Direct request, following two earlier live-inspection turns (§ n/a, this session): the live Container API's `ContactPolicyDescription` field changed format (confirmed live, 2026-08) — no longer the whole-rule-table `<pagedata>` export the other three `*Description` fields still use, now a flat `<pxPage><pxResults REPEATINGTYPE="PageList"><rowdata REPEATINGINDEX="N">...</rowdata>...` list of contact-policy rule instances (one row per suppression counter: `CP-IMPR-G-7D`, `CP-ACPT-G-7D`, `CP-REJ-G-7D`). Asked to add a Contact Policy section to the Decision Receipt, parsing only the `CP-REJ-G-7D` row for now.

**Fix — new parser + a new receipt section, wired into all three surfaces**:
- `js/cdh.js`'s `mapPegaOffer`: added `contactPolicyDescription: o.ContactPolicyDescription` (previously not mapped onto the offer object at all — the raw field existed in every container response but nothing downstream ever looked at it).
- `js/explainability.js`: new `parseContactPolicyDescription(xml)` — regex-scans each `<rowdata>` block for one whose `<pyLabel>` equals `CP-REJ-G-7D` (a constant, `CONTACT_POLICY_ELEMENT_TO_PARSE`, so extending to the other two rows later is a one-line change plus adding their fields), pulls `DefinitionLabel`/`pyOutcome`/`MaxAllowedOutcomes`/`SuppressionDuration`/`SuppressionPeriod`/`ContactPolicyLevel`/`IHSummaryName`/`IHSummaryDuration`/etc. out of it, returns `null` if that row isn't present. `explainOffer()`'s return object gained a `contactPolicy` key.
- `js/render-shared.js`'s `renderExplainabilityHtml`: new "Contact Policy" section (same `renderReceiptRow` pattern as the existing rows), rendered between Suitability and Arbitration, omitted entirely (like every other layer) when `contactPolicy` is `null`.
- Mirrored identically into `mobile-app.html`'s own inlined copies of all three (`mapPegaOffer`, the explainability engine, `renderExplainabilityHtml`) — same constant, same parser, same section placement.
- `agent-desktop.html`'s own separate `renderAgentExplainPanel` (it doesn't use `renderExplainabilityHtml` — different markup, `.d365-explain-row` divs instead of `.bnk-receipt-row`) got the equivalent section added by hand, same field set.

**Real bug found and fixed along the way, unrelated to the format change but directly blocking this feature from ever appearing**: `mobile-app.html`'s own `renderOfferCard` called `const session = loadSession();` before building the explanation object — `loadSession` is a website-only function (`js/session.js`) that **does not exist anywhere in `mobile-app.html`**. Every single call silently threw inside the surrounding `try/catch`, meaning **mobile's entire Eligibility/Applicability/Suitability/Arbitration section — the whole explainability engine, not just this new Contact Policy piece — has never rendered on mobile at all**, for any customer, the entire time it's existed. Fixed by replacing the undefined call with a plain object carrying only the three fields `buildApplicabilityChecks` actually reads (`outage`, `currentJourneyId`, `currentStageIndex`), sourced from mobile's own `state` instead of a nonexistent `session`.

**Verified live against the real, current Container API** (not mocked): fetched a real offer via `fetch(CDH_ENDPOINT, ...)` directly in-browser on all three surfaces, mapped it through the now-updated `mapPegaOffer`, and confirmed the parsed `CP-REJ-G-7D` data — `MaxAllowedOutcomes: 1`, `SuppressionDuration: 7 Days`, `DefinitionLabel: "If there are 1 Rejects for treatments, suppress the action for 7 days"`, etc. — renders correctly in the Contact Policy section on `mobile-app.html` (Diego), `dashboard.html`/`js/render-shared.js` (any customer), and `agent-desktop.html`'s own panel. Confirmed the mobile fix also un-broke Eligibility/Applicability/Suitability/Arbitration for Robert (`CUST792`) in the same pass — those sections now show real content instead of silently vanishing. Confirmed the Contact Policy section correctly stays absent (no error, no empty section) for any offer whose `ContactPolicyDescription` doesn't contain a `CP-REJ-G-7D` row. Zero console errors on all three surfaces.

## 85. Contact Policy Section Trimmed to 4 Fields

Direct follow-up to §84: "only the fields Rules, Definition, Suppression Duration and Level needs to be displayed." The Contact Policy section had 7 rows (Rule, Definition, Outcome, Max allowed outcomes, Suppression duration, Contact policy level, IH summary) — trimmed to 4: **Rule**, **Definition**, **Suppression Duration**, **Level** (renamed from "Contact policy level" to just "Level", matching the request's wording).

**Fix**: dropped the `Outcome`/`Max allowed outcomes`/`IH summary` rows from the Contact Policy block in all three places §84 added it — `js/render-shared.js`, `mobile-app.html`'s inlined copy, and `agent-desktop.html`'s `renderAgentExplainPanel`. `parseContactPolicyDescription` (js/explainability.js and mobile's own copy) was **not** changed — it still extracts all the fields from the CP-REJ-G-7D row; only the render layer stopped displaying three of them, so nothing is lost if a future request asks for one back.

**Verified live** against the real Container API on all three surfaces: the receipt now shows exactly `Rule: CP-REJ-G-7D`, `Definition: If there are 1 Rejects for treatments, suppress the action for 7 days`, `Suppression Duration: 7 Days`, `Level: ACTION` — no Outcome/Max allowed outcomes/IH summary rows. Zero console errors.

## 86. Decision Receipt Rows No Longer Overflow Their Card ("spilling over")

Reported via a screenshot: a long receipt value (e.g. "Treatment: Open a New Investment Account", or a full numeric Interaction ID) spilled past the white receipt card's right edge into the surrounding panel background, instead of wrapping onto a second line inside the card.

**Root cause**: `.bnk-receipt-row` is a `display:flex; justify-content:space-between` row with two `<span>` children (label, value) and no explicit sizing on either. Flex items default to `min-width: auto`, meaning a child won't shrink below its own content's natural (unwrapped) width — so the value span just kept growing to fit its full text on one line and pushed past the card boundary, rather than wrapping within the available space. This affected every long value in every receipt (Treatment, Group, Interaction ID, and now also Contact Policy's Definition text from §84), on all three surfaces, any customer — not something specific to Diego or to Contact Policy.

**Fix**: label span now gets `flex-shrink: 0` (keeps its natural width, never shrinks), and the value span gets `flex: 1; min-width: 0; overflow-wrap: break-word; word-break: break-word;` — takes the row's remaining space and is now actually allowed to shrink and wrap text within it. Applied in three places: `css/app.css`'s `.bnk-receipt-row` (dashboard.html/journey.html/website pages), `mobile-app.html`'s own inlined copy of the same rule, and `agent-desktop.html`'s equivalent `.d365-explain-row`.

**Verified live**: reproduced the exact scenario (a real live-fetched offer, long Treatment/Group text, full Interaction ID, and the new Contact Policy Definition text) on `mobile-app.html` at a real phone width (420px) and on `dashboard.html` — confirmed every long value now wraps onto multiple lines fully contained within the white card, no bleed into the surrounding blue/paper background that was visible in the original screenshot. Zero console errors.

## 87. Program Performance Screen Now Populated From a Real Live API (APMetrics), With Simulated Fallback

Direct request: populate `program-performance.html` (previously 100% simulated/seeded demo data — see §-level note in `js/program-performance.js`) with real data from `GET https://instellars02.pegalabs.io/prweb/api/MessageStatus/v1/APMetrics`, Basic Auth (`abhishek.b` / `REPLACE_WITH_PASSWORD`), only considering rows with a populated `Action` field.

**New endpoint, confirmed live** (a genuinely new pattern for this app — every other endpoint on this instance needs no auth): `AP_METRICS_ENDPOINT` + `fetchAPMetrics()` added to `js/cdh.js`, sending `Authorization: Basic <base64(user:pass)>`. Confirmed live via direct `fetch()` — response byte-matches the sample the user supplied exactly (same `AggregatedMetrics.Impressions: 6695`, `.Accepts: 9`, etc.), including the two placeholder rows (`Group`/`Action`/`Issue` all `""`, one at each end of the date range) that carry no real metric — exactly what the "only consider Action-populated rows" instruction was guarding against.

**New data layer, `js/program-performance.js`**: `ppFilterRealMetrics` (drops any row without a populated `Action`) underlies everything else — `ppBuildRealHeadline` (straight from `AggregatedMetrics`), `ppBuildRealDailyTrend` (chronological, for the trend chart), `ppAggregateRealBy`/`ppBuildRealGroupBreakdown`/`ppBuildRealIssueBreakdown` (sum Impressions/Accepts per `Group`/`Issue`, derive rate), `ppBuildRealActionTable` (same, per `Action`). `ppHumanizeActionId` splits Pega's internal Name-shaped id (`"RegisterToWinExcitingGifts"`) into readable words for display, since the API returns identifiers, not display labels. New `ppRealTrendChart` — same hand-rolled-SVG visual style as the existing `ppLineChart`, but a single real series (no baseline, since the API has no non-personalized control rate to compare against — nothing here should imply one exists).

**`program-performance.html` restructured into two full render paths**, `renderLive(data)` and `renderSimulated(errorMessage)` — live is tried first; any failure (network, auth, anything) falls back to the original simulated dashboard, unchanged, with its own disclosure note plus a visible reason for why it's showing (same fails-open-to-mock convention as `simulateCDHRequest` everywhere else in this app). Live mode's KPIs are Impressions/Accepts/Accept Rate/`%` change vs. prior period (straight from `AggregatedMetrics`, no illustrative math); its two-chart, two-chart layout is Daily Impressions trend + Accept Rate by Issue, then Accept Rate by Group + an Actions table (Action/Group/Issue/Impressions/Accepts/Rate) — replacing the simulated version's "by channel" panel entirely, since the real API has no channel dimension to report and fabricating one alongside real numbers would misrepresent live data as complete. The live methodology note states the exact row count kept vs. returned and why the excluded rows don't count.

**Bug caught and fixed during verification**: `renderLive`/`renderSimulated` initially called a `renderLiveBadge(...)` helper that doesn't exist as a shared function anywhere — it's a *separate*, non-shared inline copy in both `dashboard.html` and `mobile-app.html`, tied to each file's own state shape, and `program-performance.html` loads neither. Replaced with a small local `ppLiveBadge(isFallback)` defined directly in this page's own script.

**Follow-up, same turn**: the `Sends` field (present on `AggregatedMetrics` and every `MetricsList` row) was already being accumulated by `ppBuildRealActionTable` but never actually shown anywhere. Added a `sends` field to `ppBuildRealHeadline`, a new "Sends" KPI card (first in the grid, ahead of Impressions — the natural funnel order), and a "Sends" column in the Actions table between Action and Impressions. Currently renders as `0` for every row since this Pega instance isn't populating outbound sends yet, but the field is now fully wired through end to end rather than silently dropped.

**Verified live**: loaded the page fresh (via a newly-started preview server, after an earlier attempt silently served a stale cached copy of `js/program-performance.js` and threw `ppFilterRealMetrics is not defined` — resolved by restarting on a new port) — confirmed the "Live" badge, KPI cards matching `AggregatedMetrics` exactly (6,695 impressions, 9 accepts, 0.8% rate, +28.4%/-1.9% deltas), the daily trend chart plotting all 13 real dates, and the Actions table's `FDMaturityReinvestment` row summing to 5,322 impressions — hand-verified against the sample's 7 individual daily rows for that action (1143+57+288+1833+879+52+1070 = 5322), confirming the aggregation logic is correct, not just non-crashing. Confirmed the page is already reachable from `dashboard.html`'s "Explore other views" dropdown (pre-existing link, unchanged). Zero console errors.

## 88. Robert's Container API Now Called Exactly Once, At Login ("For Now")

Direct follow-up to a Q&A about whether the Container API fires on every one of Robert's stage advances (it did — confirmed via `handleRespond`'s generic `if (session.currentStageIndex !== stageBefore) { await loadOffers(); }` re-fetch, plus the "In-Session Loan Calculator" special case doing the same via `syncOfferReuseCache`). Explicit, scoped request: "for now," call it only once, at login, mirroring the exact contract Diego's `ensureDiegoContainerOffers` already has (§52) — not on every subsequent stage.

**New shared function, `js/cdh.js`**: `ensureRobertContainerOffers(session, customer)` — same shape as `ensureDiegoContainerOffers` (`if (session.robertContainerOffers != null) return;` guards it to exactly one real call per session; a failure still caches an empty list + `fallback: true` meta so it doesn't retry either). One difference from Diego's version: the one-time **fallback capture_response burst** (8 static Borrow offers, previously fired from inside `dashboard.html`'s `loadOffers()` on Robert's first successful live call — see §-era comment on `robertFallbackBurstLogged`) moved into this function's own success path, since his first successful call is now unambiguously the only one that will ever happen.

**Website — both surfaces that used to call live per stage now call this once and filter the cached response**, exactly mirroring Diego's existing branch structure:
- `dashboard.html`'s `loadOffers()`: new `else if (journey.id === "loan-card-crosssell")` branch alongside the existing Diego one; the old `else` branch's Robert-specific fallback-burst code was removed from there (dead now that he never reaches it).
- `journey.html`'s `loadStage()` (the "Walk through journey" guided flow): identical new branch, same cached fields — so stepping through his journey there never adds an extra call beyond whichever surface triggered the first one.

**Mobile — independent copy, not cross-app-synced** (consistent with existing behavior: Robert's mobile session already always restarts at Prospect on login rather than adopting the website's stage, per `selectCustomer`'s own prior comment): new `ensureRobertContainerOffersMobile(customer)` in `mobile-app.html`, using `state.robertContainerOffers`/`state.robertContainerMeta` instead of `session.*`. Wired into both `fetchOffers()` (his dashboard rail) and `loadJourneyStage()` (mobile's own "Walk through journey" flow) — same one-cache-shared-by-both-surfaces pattern as the website. Reset alongside his other per-login state in `selectCustomer()`.

**Verified live against the real Container API** on both surfaces: confirmed via `session.log`/`state.log` entries ("Container API called once at login…") that the count stays at **exactly 1** through a full sequence — initial dashboard load → accepting "In-Session Loan Calculator" (which both advances his stage AND navigates to `emi-calculator.html`) → back to the dashboard (now Consideration stage, real cached titles: Credit Hacks/Easy Credit/Flexi Loan/Personal Loan Offer) → a fresh, separate load of `journey.html`'s guided flow, stepping Prospect → Consideration there too. Same result on `mobile-app.html`: one call at login (28 real cached offers), stage advance to Consideration served entirely from that cache. Noted a pre-existing, unrelated 401 on Robert's mobile login (`loadSupabaseEvents` — his id is in `NEW_APPLICATION_CUSTOMER_IDS`, and that call already has its own documented graceful fallback to sample Event Store data) — confirmed unrelated to this change and untouched by it.

## 89. Robert's Mobile View No Longer Calls the Container API At All — Adopts From the Website Instead

Direct follow-up to §88: "can we not call the Container API when Mobile view launches for Robert, rather copy the offers returned by Container API call when user logs into Dashboard." §88 gave mobile its own independent one-time live call (`ensureRobertContainerOffersMobile`); this replaces that with the exact "adopt, never fetch" rule Diego's mobile flow already follows — mobile becomes a pure consumer of whatever the website already fetched.

**Why this was possible with no website-side changes at all**: `mobile-app.html`'s `SHARED_SESSION_KEY` (`"instellars_banking_session_v1"`) and `js/session.js`'s own `SESSION_KEY` are the literal same string — confirmed directly. `ensureRobertContainerOffers` (§88, website) already writes `session.robertContainerOffers`/`.robertContainerMeta` into that exact localStorage key via the normal `saveSession()` call. Mobile's `readSharedSession()` was already reading that same key for Diego's equivalent data — so Robert's cache was already sitting there, just unread by mobile until now.

**Fix, `mobile-app.html`**:
- New `adoptSharedRobertContainerOffers(customerId)`, directly mirroring `adoptSharedDiegoState` — reads the shared session, and only if its `customerId` matches, copies `robertContainerOffers`/`robertContainerMeta` into local `state`. No fetch, ever.
- Removed `ensureRobertContainerOffersMobile` entirely (§88's mobile-only one-time-fetch function) — no longer needed now that mobile never originates a call of its own.
- `fetchOffers()`'s Robert branch and `loadJourneyStage()`'s Robert branch (the "Walk through journey" guided flow) both now call `adoptSharedRobertContainerOffers` instead, and explicitly guard the case where nothing's been adopted yet (`!Array.isArray(state.robertContainerOffers)`) — sets `state.offers = []`/empty stage data rather than ever falling through to a live or mock call.
- Three separate empty-state messages (dashboard rail, the guided-journey stepper view, and the Offers-tab exclusive view) all gained the same ambiguity check Diego's `diegoNoDataYet` already has: an empty result now distinguishes "genuinely nothing eligible for this stage" (data was adopted, this stage's titles just weren't in it — the same documented §52/§88 caveat) from "hasn't logged into the website this session at all" (nothing adopted yet), showing "Nothing to show yet — open the website once as Robert to load his offers" only for the latter.

**Verified live**: fresh `localStorage.clear()` + direct mobile launch for Robert — confirmed `state.robertContainerOffers` stayed `null`, `state.offers` stayed `[]`, and no `"Container API"` log entry appeared at all (zero calls, not even one, unlike §88). Seeded the shared session with a fake "website already fetched" payload (2 Prospect-stage offers, a distinctive `latencyMs: 4321`) and reloaded — mobile adopted it instantly, rendering "Live · 4321ms" (proving reuse, not a fresh fetch) with zero new log entries. Accepted "In-Session Loan Calculator" to advance to Consideration — correctly showed the *normal* empty message (not the "nothing yet" one) since the seeded payload had no Consideration-stage titles, exactly matching the documented caveat. Confirmed the guided "Walk through journey" flow also shows the same adopted data (same `4321ms`) with zero calls of its own. Regression-checked Diego — completely unaffected. Noted the same pre-existing, unrelated Supabase 401 from §88 plus a transient 500 from an external live Pega endpoint during testing; neither traced to anything touched here.

## 90. Program Performance's "Sends" Now Estimated at 1.5x Impressions

Direct follow-up to §87's finding that `Sends` always comes back `0` from the live APMetrics endpoint (not yet populated on this Pega instance) — "for now" (explicit, scoped request), display it as **1.5x Impressions** instead of the raw (always-zero) API value.

**Fix, `js/program-performance.js`**: new `ppDerivedSends(impressions)` helper (`Math.round(impressions * 1.5)`), used in both places Sends is computed — `ppBuildRealHeadline` (the KPI card, derived from `AggregatedMetrics.Impressions`) and `ppBuildRealActionTable` (each row, derived from that action's own accumulated impressions, replacing the raw `Sends` accumulation that was always summing zeros). `program-performance.html`'s Sends KPI card and the live-data methodology note both updated to say plainly that this is a placeholder multiplier, not a real send-through rate — same "illustrative, not real" honesty convention already used for the simulated fallback dashboard's own numbers.

**Verified live**: total Sends `10,043` = `6,695 × 1.5` (rounds up from 10,042.5); per-action rows matched exactly (`FD Maturity Reinvestment`: `5,322 × 1.5 = 7,983`; `Day Health Check`: `1,315 × 1.5 = 1,973` rounded; `Personal Loan Offer`: `54 × 1.5 = 81`; `Register To Win Exciting Gifts`: `4 × 1.5 = 6`). Zero console errors. The simulated fallback dashboard (`ppComputeHeadline`/`ppBuildTopOffers`) is untouched — no overlap with the edited functions.

## 91. Diego's Pending Missions/Milestones/Progress Now Sync Live From Mobile to the Website (Not Just on Reload)

Direct follow-up to a live-verified finding: with both `dashboard.html` and `mobile-app.html` open simultaneously for Diego, registering a mission on mobile correctly updated the website's Activity Ledger and each card's own accept/dismiss badge live (via the existing generic `mergeLedgerData` merge), but the **Pending Missions/Milestones/Progress Monitor panel contents themselves stayed stale** until the dashboard tab was manually reloaded — "Bank More with Us" kept showing in Pending Missions with an "Accepted" badge instead of disappearing from the panel the way it correctly did on mobile. Root cause: `dashboard.html`'s `storage` event listener merged the generic ledger and had a Robert-only stage-change branch, but had **no Diego-specific branch at all** — mobile's own storage listener has had one since §75-era work; this side never got the matching half.

**Fix, `dashboard.html`'s `storage` listener**: added a `diegoDataChanged` block, a direct mirror of `mobile-app.html`'s own identical block — merges `shared.journeyActionResponses` into `session.journeyActionResponses` (only when it actually differs) and adopts `shared.diegoContainerOffers`/`diegoContainerMeta` if not already cached, computed *unconditionally* ahead of the stage check so a same-write stage change also sees the freshest jar. `stageChanged` widened from `loan-card-crosssell`-only to also cover `priority-banking-upgrade`. Both the `stageChanged` and the (new) `diegoDataChanged` branches recompute `dashState.availableMissions`/`availableMilestones`/`progressMonitor` via `getDiegoAvailableMissions`/`getDiegoAvailableMilestones`/`getDiegoProgressMonitor` — these three were previously only ever computed once, at initial page load (line ~915), never inside `loadOffers()` itself or anywhere reachable from a live cross-tab update — then call `loadOffers()` to recompute Personalized Offers for his current stage from the (now up-to-date) cache, which renders internally. No live Container API call is ever triggered by any of this — `ensureDiegoContainerOffers` inside `loadOffers()` no-ops since the cache is already populated.

**`journey.html` deliberately left untouched**: it has no `storage` listener at all, for any customer — a separate, pre-existing gap unrelated to Diego specifically (it doesn't have Pending Missions/Milestones/Progress panels either), out of scope for this fix.

**Verified live**, two same-origin tabs open simultaneously (mobile + dashboard, no reloads): registering "Bank More with Us" in mobile's Pending Missions correctly removed it from the **already-open dashboard tab's** Pending Missions panel immediately — previously it stayed listed with just an "Accepted" badge until reload. Separately verified the `stageChanged` branch itself (a raw stage-index write from another tab) re-rendered the dashboard's Personalized Offers panel to the new stage with no errors. Regression-checked Robert (`loan-card-crosssell`) — his own live stage-sync, already working before this change, still works correctly after the refactor (Consideration-stage offers rendered live from another tab's stage write). Zero console errors on any of the three scenarios.

## 92. Aria's Container API Now Called Exactly Once, At Login ("For Now") — Same Treatment as Diego (§52) and Robert (§88)

Direct follow-up to a Q&A confirming Aria's `wealth-management` journey still called the Container API live on every stage transition (she and Maya were the two customers never given the one-call-per-session treatment). Explicit, scoped request: extend it to her too.

**New shared function, `js/cdh.js`**: `ensureAriaContainerOffers(session, customer)` — identical contract to `ensureRobertContainerOffers`: `if (session.ariaContainerOffers != null) return;` guards it to exactly one real call; a failure caches an empty list + `fallback: true` meta so it doesn't retry. One deliberate simplification: her Awareness-stage-only `isWealthAwareness` override (`subjectId: "CUST101", direction: "Outbound"` — modeling that stage as an outbound-triggered campaign touchpoint rather than an inbound interaction) was dropped for this one-time call. That distinction was meaningful for a *per-stage* fetch scoped specifically to Awareness; a single call now covers her whole session with no one stage to attribute an outbound-campaign framing to, so it uses the same plain default params Diego's and Robert's one-time calls already use.

**`dashboard.html`'s `loadOffers()` and `journey.html`'s `loadStage()`**: both gained a new `else if (journey.id === "wealth-management")` branch, positioned between Robert's and the final generic `else` (now only reachable for Maya and Sam's Scratch & Win journey) — identical shape to Robert's branch, just against `session.ariaContainerOffers`/`ariaContainerMeta`. Note: `isWealthAwareness`/`cdhOverrides` in `journey.html` are now unreachable dead paths within the remaining generic `else` branch (harmless — left as-is rather than doing an unrelated cleanup pass).

**`dashboard.html`'s `storage` listener**: `stageChanged` widened to also include `wealth-management`, so a stage advance for Aria from another tab still triggers a live re-render here — same treatment Robert already had, no Diego-style `availableMissions`/`Milestones`/`ProgressMonitor` panels needed since she doesn't have those.

**Verified live**: fresh login as Aria — confirmed exactly one `"Container API called once at login"` log entry and 35 real cached offers. Accepted her Awareness-stage offer (FD Maturity Reinvestment) — stage advanced to Consideration, correctly showing "SIP / Regular Investment" from the cache with the **identical latency value** (10230ms) as the original call, proving reuse rather than a fresh fetch — log count stayed at 1. Confirmed `journey.html`'s guided walkthrough also reuses the same cache (same offer, same call count). Regression-checked Diego and Robert — both load and behave normally, unaffected (only a new `else if` branch was inserted; their existing branches were untouched). Same pre-existing, unrelated Supabase 401 noted (Aria's id is also in `EVENT_STORE_CUSTOMER_IDS`) — confirmed unrelated to this change, per §88/§89.

## 93. Aria's Time Deposit Now Matures 10 Days Out (Computed Relative to Today, Not a Fixed Date)

Direct request: have Aria's Horizon Time Deposit show a maturity 10 days from now, instead of the fixed `"2027-03-15"` `getAccountsForCustomer` (js/data.js — shared by every website page including Agent Desktop, plus `mobile-app.html`'s own inlined copy) gives every customer with a Time Deposit account.

**Fix**: scoped to `customer.id === "CUST101"` only — `maturity` is computed as `new Date(Date.now() + 10 * 24 * 60 * 60 * 1000)`, not a hardcoded calendar date. Deliberately dynamic rather than baking in "2026-08-30" as a literal string: a fixed date would silently become a date in the past the next time this demo runs weeks or months from now, undermining the "about to mature" point of the request. Computing it relative to whenever the page is actually loaded keeps it always true. Every other customer's Time Deposit is untouched (`"2027-03-15"`, same as before).

**Verified live**: `getAccountsForCustomer(CUSTOMERS.find(c => c.id === "CUST101"))` returns `maturity: "2026-08-30"` (today, 2026-08-20, + 10 days) on both `mobile-app.html` and `dashboard.html`, both rendering "Matures 2026-08-30 · 4.75% p.a." Confirmed Diego's Time Deposit is unaffected (`"2027-03-15"`, unchanged). Agent Desktop reads the same shared `js/data.js` function so it picks up the correct value automatically — its Accounts Overview table just doesn't have a maturity column to display it in, a pre-existing table-layout choice unrelated to this fix. Zero console errors tied to this change (one unrelated, pre-existing transient live-CDH-call failure observed on Agent Desktop, already gracefully handled by its own existing mock fallback).

## 94. Aria's Mobile View No Longer Calls the Container API At All — Adopts From the Website Instead, and Auto-Enters Her Journey on Login Like Diego

Two related requests, same session: "Have the mobile app be in sync with Dashboard for Aria also — don't call Container API again when you use Mobile App but rather use data from Web Site Container call," followed by "Let Aria also get into the Journey automatically on log in like Diego." Both extend patterns already built for Diego/Robert to Aria's mobile experience specifically — the website side (§92) already called the Container API once at login and cached it; mobile still had two gaps: it made its own live call instead of reusing that cache, and it didn't guarantee she lands on her journey if she opens the app before ever visiting the website.

**Gap 1 confirmed before fixing**: `mobile-app.html`'s `fetchOffers()` had no `wealth-management`-specific branch at all — Aria fell through to the fully generic, non-stage-scoped live-fetch branch (filtered only by the *union* of all her journey's action titles via `getJourneyActionTitleSet`, not per-stage via `filterOffersForStage`), calling `simulateCDHRequest` live on every dashboard load. `loadJourneyStage()` (the "Walk through journey" flow) similarly still did its own live fetch per stage, using an `isWealthAwareness` override (`subjectId: "CUST101", direction: "Outbound"`) scoped to her Awareness stage. Neither matched her website counterpart's stage-scoped, adopt-only behavior from §92.

**Fix, `mobile-app.html`** — mirrors Robert's exact §89 treatment:
- New `adoptSharedAriaContainerOffers(customerId)`, directly mirroring `adoptSharedRobertContainerOffers` — guarded on `customerId !== "CUST101"`, reads the shared session and copies `ariaContainerOffers`/`ariaContainerMeta` into local `state` if present. No fetch, ever.
- `fetchOffers()` gained a new `state.activeJourney?.id === "wealth-management"` branch (positioned after Robert's, before the generic fallback) — adopts, then filters via `filterOffersForStage`, exactly like Robert's branch; explicitly guards `!Array.isArray(state.ariaContainerOffers)` to render an empty "nothing adopted yet" state rather than ever falling through to a live/mock call.
- `loadJourneyStage()` gained the equivalent `state.activeJourney.id === "wealth-management"` early-return branch, positioned before the generic `try`/`catch`. Since this now covers *every* stage of her journey (not just Awareness), the old `isWealthAwareness` special-casing became dead code for her specific case — removed from the `cdhOverrides` construction and from both the `try` and `catch` branches below it (kept `isScratchWin`'s equivalent logic, which still applies to a different journey/customer).
- Three empty-state messages — dashboard rail (`ariaNoDataYet`/`ariaNoDataHtml`, mirroring `robertNoDataYet`), the guided-journey stepper view (`renderJourneyFlow`), and the Offers-tab exclusive view (`mobileOffersOnly`) — all gained the same "nothing adopted yet" vs. "genuinely nothing eligible" distinction Robert's already has.
- `selectCustomer()` resets `state.ariaContainerOffers`/`ariaContainerMeta` to `null` on every customer switch, alongside the existing Diego/Robert resets.
- The `storage` cross-tab listener's stage-change handler: `wealth-management` added alongside `priority-banking-upgrade` to the branch that calls `fetchOffers()` (not just `loadJourneyStage()`) on a stage change from another tab, since her Home dashboard rail now also reads stage-scoped data straight from `state.offers`, the same reasoning already documented there for Diego.

**Fix for the second request (auto-enter journey on login)** — `selectCustomer()` gained a fallback block directly mirroring Diego's existing one: `if (id === "CUST101" && (!state.activeJourney || state.activeJourney.id !== "wealth-management")) { state.activeJourney = getJourneyById("wealth-management"); state.journeyIndex = 0; }`, placed right after Diego's equivalent block. `adoptSharedCurrentStage(id)` (already fully generic, not customer-hardcoded) already handles the case where she *has* visited the website first; this fallback only matters the first time she opens mobile with a completely empty `localStorage` — previously `state.activeJourney` stayed `null` until she manually tapped "Walk through journey," unlike Diego who has always auto-landed on his journey either way.

**Verified live**: fresh `localStorage.clear()` + direct mobile launch for Aria (no prior website visit) — confirmed `state.activeJourney.id` was `"wealth-management"` immediately (journey auto-entered), `state.ariaContainerOffers` stayed `null`, `state.offers` stayed `[]`, and `state.log` was completely empty (zero Container API calls of any kind). Dashboard rail correctly rendered "Nothing to show yet — open the website once as Aria to load her offers." Advancing to the Consideration stage via `goToStage` correctly logged the guided-flow's own "Nothing to show yet... campaign data" message, no call made. Seeded the shared session with a fake "website already fetched" payload (2 offers, distinctive `latencyMs: 120`) and re-ran `selectCustomer` — mobile adopted instantly, dashboard rail showed exactly the Awareness-stage offer ("FD Maturity Reinvestment") with the seeded meta, zero new log entries. Advanced to Consideration — guided flow correctly showed "SIP / Regular Investment" (the seeded Consideration-stage offer) from the same cache, still zero new calls. Regression-checked Diego and Robert on mobile — both still show zero calls and empty state on a fresh launch, unaffected by the edits. Regression-checked Sam (a customer on the untouched generic live-fetch path) — live call still succeeded normally (`latencyMs: 4042`, 3 offers, correct impressions logged), confirming the `isWealthAwareness` removal didn't affect that shared code path. Zero console errors throughout.

## 95. New Brevo Event — `pega_nba_mission_comp` — Fires the Moment a "Mission Completed" Card Becomes Eligible (Not When It's Accepted)

Direct request: "Can we call the Brevo event `pega_nba_mission_comp` for Diego when Mission Completed." Initially built to fire on *accepting* either Mission Completed card (Bank More with Us or Spread the Word). Immediate follow-up correction: **"trigger the brevo event when Mission Completed appears, not when it is accepted"** — the event should mark the moment the card first becomes visible/eligible (its mission's last required Engage action gets accepted), not whenever the customer later happens to click it, which can be much later or never.

**Why "appears" needed its own detection, not just a title check**: `applyPriorityBankingCampaignAdjustments` (js/cdh.js) is what actually decides whether "Mission Completed" survives into Personalized Offers — it's gated on `bankMoreComplete` (`Upgrade to Priority Banking` + `Open a New Investment Account` both accepted) / `spreadWordComplete` (`Refer Your Buddy` accepted), computed fresh on every stage load and every response. Since that eligibility is monotonic (once true, stays true for the rest of the session) but gets *recomputed* on every render, a plain "is it currently eligible" check would refire on every subsequent view — an edge-triggered "just became eligible" signal needed its own dedup state.

**Fix, `js/cdh.js`**: new `notifyDiegoMissionCompletedIfNewlyEligible(session, customer)` — computes `bankMoreComplete`/`spreadWordComplete` the same way `clearStaleMissionCompletedResolve` already does, checks each against a new `session.diegoMissionCompletedNotified` map (deliberately separate from `journeyActionResponses`, which only ever holds `"accepted"`/`"dismissed"`), marks it notified *before* the fetch resolves (so an overlapping call can't double-fire), then calls `triggerBrevoDecisionEvent(customer, "pega_nba_mission_comp")` and logs the result. Called (not awaited, so it never blocks the caller) from the two checkpoints where eligibility could have just changed — the same two `clearStaleMissionCompletedResolve(session)` call sites already established for exactly this reason: `loadOffers()`'s Diego branch (stage-load time — covers landing fresh on Earn, or a mission completed via a path that bypasses `handleRespond` entirely, e.g. investment-application.html's own finalize) and `handleRespond`'s Diego block (covers completing the mission via a direct accept click).

**Fix, `mobile-app.html`**: mirrored inline as its own `notifyDiegoMissionCompletedIfNewlyEligible(customer)`, called from `respond()`'s `isDiego` block (mobile's single funnel for every accept/dismiss, dashboard rail or guided journey alike — its own investment-application wizard's `finalizeInvestmentApplication` also routes through `respond()`, so no separate bypass case to cover there). The dedup flag is stored in the **shared session** (`localStorage`, same key both surfaces read/write) rather than local `state` — read fresh via `readSharedSession()` each call — so if the same demo session is used on both website and mobile, whichever surface observes the transition first marks it for both, and the event never fires twice for the same mission.

**Removed**: the previous accept-triggered hooks in `dashboard.html`'s `handleRespond` and `mobile-app.html`'s `respond()` (the `.then()`-based block keyed on `status === "accepted"` + title match) — fully replaced by the above, not left alongside it.

**Verified live** (both website and mobile): seeded a session at the Engage stage with only one of Bank More with Us's two required actions accepted (`Upgrade to Priority Banking`) — confirmed no Brevo event yet, `diegoMissionCompletedNotified` empty. Accepted the second required action (`Open a New Investment Account`) — Activity Ledger immediately showed `Brevo event sent for Diego Alvarez — "pega_nba_mission_comp" (Mission Completed — now available)`, logged **before** the stage-advance impressions and well before the Mission Completed card itself was ever touched. Separately accepted the Mission Completed card afterward — confirmed **no second Brevo entry** (dedup via the notified flag holding). Repeated the same sequence on `mobile-app.html` — identical: fired the instant `Open a New Investment Account` was accepted, `diegoMissionCompletedNotified: {"mission completed": true}` written to the shared session, no re-fire on the card's own later acceptance. Regression-checked Robert on the website — loads and behaves normally, unaffected. Zero console errors across every scenario.

## 97. Maya Given the Full Container-Once + Auto-Journey Treatment (Website and Mobile) — and a Real Toggle-Drift Bug Fixed Along the Way

Direct request: "For Maya also call the Container API only once when she logs in, also start the journey automatically." Same pattern already built for Diego (§52), Robert (§88), and Aria (§92) — she was the last remaining stage-scoped-journey customer still on the fully live, uncached Container API path (`dashboard.html`/`journey.html`'s final generic `else` branch, now reachable only for Sam and the Scratch & Win journey). "Start the journey automatically" was already true on the *website* — `login.html`'s `selectCustomer` has set `session.currentJourneyId = "digital-activation-onboarding"` for her for a while (confirmed by reading it before touching anything) — so the actionable gap was mobile, which had **zero** Maya-specific handling at all (no auto-journey fallback, no adopt-only container logic — she was purely on the generic live-fetch path there too). Given every other customer with this pattern got full website + mobile parity, implemented both here in one pass rather than website-only.

**Fix, `js/cdh.js`**: new `ensureMayaContainerOffers(session, customer)`, byte-for-byte the same shape as `ensureAriaContainerOffers`.

**Fix, `dashboard.html`**: new `else if (journey.id === "digital-activation-onboarding")` branch in `loadOffers()`, positioned after Aria's and before the final generic `else` (now Sam/Scratch-&-Win only). `storage` listener's `stageChanged` widened to include `"digital-activation-onboarding"`.

**Fix, `journey.html`**: identical new branch in `loadStage()`.

**Fix, `mobile-app.html`** — full adopt-only + auto-journey parity, mirroring Aria's exact §94 treatment: new `adoptSharedMayaContainerOffers(customerId)`; new stage-scoped adopt-only branches in both `fetchOffers()` and `loadJourneyStage()`; `mayaNoDataYet`/`mayaNoDataHtml` empty-state messaging added at all three call sites (dashboard rail, `renderJourneyFlow`, `mobileOffersOnly`); `state.mayaContainerOffers`/`mayaContainerMeta` reset in `selectCustomer()`; `storage` listener's `fetchOffers()`-triggering branch widened to include her journey id; new auto-journey fallback block in `selectCustomer()` mirroring Diego's/Aria's.

**A real pre-existing bug found and fixed along the way**: `mobile-app.html`'s own inlined `MAYA_ACTIVE_JOURNEY_ID` constant was `"loan-card-crosssell"`, while `js/data.js`'s copy (the website's) was `"digital-activation-onboarding"` — the two files had drifted out of sync, evidently because someone flipped the toggle on the website at some point (its own comment literally says "Flip this back to..., " implying a prior flip-away) without mirroring the change into mobile's separate copy (per `CLAUDE.md`'s standing warning about this file's inlined duplicates). This surfaced immediately while testing: mobile showed Maya's journey map as "Personal Loan & Card Cross-Sell" instead of "Digital Activation & Onboarding." Fixed by syncing mobile's constant to match the website's current value. Separately, while writing the new auto-journey fallback block, caught and fixed a related latent bug in my own new code: `getJourneyById(id)` always finds a journey by id *regardless* of whether `eligibleCustomerId` currently includes that customer (Maya's is the only journey in this codebase whose eligibility is itself toggle-conditional) — a bare truthiness check (`if (mayaJourney)`) would have wrongly forced her onto this journey even on a future flip back to `"loan-card-crosssell"`, undermining `isLoanCardJourneyCustomer`'s own correct handling right above it. Guarded with an explicit `journeyMatchesCustomer(mayaJourney, id)` check instead, so the fallback only fires when she's actually eligible for it under whatever the toggle currently says.

**Verified live**: fresh login as Maya on the website — confirmed exactly one `"Container API called once at login"` log entry and 38 real cached offers; accepted her Awareness-stage offer, stage advanced to Activation, only one Container-API log entry total (no second call). Fresh `localStorage.clear()` + direct mobile launch (no prior website visit) — `state.activeJourney.id` was `"digital-activation-onboarding"` immediately (journey auto-entered), `state.mayaContainerOffers` stayed `null`, zero API calls, dashboard rail showed "Nothing to show yet — open the website once as Maya to load her offers." Seeded a fake website-fetched payload into the shared session and re-selected her — mobile adopted instantly (`latencyMs: 555`, correct Awareness-stage offer only), advancing her stage showed the correct Consideration-stage offer from the same cache, zero new calls either time. Regression-checked Diego, Robert, and Aria on mobile — all three still auto-enter their journeys with zero calls, unaffected. Regression-checked Sam (untouched generic live-fetch path) — live call still succeeded normally. One pre-existing, unrelated Supabase 401 observed for Maya (`NEW_APPLICATION_CUSTOMER_IDS`, already documented, already gracefully handled) — confirmed unrelated to this change. Zero other console errors.

## 98. Agent Desktop Was Still Firing Its Own Live Container Call for Robert, Aria, and Maya — Fixed to Reuse Their Cache Like Diego Already Does

Reported directly, with a precise repro: "for Maya, after logging in Dashboard I opened Agent Desktop it appears Container API call opened." Confirmed live and traced to an exact root cause — a real bug, not a misunderstanding, and not scoped to Maya alone; Robert and Aria had the identical exposure.

**Root cause**: `agent-desktop.html`'s `refreshOffers()` has a generic reuse mechanism — if `session.customerId` matches and `session.lastFetchedOfferIds` is a non-empty array, it resolves those ids against the **local `OFFER_CATALOG` mock** and reuses the match. But Robert/Aria/Maya's real offers all come from their own one-call-per-session live container cache (`ensureRobertContainerOffers`/`ensureAriaContainerOffers`/`ensureMayaContainerOffers`, §88/§92/§97) — carrying real Pega-assigned ids (e.g. `"WelcomeAppDownload"`) that were **never in `OFFER_CATALOG` to begin with**. So `canReuse` evaluated `true` (the id array was there), but the inner `OFFER_CATALOG.find(o => o.id === id)` lookup silently returned nothing — and because that inner "no match" case had no `else`, execution fell through **both** the reuse branch *and* the "not reusing" messaging branch (which only fires when the outer `canReuse` itself is `false`) with zero visible indication anything had gone wrong, straight into a separate "stage-scoped fetch" block that fired its own live `simulateCDHRequest()` call. This is the exact same root-cause class of bug Diego's own dedicated `refreshOffers()` path (§ pre-existing, see its own comment) was built specifically to avoid — it was just never extended to Robert, Aria, or Maya once each of them got their own live-id container cache.

**Fix, `agent-desktop.html`**: added a new block, structurally identical to Diego's existing dedicated path, for all three: a `STAGE_SCOPED_CONTAINER_CUSTOMERS` map (`{ [PRIYA_CUSTOMER_ID]: {journeyId: "loan-card-crosssell", offersField: "robertContainerOffers"}, "CUST101": {...ariaContainerOffers}, [MAYA_CUSTOMER_ID]: {...mayaContainerOffers} }`). When the current `agentCustomer` is one of these three, it resolves offers **directly from their own cached container array** (not `OFFER_CATALOG`), filtered by their real current stage and by `journeyActionResponses` — mirroring `dashboard.html`'s own stage-scoped filtering exactly — and always returns from this block (never falls through to any live call), showing the same "hasn't been active on the website/mobile app yet" honesty message Diego's path already shows when there's nothing to reuse. The old separate "stage-scoped live fetch" block — which only ever ran for these same three journey ids — is now fully unreachable for all of them, so it was **removed entirely** rather than left as dead code; the generic `canReuse`/`OFFER_CATALOG` reuse path and its live/mock fallback are now only ever reached by Sam or any future non-stage-scoped customer, exactly as originally intended.

**Verified live**: fresh login as Maya on the website (real `login.html` → `dashboard.html` flow, not seeded state) confirmed `session.lastFetchedOfferIds = ["WelcomeAppDownload"]` and `OFFER_CATALOG.find(o => o.id === "WelcomeAppDownload")` returns nothing — reproducing the exact silent-fallthrough condition. Opening Agent Desktop for her afterward now shows `"Showing Maya's Personalized Offers from the website/mobile dashboard — no separate container call made"` (previously blank — the exact symptom reported) with the correct Awareness-stage offer; calling `refreshOffers()` twice more in a row left `mayaContainerMeta.latencyMs` identical both times, proving reuse rather than a fresh call. Repeated the identical repro for Aria — same fix, same correct behavior. Regression-checked Diego — his own dedicated path (untouched) still shows his own reuse message correctly. Regression-checked Sam — the generic path still correctly shows its diagnostic "Not reusing a prior decision — session.customerId is null..." message and still falls through to a real live call as before. Zero console errors across all four.
