No `else if` branch was added for her specifically, so she falls into the existing generic rule ("advance once every offer in the stage has a response") — the same rule Aria's and Maya's non-special-cased stages already use. `mobile-app.html`'s own `maybeAdvanceJourneyStage` was deliberately left untouched — it already only auto-advances Diego and Robert from the dashboard rail, with everyone else (Aria, Maya, and now Farah) progressing only through the guided "Walk through journey" stepper, an existing asymmetry this journey doesn't change.

**Verified live**: Farah Osei appears correctly on the login screen and dashboard; her "SME Cards & Payments — customer journey" tracker renders all three stages with the right action chips (matching the screenshot exactly). Walked through the full guided journey on the website — Awareness → Consideration → Decision — accepting/dismissing each action and confirming `session.currentStageIndex` auto-advanced correctly at each step (only after both Consideration actions were handled, matching the generic rule). Confirmed her Decision Receipt renders with her real profile data and the correct derived eligibility clause (`id === SME_CUSTOMER_ID`). On `mobile-app.html`: confirmed `getJourneysForCustomer(Farah)` returns exactly `["sme-cards-payments"]`, and that `scoreOffers`/`filterOffersForStage` correctly resolve all three stages to their exact intended titles. Confirmed the illustrated offer card renders correctly (screenshotted) once the mock fallback path was hit — this sandbox's CORS behavior against the live Pega endpoint is intermittent per customer load (already documented, `CLAUDE.md`), same as every other customer; when live succeeds, a stage legitimately shows "no eligible actions" since the real Pega instance has never heard of these brand-new demo-only titles, the same documented caveat every other customer's live container path already carries. Zero unexpected console errors — only the same pre-existing CORS/DNS failures against the live endpoints every customer already hits in this sandbox.

## 103. Fixed: "Corporate Card Reward Nudge" Could Silently Disappear Whenever the Live Container Call Happened to Succeed

Direct bug report: "Not able to see Corporate Card Reward Nudge in the catalog." §102's own "Verified live" notes had actually already observed the underlying cause during testing and mis-filed it as an acceptable, already-documented caveat ("when live succeeds, a stage legitimately shows 'no eligible actions'") rather than recognizing it as a real functional defect specific to this brand-new journey.

**Root cause**: unlike Sam's/every other pre-existing journey (each transcribed from a real Pega NBA Designer export, genuinely configured in the real backend), Farah's four "SME Cards & Payments" actions were invented directly from a screenshot for this session and were **never configured in the actual Pega instance** this demo points to. Every stage-scoped fetch path (`dashboard.html`'s Personalized Offers panel, `journey.html`'s guided walkthrough, and both equivalents in `mobile-app.html`) attempts a real live Container API call first, only falling back to local mock scoring if that call *fails* (network/CORS error) — never if it *succeeds* but simply returns nothing matching the current stage. Since the real backend can never return titles it was never given, a live call succeeding for Farah (this sandbox's CORS behavior is intermittent, per `CLAUDE.md` — it won't always fail) would silently leave her stage showing zero offers, forever, with no error and no retry — exactly the reported symptom.

**Fix**: added a dedicated `else if (journey.id === "sme-cards-payments")` branch to all four fetch paths — `dashboard.html`'s `loadOffers()`, `journey.html`'s `loadStage()`, and `mobile-app.html`'s `fetchOffers()`/`loadJourneyStage()` — that goes straight to `scoreOffers(OFFER_CATALOG, customer)` local mock scoring unconditionally, never attempting a live call at all for this journey. Also had to widen `dashboard.html`'s outer stage-scoped gate (the `if (session.currentJourneyId === "loan-card-crosssell" || ...)` condition guarding the whole block) to include `"sme-cards-payments"` — without that, the inner branch was unreachable dead code, since Farah's `currentStageIndex`-scoped Personalized Offers experience never even started without it.

**Verified live**: reproduced the exact failure condition directly — monkey-patched `simulateCDHRequest` to return a "successful" live response containing only an unrelated offer (simulating the real backend genuinely not knowing her titles) — and confirmed, on all four surfaces, "Corporate Card Reward Nudge" (and the Consideration/Decision actions) now render correctly regardless, since none of them ever consult the live response anymore for this journey. Re-ran the full Awareness → Consideration → Decision walkthrough on both website and mobile with this patch active throughout — every stage showed its correct action every time. Regression-checked that Robert's, Aria's, Maya's, and Diego's own live-container paths were untouched (this fix only branches on `journey.id === "sme-cards-payments"`, nothing else). Zero new console errors — same pre-existing unrelated network noise (401/CORS/DNS against live third-party endpoints) observed throughout this whole session on every customer.

## 112. New `enableTealiumAnalytics()` (utag.js) Added, Called Right After Segment — Then a Real Race-Condition Bug Fixed

Direct request: add a supplied `enableTealiumAnalytics()` function to `new-application.html` and call it right after `enableSegmentAnalytics()`. This is a different, more standard Tealium integration approach (the real Universal Tag, `utag.js`, with a `window.utag_data` data layer plus `utag.view()`/`utag.link()` calls) than the earlier Collect-API approach (§100/§107-109, now commented out per §111) — both are left in the file, the older one dormant.

**Added exactly as supplied**: `window.utag_data` populated with `customer_id`/`customer_name`/`customer_segment`/`tenure_years`/`application_completed`; a `utag.js` script tag inserted for `instellars-sandbox`/`abhishek.banerjee`/`prod` if not already loaded; `utag.view(window.utag_data)` (pageview parallel to `analytics.page()`) and `utag.link({...})` (the actual submit/abandon conversion event). Called via `enableTealiumAnalytics();` immediately after `enableSegmentAnalytics();` at `stage === 2`.

**A real bug found immediately on first live verification**: the supplied code called `window.utag.view(...)` and `window.utag.link(...)` on the very next line after inserting the `<script>` tag — but script loading is asynchronous, so `window.utag` isn't defined yet at that point. On every real page load (this page fully reloads on every stage change, so `window.utag` never carries over from a previous visit), this threw `TypeError: Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'view')` immediately, meaning `utag.link()` — the one call that actually captures the conversion event — never ran at all. Confirmed this is a genuine, real, live Tealium account (the script does load successfully and `window.utag_data` ends up fully populated with real Tealium session/account data), so the fix is purely about sequencing, not a broken/fake destination.

**Fix, `new-application.html`**: extracted the `.view()`/`.link()` pair into a local `fireTealiumViewAndLink()` closure. If `window.utag` already exists, call it immediately (defensive, for a same-page-life re-call — doesn't happen today since every stage change is a full navigation). Otherwise, attach `fireTealiumViewAndLink` as the script tag's `onload` handler (plus an `onerror` handler that just logs, so a failed script load doesn't throw) — the same deferred-until-loaded pattern Tealium's own real production snippets use for this exact reason.

**Verified live**: on two separate completely fresh page loads (new browser tabs, no prior `window.utag` state), zero console errors either time — the previous `Cannot read properties of undefined` error is gone. Confirmed `window.utag_data.tealium_event` becomes `"view"` shortly after load (Tealium's own internal tracking recording the deferred `.view()` call actually landing), and confirmed `.link()` also actually executes (monkey-patched `window.utag.link` and called `enableTealiumAnalytics()` again once loaded — captured the exact expected payload `{event_name: "application_abandoned", application_id: null}` with no exception). Segment Analytics and the (dormant, commented-out) old Tealium Collect call are both unaffected.

## 115. Tealium Events Investigation: Dev/QA/Prod Comparison, a Confirmed Tag Live in Prod, and a Transient 400

Follow-up investigation, no code changes, into "Tealium events are still not flowing thru." User reported seeing a "Tealium Collect" tag (UID 1) configured in their account, which didn't match the earlier finding that `window.utag.loader.cfg`/`window.utag.sender` were empty.

**Explained what those two fields mean** (`loader.cfg` = utag.js's table of every configured tag/connector for the profile, keyed by uid; `sender` = the runtime registry of tag instances actually created from that table) — both empty means no tags exist in the currently-served build, not "tags configured but not firing."

**Checked all three environments directly** by injecting `utag.js` manually for each (`.../instellars-sandbox/abhishek.banerjee/{env}/utag.js`, one per isolated browser tab to avoid `window.utag` bleeding between checks): **Dev** and **QA** both still came back with `loader.cfg` completely empty (`{}`) — no tags published to either. **Prod**, on a recheck, had changed since the very first check: `loader.cfg["1"]` was now present (`{"load":1,"send":1,"v":202609010905,...,"executed":1}`), with a newer build version stamp than before — confirming a fresh Prod publish happened in between. Running the real app flow confirmed the tag actually fires (`sender["1"]` populated, not just listed).

**A 400 error appeared once** right where the Collect tag's own outbound request would fire — couldn't inspect the exact request/response (cross-origin, not exposed to page scripts or this browser tooling), so flagged it to the user as something to check via the tag's own field mappings or Tealium's request log. **Retested on request** (three separate loads — two same-tab reloads, one completely fresh tab) — zero console errors on all three; the 400 did not reproduce. Likely transient (a momentary receiving-endpoint issue) rather than a code-side problem, since nothing in this app's code changed between the two checks.

## 118. Diagnosed and Fixed a Real Cause of Intermittent Tealium Event Loss: Navigation Cutting Off the In-Flight Request

Follow-up report: "it still appears sometimes the events are passing to Tealium but not always." Since the §112 load-order race was already ruled out (confirmed correct in §117), walked through the other plausible causes: browser extensions/ad-blockers, Tealium account-side load rules or consent gating, the already-observed transient 400 (§115) — and one genuinely code-controllable cause: the confirmation page (`new-application.html?stage=2`) puts "Back to dashboard," "Start over," and the header "Exit to dashboard" link right next to where the Tealium call fires, with nothing stopping a user from clicking through immediately. A fast click can cause the browser to abort the still-in-flight `utag.js` fetch (or the event send it triggers) on navigation — which would explain "sometimes," since it depends entirely on how quickly the user clicks away.

**Fix, `new-application.html`**: added a navigation guard scoped to the confirmation page only.
- A `tealiumNavGuard` promise is created alongside `enableTealiumAnalytics()`, resolved by `releaseTealiumNavGuard()` — called ~150ms after `fireTealiumViewAndLink()` actually runs (giving the browser a beat to dispatch the request), and immediately from the script's `onerror` handler (nothing to wait for if `utag.js` failed to load).
- `navigateAfterTealium(href)` races that guard against a hard 1500ms timeout, then navigates — so it never blocks the user for long even if something hangs.
- The header "Exit to dashboard" link and the confirmation panel's "Back to dashboard" link both gained `onclick="handleExitToDashboard(event)"`. That handler is a no-op (`return`, letting the plain `href` navigate instantly) on every stage except stage 2, where it calls `e.preventDefault()` and routes through `navigateAfterTealium("dashboard.html")`.
- `handleStartOver()`'s `window.location.href = stageHref(0)` was changed to `navigateAfterTealium(stageHref(0))` (only reachable from the Abandoned-outcome confirmation page, so no stage check needed there).

**Verified live** (Robert, `new-application.html?stage=2`, preview server on port 8793):
- Manually invoked `handleExitToDashboard({preventDefault(){...}})` and also dispatched a real `.click()` on the "Back to dashboard" `<a>` — both correctly held the page in place immediately after the click (confirmed via `window.location.href` still pointing at `new-application.html?stage=2` right after), then navigated to `dashboard.html` shortly after once the guard resolved. No console errors, no hang.
- On stage 0, clicked "Exit to dashboard" and confirmed it navigated to `dashboard.html` immediately (no delay) — the stage-check short-circuit in `handleExitToDashboard` works as intended, so this fix doesn't add any latency outside the confirmation page.
- Also confirmed via direct inspection that `utag.js` (real `instellars-sandbox`/`abhishek.banerjee`/`prod` profile) loads and populates `window.utag_data` correctly, and that `window.utag.sender` shows an entry for uid `1` — the account-side tag from §115 is still live.

**Not fixed by this change** (out of scope / not code-controllable): ad-blocker interference, Tealium account-side load rules or consent gating, and infra-side transient errors like the 400 seen in §115. If events are still missing after this fix, the next diagnostic step is checking the browser console for the `"Tealium utag.js failed to load"` line and the Network tab for whether `tags.tiqcdn.com/utag.js` even started before any navigation, on a run where the event doesn't land.

## 121. BehavioralData Call Gained a Site-Wide Enable/Disable Flag, and Disabled for Diego

Direct question then request: confirmed the `BehavioralData` Insert call (`js/behavioral.js`) fires as a direct client-side `fetch()` from the browser straight to `https://instellars02.pegalabs.io/prweb/api/BehavioralData/01.01/Insert` on every click, site-wide, for whichever customer is currently logged in (not Diego-specific — it's wired into `dashboard.html`, `journey.html`, `new-application.html`, `browse.html`, `emi-calculator.html`, `investment-application.html`, `sip-application.html`, `login.html`). Follow-up: "Can we add a flag so that we can enable disable this functionality and also for now disable this for Diego."

**Fix, `js/behavioral.js`**:
- Added `const BEHAVIORAL_DATA_ENABLED = true;` — a single site-wide switch checked at the top of `sendBehavioralData()`; flipping it to `false` stops every BehavioralData call without touching the click listener itself.
- Added `const BEHAVIORAL_DATA_DISABLED_CUSTOMER_IDS = ["C1004"];` (Diego, "for now" per explicit scoped request — not a permanent exclusion) — checked right after `customerId` is resolved from the session; if it matches, `sendBehavioralData()` returns before building the payload or calling `fetch`.
- Both checks are early returns inside the existing function — no changes to the click listener, `describeClickedElement`, or the payload shape for anyone not on the disabled list.

On a separate fresh session as Diego (`C1004`), the same dispatched click produced zero calls to the endpoint — confirmed via both a direct call to `sendBehavioralData()` and a dispatched `click` event on `document.body`. (Note while testing: `dashboard.html?customer=X` only sets the active customer on a session that doesn't already have one — an existing `localStorage` session silently overrides the query param. Not a bug, just something to know when manually driving this app via URL rather than clicking through `login.html`'s own customer-picker links.)

## 124. All Four SME Journeys: Auto-Enter on Login, Stage-Scoped Offers, Auto-Advance

Direct request, given as three behaviors for "all SME Journeys": (1) auto-position a user at the first stage on login, (2) only show offers applicable to the stage the user is currently on, (3) once every offer on a stage has been viewed or dismissed, auto-advance to the next stage.

**Investigation first, before writing anything**: (2) and (3) were already fully built — `dashboard.html`'s `loadOffers()` already had a dedicated stage-scoped branch for all four `sme-*` journey ids (added in §103, always-mock per the "invented journey" pattern) that filters the catalog to the current stage via `filterOffersForStage`, and `maybeAdvanceJourneyStage` (`js/session.js`) already includes all four SME ids in its top-level guard and falls through to the generic "advance once every offer in the stage has a recorded response (accepted or dismissed)" rule — and in this codebase, clicking an offer's primary CTA ("View offer"/"See rewards"/etc.) already calls `handleRespond(id, "accepted")`, so "viewed" and "accepted" are the same recorded status here, not two different things to track separately. The only actual gap: **nothing ever set `session.currentJourneyId` to an SME journey id**, so the stage-scoped branch's outer guard (`session.currentJourneyId === "sme-cards-payments" || ...`) was always false in practice — the four SME customers instead fell through to the generic, non-stage-scoped offer path, which just showed whichever single catalog offer scored highest across their *entire* journey regardless of stage (explaining why Farah's dashboard was showing her Decision-stage "SME Credit Card" card with no journey entered at all, seen incidentally during §123's testing).

**Fix, `login.html`**: added an `else if` branch per SME customer (`SME_CUSTOMER_ID`/`SME_LOYALTY_CUSTOMER_ID`/`SME_LENDING_CUSTOMER_ID`/`SME_DIGITAL_BANKING_CUSTOMER_ID`) to `selectCustomer(id)`, each setting `session.currentJourneyId` to that customer's journey id and `session.currentStageIndex = 0` — identical shape to the existing Aria/Maya/Diego blocks. This alone activates both (1) and (2)/(3) together, since the downstream logic was already stage-aware and journey-agnostic.

**Fix, `dashboard.html`**: the cross-tab `storage` listener's `stageChanged` check (used to pick up a stage move made from another tab/the mobile app while this tab is open) only recognized `loan-card-crosssell`/`priority-banking-upgrade`/`wealth-management`/`digital-activation-onboarding` — widened to include all four `sme-*` ids too, so a stage advance from mobile is reflected live here as well (same fix shape as Maya's §97 extension).

**Fix, `mobile-app.html`**: mirrored the same "default to a fresh Awareness-stage start only if nothing was already adopted from a shared session" pattern already used for Diego/Aria/Maya, in its own `selectCustomer(id)` — one block per SME customer, so opening the mobile app fresh (before ever visiting the website) also lands the right customer on stage 0 of their own journey rather than nothing at all.

**Verified live**, full round-trip as Farah (`CUST652`, "SME Cards & Payments" — Awareness → Consideration → Decision):
- Fresh login → `session.currentJourneyId === "sme-cards-payments"`, `currentStageIndex === 0` — confirmed via `loadSession()`.
- Dashboard's Personalized Offers showed **only** "Corporate Card Reward Nudge" (Awareness) — not the Decision-stage "SME Credit Card" that used to show pre-fix.
- Dismissed it → auto-advanced to Consideration (`currentStageIndex === 1`), now showing **both** of that stage's offers ("Payroll Account Setup," "POS Merchant Setup").
- Dismissed one → stayed on Consideration (stage only advances once *every* offer in it is handled) — confirmed via `currentStageIndex` still `1`.
- Dismissed the other → auto-advanced to Decision (`currentStageIndex === 2`), now showing "SME Credit Card."
- Spot-checked Elena (`CUST651`, "SME Lending & Working Capital") — fresh login also correctly landed on `currentStageIndex === 0`.
- `mobile-app.html`, opened fresh (cleared `localStorage`, no prior website visit) and calling its own `selectCustomer(SME_CUSTOMER_ID)` — landed on `sme-cards-payments` stage 0, and its own Home dashboard showed the same single Awareness-stage offer as the website.

## 125. SME Journeys Switched From Always-Mock Back to Live-First (§103's Condition No Longer Holds)

Reported as a regression — "Some thing broke, Farah directly going to fallback mode" — right after §124 made her actually enter her journey on login. Investigated live first: zero console errors, and the activity ledger explicitly read `... (mock)`, confirming this was §103's deliberate always-mock branch firing exactly as designed, now visible for the first time because §124 finally routed her through it. Explained this to the user and asked whether to leave it or attempt live now — reply: **"Attempt a live call as these actions exist in pega now."**

**Verified the premise first** via a raw `fetch` straight to `CDH_ENDPOINT` for `CUST652`: the live Container API now genuinely returns all four SME Cards & Payments titles (`CorporateCardRewardNudge`, `PayrollAccountSetup`, `POSMerchantSetup`, `SMECreditCard`), plus titles for the other three SME journeys (`WorkingCapitalOffer`, `SMEAnniversary`, `CashFlowInsightAlert`) — confirming §103's original premise ("never configured in real Pega") no longer holds.

**Fix, `dashboard.html` (`loadOffers()`) and `journey.html` (`loadStage()`)**: replaced each SME branch's unconditional local-mock call with the exact same try/catch shape already used by the generic (non-SME) stage-scoped branch right below it — attempt `simulateCDHRequest(customer)`, filter to the current stage, and only fall back to local mock scoring on an actual thrown error (network/HTTP failure), not merely an empty stage match. No caching layer added (unlike Diego/Robert/Aria/Maya's `ensureXContainerOffers` "once per session" pattern) — this calls live fresh per stage view, matching what was actually asked.

**Fix, `mobile-app.html` (both SME branches — `fetchOffers()`'s dashboard-rail block and the guided-journey stage block)**: same try/catch swap, but with one addition found only through live testing (see below): both calls now pass `{ channel: "Web" }` explicitly, overriding this file's own `simulateCDHRequest` default of `channel: "Mobile"`.

**A real bug found via live verification, not by inspection**: after wiring up the naive live call, `mobile-app.html`'s own fetch for Farah came back with **zero** offers on every attempt, while the exact same live query run manually (raw `fetch` → `dedupeByName` → `mapPegaOffer`, replicating `simulateCDHRequest`'s own internals line for line) reliably returned all 7. Bisected the difference down to a single request field: `simulateCDHRequest(customer, { channel: "Web" })` returns 7 offers; `simulateCDHRequest(customer, { channel: "Mobile" })` — `mobile-app.html`'s own default — returns 0, every time, confirmed over 5 consecutive calls. Conclusion: these four SME actions are currently configured in Pega for the **Web** channel only, not Mobile — a real, live backend eligibility fact, not a client-side bug (same category as Diego's §52 caveat, just channel-scoped instead of stage-scoped). Diego/Robert/Aria/Maya's mobile views never hit this because they don't call live independently at all — they *adopt* whatever the website already fetched over Web. Rather than build that same cache-and-adopt architecture for SME (a bigger change than what was asked), the minimal fix was to have `mobile-app.html`'s two SME call sites request `channel: "Web"` explicitly, so mobile shows the same live decisioning result the website gets — the intent being parity with the website's data, not a literal separate Mobile-channel call these actions were never configured for.

**Verified live**, full pass as Farah (`CUST652`):
- `dashboard.html`: badge now reads "Live" (not "Fallback mode"); Awareness stage correctly shows only "Corporate Card Reward Nudge" from the real response. Dismissed it → advanced to Consideration → both "Payroll Account Setup" and "POS Merchant Setup" shown, still "Live." Zero console errors throughout.
- `journey.html`, jumping directly to the Consideration stage: "Live · Instellars Real-Time Engagement Engine · ...ms · Subject: Farah Osei," both offers present.
- `mobile-app.html`, opened fresh and calling `selectCustomer(SME_CUSTOMER_ID)` directly: Home dashboard shows "Live" with the same single Awareness-stage offer as the website, `meta.channel: "Web"` confirmed in state. Before the channel fix, this same flow reliably returned zero offers.
- Not otherwise re-verified for the other three SME customers/journeys in this pass (Carlos/Elena/Daniela) — the fix is journey-id-driven and identical in shape to the already-verified Farah path, so it should carry over the same way §124's fix did, but this wasn't independently re-confirmed live per customer this round.

---

## 126. SME Journeys Switched to the Same "Call Once Per Session, Cache" Pattern as Diego/Robert/Aria/Maya

Direct follow-up to §125. Asked directly whether the Container API was being called for every stage of an SME journey — confirmed yes (fresh `simulateCDHRequest` on every stage view/page load/Refresh/outage-toggle, no caching), unlike Diego/Robert/Aria/Maya's dedicated `ensureXContainerOffers` "call once at login" pattern. Offered to switch SME to the same pattern; reply: **"Yes please."**

**Fix, `js/cdh.js`**: added `ensureFarahContainerOffers`/`ensureCarlosContainerOffers`/`ensureElenaContainerOffers`/`ensureDanielaContainerOffers`, each an exact structural copy of `ensureMayaContainerOffers` — guard on `session.<name>ContainerOffers != null`, call `simulateCDHRequest(customer)` once, cache the result (or an empty-list-with-`fallback:true` on a thrown error) into `session.<name>ContainerOffers`/`<name>ContainerMeta`, log, save.

**Fix, `dashboard.html` (`loadOffers()`) and `journey.html` (`loadStage()`)**: replaced the single combined try/catch SME branch with four separate `else if` branches (one per journey id), each calling its matching `ensureXContainerOffers` then `filterOffersForStage` against the cached response — same shape as the existing Diego/Robert/Aria/Maya branches right above them.

**Fix, `mobile-app.html`** — the larger change, since Robert/Aria/Maya/Diego's mobile parity requires more than just the fetch call:
- Added `adoptSharedFarahContainerOffers`/`adoptSharedCarlosContainerOffers`/`adoptSharedElenaContainerOffers`/`adoptSharedDanielaContainerOffers`, mirroring `adoptSharedMayaContainerOffers` — mobile never calls the Container API for these four customers either, only copies whatever the website already cached into the shared session.
- Both `fetchOffers()`'s SME block and the guided-journey-stage function's SME block were replaced with four "adopt, don't fetch" blocks each (mirroring Maya's blocks exactly, including the "nothing to show yet" no-op when nothing's been adopted).
- `selectCustomer()`'s reset block gained `state.farahContainerOffers`/`Meta` (and Carlos/Elena/Daniela's) reset-to-null lines, alongside the existing Diego/Robert/Aria/Maya ones.
- The cross-tab `storage` listener's journey-id check (which decides whether a stage change from another tab re-runs `fetchOffers()` vs. the generic `loadJourneyStage()`) widened to include all four SME ids.
- The three "an empty offers list is ambiguous — genuinely nothing eligible, or nothing adopted yet" UI messages (two on the Home dashboard's Personalized Offers panel, one on the Journeys tab's guided-flow view) each gained four more branches for Farah/Carlos/Elena/Daniela, matching Robert/Aria/Maya's existing ones.
- This also fully superseded and removed the `{ channel: "Web" }` override added in §125 — mobile no longer calls `simulateCDHRequest` for these journeys at all, so the earlier-discovered Mobile-vs-Web channel eligibility gap (see §125) is now moot: mobile simply reads whatever the website's one real Web-channel call already cached, the same way Robert/Aria/Maya/Diego's mobile views already did.

**A real data finding surfaced while verifying, not a code bug**: testing Carlos (`CUST654`, "SME Loyalty & Protection") after Farah showed the live call now succeeds but only returns **one of his three** stage titles — `SME Anniversary` (Decision) — not `Business Insurance Renewal Reminder` (Awareness) or `Business Health Check-In` (Consideration), so his Awareness stage currently, correctly, shows "No personalized offers available right now." Checked the other two journeys' titles against the same live response to get the full picture: **Farah's "SME Cards & Payments" is the only one of the four fully configured live** (all 4 titles present); Elena's "SME Lending & Working Capital" has only `Working Capital Offer` (Consideration) present, missing `Invoice Discounting Offer` (Awareness) and `Overdraft Facility Offer` (Decision); Daniela's "SME Digital Banking & Cash Flow" has `Tax Filing Reminder` (Awareness) and `Cash Flow Insight Alert` (Decision) present, missing `Bulk Payment Upload Nudge` (Consideration). This is the same category of fact as Diego's §52 caveat — the code is correctly and honestly reflecting exactly what the live Container API returns; any gaps are on the Pega-configuration side, not a client bug.

**Verified live**, full pass as Farah (`CUST652`):
- Dashboard's Personalized Offers stayed "Live" with the same `latencyMs` (1752ms) across a page reload, a manual "Refresh" click, and a stage advance (Awareness → Consideration, both offers shown) — confirming the call only fires once and every subsequent view reuses `session.farahContainerOffers` from cache.
- `journey.html`, jumped to a specific stage directly — same cached `latencyMs`, correct stage-filtered offers, no new network call.
- `mobile-app.html`, opened in the same browser session and calling `selectCustomer(SME_CUSTOMER_ID)` — adopted the exact same cached response (`meta.latencyMs` identical to the website's), correct Consideration-stage offers, confirming the "call once on the website, mobile only ever adopts" contract now holds for SME the same as it does for Robert/Aria/Maya/Diego.
- `mobile-app.html`, opened with a fully cleared `localStorage` (no shared session at all) — correctly showed "Nothing to show yet — open the website once as Farah to load her offers (this view never fetches it directly)," no console errors.

## 127. Fixed: A Cached Container-API Failure for the Four SME Customers No Longer Sticks for the Whole Session

Reported: "Elena still going to fallback directly." Two independent fresh-login tests (dashboard + journey.html) both showed "Live" for Elena, not fallback — so the code wasn't unconditionally broken. Stress-tested the raw live endpoint for her subject id (`CUST651`) in a tight loop to check for the transient-failure pattern already seen once before (§115's Tealium 400): the **first** call succeeded genuinely (12 offers, 1661ms); every call fired shortly after came back in ~250ms with no `Status` field at all, which `simulateCDHRequest` correctly treats as a thrown error. Conclusion: this Pega instance appears to rate-limit/throttle repeated calls for the same subject.

That's exactly the failure mode §126's "call once, cache forever" design doesn't handle gracefully: if the one call `ensureElenaContainerOffers` (or the other three) ever gets happens to land inside that throttle window — for any reason, including just testing the same customer twice in quick succession — the failure gets cached permanently for the rest of that session, and Refresh can't help because the guard clause no-ops before ever attempting a new call. This most likely explains what was reported.

**Fix, `js/cdh.js`**: changed all four SME `ensure*ContainerOffers` functions' guard from `if (session.xContainerOffers != null) return;` to `if (session.xContainerOffers != null && !session.xContainerMeta?.fallback) return;` — a genuine success still only ever fetches once per session (unchanged), but a cached failure is now retried on the very next stage view or Refresh instead of sticking forever. Updated both `pushSessionLog` messages (success and failure) to stop claiming "no further calls will be made," since that's no longer accurate for these four. **Deliberately scoped to only the four SME functions** — Diego/Robert/Aria/Maya's `ensure*ContainerOffers` keep their original "cache forever, even failures" contract, since that behavior was already established and accepted for them previously and wasn't part of what was reported broken here.

**Verified live**: seeded a fake cached failure into `session.elenaContainerMeta` (`fallback: true`) before loading the dashboard, confirming the existing bug reproduces the reported symptom on demand. Reloading `dashboard.html` correctly detected the cached failure and retried — landed on a genuine fresh success (`latencyMs: 1716`, 12 offers, no `fallback` flag), correctly overwriting the seeded failure. Clicked Refresh again afterward and confirmed the now-genuinely-cached success was *not* re-fetched (`latencyMs` unchanged) — so the "call once" contract still holds once a real success is in hand. Zero console errors throughout.

This is the same per-subject throttling/inconsistency already diagnosed for Elena — repeated rapid live calls for one subject on this shared demo Pega instance don't always come back complete. Not fixed here, since Sam wasn't asked to use the "call once, cache" pattern that would insulate him from it (like the SME customers now have) — flagged to the user as a known limitation if it recurs.

## 134. Fixed: Elena Permanently Stuck on Awareness — A Declared-But-Not-Yet-Live Action Was Blocking Stage Advancement

Reported: "Elena not moving to consideration stage once Awareness is done." Root-caused directly from the previous message's live-call finding (§133 follow-up): Elena's Awareness stage declares two actions, `Equipment Financing Nudge` and `Invoice Discounting Offer`, but only the latter is actually live in Pega right now. `maybeAdvanceJourneyStage`'s generic rule (`js/session.js`, shared by Aria's/Maya's other stages, all four SME journeys, and Sam) requires a recorded response for **every** action declared in `stage.actions` before advancing — and since `Equipment Financing Nudge` can never be shown (it's never returned live, and the SME journeys don't fall back to mock on a merely-incomplete response — §125/§126), the customer can never respond to it, so the `.every()` check could never pass. Elena was permanently stuck on Awareness no matter how many times she handled the one card that actually appears — this is the exact same category of gap as Diego's §52 caveat, just with a materially worse consequence (blocked progression, not just fewer visible offers).

**Fix, `js/session.js`** (and mirrored conceptually — see below — in `mobile-app.html`, though no code changes were needed there): changed the generic advance rule so a declared action only counts against the "every action must be handled" requirement if it was **actually shown** at least once this stage — checked by searching `session.log` for an "impression" entry matching that stage and title (`pushSessionLog`'s own impression-logging already records every title actually returned, not just what's currently on screen — confirmed this holds true even for a title never displayed as a card, since dashboard.html's top-3 display cap logs impressions off the full `matched` list, not just what's rendered). A declared action that was never impressed is now treated as vacuously satisfied — excluded from the block, not silently required forever.

**Why no change was needed in `mobile-app.html`**: its own `maybeAdvanceJourneyStage(offer, status)` only ever handles `priority-banking-upgrade` and `loan-card-crosssell` explicitly — it was never wired up to independently advance Aria's/Maya's/the SME customers'/Sam's stages at all. For those customers, stage progression is driven entirely by the website's `js/session.js` function (via `dashboard.html`'s `handleRespond`); mobile only ever *adopts* the resulting `currentStageIndex` afterward through the shared session. So this bug — and the fix — only ever lived in one place.

**Verified live**, full round-trip as Elena (`CUST651`): fresh login → Awareness correctly showed only `Invoice Discounting Offer` (confirming the missing-live-title premise). Dismissing it now correctly advances to Consideration (`currentStageIndex` 0 → 1) — previously would have stayed stuck. Handled one of Consideration's three offers → correctly stayed on Consideration (`currentStageIndex` still 1, confirming the fix didn't weaken the "every *shown* action" requirement). Handled the remaining two → correctly advanced to Decision (`currentStageIndex` → 2). **Regression check** on Farah (`CUST652`), whose Consideration stage has 5 actions all genuinely live: handling only 1 of the 5 correctly did **not** advance her — confirming the fix only excuses actions that were never shown, not actions merely left unhandled. Zero console errors throughout.

## 137. Robert-Only Toggle: Swap the CDH Container Call for a Simulated Braze Canvas Trigger + Banners Flow (Per-Stage, Not Cached)

Direct request, following the earlier Braze Web SDK discussion (Banners' `requestBannersRefresh`/`subscribeToBannersUpdates`/`insertBanner`): add a toggle, Robert-only for now, that — when on — replaces the CDH Container call with "a corresponding braze canvas call," fired on **every** journey stage (unlike the Container call's existing once-per-session cache), and updates Personalized Offers with a Braze banner. Clarified before building: "canvas call" means **both** an actual Canvas trigger **followed by** the Banners flow already discussed — and since no real Braze account/API key/Canvas ID/Placement ID exists yet, this was built fully mocked (no real network calls to Braze), matching the same "invented, no live backend yet" convention already used for the SME journeys before their own real Pega config existed (§103).

**Fix, `js/session.js`**: added `robertBrazeEnabled: false` to `defaultSession()`.

**Fix, `js/cdh.js`**: added `simulateBrazeCanvasAndBanner(session, customer, stage)` plus four placeholder constants (`BRAZE_API_KEY`, `BRAZE_SDK_ENDPOINT`, `BRAZE_CANVAS_ID`, `BRAZE_BANNER_PLACEMENT_ID`) clearly marked for swapping in real values later. Simulates the two-step flow as two separate `pushSessionLog` "system" entries (Canvas trigger, then Banners refresh — visible as two distinct ledger rows, in that order, matching the clarified two-step shape) with a short artificial delay, then picks the current stage's single top-ranked local-catalog action (`filterOffersForStage(scoreOffers(...))`) as the banner's content — deliberately returning **at most one** offer, since a real Braze Banner placement returns exactly one banner, unlike Pega's multi-offer stages. The returned offer is tagged `brazeSource: true` and `meta.source = "braze"`. Not cached anywhere — called fresh every time `loadOffers()` runs, per the explicit "each journey stage" requirement.

**Fix, `dashboard.html`**: `loadOffers()`'s Robert branch now checks `session.robertBrazeEnabled` first — when true, calls `simulateBrazeCanvasAndBanner` instead of the existing `ensureRobertContainerOffers` cached path (which is otherwise unchanged and untouched). Added a checkbox toggle ("Use Braze Canvas + Banner") next to "Simulate outage"/"Refresh," rendered only when `customer.id === PRIYA_CUSTOMER_ID` — invisible for every other customer. `renderLiveBadge` and the panel's engine badge/footer note all branch on `meta.source === "braze"` to show a distinct "Braze"/"Braze Banner" identity instead of "Live"/"Fallback mode," so simulated Braze content is never mistaken for a real or failed Pega call.

**Fix, `js/render-shared.js` (`renderOfferCard`)**: added an `isBraze` flag (`!!offer.brazeSource`) that suppresses the Decision Receipt toggle (no Pega decision exists behind a Braze banner) and the three Pega-specific "Apply Now" deep-link flows (loan calculator/SIP/investment wizards) — a Braze-sourced card always renders as a plain accept/dismiss card, plus a small "Powered by Braze" badge next to its stage badge.

**Fix, `js/session.js` (`respondToOffer`)**: skips the Pega `sendCaptureResponse` call for `offer.brazeSource` offers (there's no Pega decision to report a response against) and logs a simulated Braze conversion event instead; the generic accept/dismiss ledger line also now reads "response recorded" rather than "response sent to Interaction History" for Braze-sourced offers.

**Verified live**, full toggle cycle as Robert (`CUST792`): fresh login → normal CDH mode unaffected (cached, `13769ms`, unchanged from before this change). Toggled on → panel badge became "Braze," live badge became "Braze Banner," footer note read "Braze Canvas + Banner (simulated) · 970ms," ledger showed the Canvas-trigger entry immediately followed by the Banners-refresh entry, and exactly **one** card rendered (`Pre-Approved Loan Offer`) despite Prospect declaring three actions — with no Decision Receipt and a "Powered by Braze" badge. Clicked Refresh twice more — each produced a **new**, freshly-timestamped pair of Canvas/Banners log entries (confirmed genuinely uncached, unlike the Container call). Dismissed the banner → ledger correctly read "response recorded" and "Braze conversion event sent (simulated) — ... Dismissed" (no `sendCaptureResponse` call). Toggled back off → reverted cleanly to the cached CDH data (`13769ms`, byte-identical to the pre-toggle value, confirming the Braze detour never disturbed `session.robertContainerOffers`); `currentStageIndex` correctly stayed at 0 throughout (dismissing "Pre-Approved Loan Offer" doesn't satisfy Robert's Prospect-stage rule, which requires "In-Session Loan Calculator" specifically — unaffected by this change). Confirmed the toggle checkbox is entirely absent from Farah's (`CUST652`) dashboard. Zero console errors throughout.

**Known limitation, by design**: all Braze credentials/ids are placeholders and no real Braze account exists — swap `BRAZE_API_KEY`/`BRAZE_SDK_ENDPOINT`/`BRAZE_CANVAS_ID`/`BRAZE_BANNER_PLACEMENT_ID` (top of the new block in `js/cdh.js`) and replace `simulateBrazeCanvasAndBanner`'s body with real `fetch`/Braze Web SDK calls once real credentials exist. `mobile-app.html` and `journey.html` were not touched — this is scoped to `dashboard.html`'s Personalized Offers rail only, per the request.

## 138. Braze Toggle Moved From `dashboard.html` to `login.html` (Still Robert-Only)

Direct follow-up to §137: "instead of the dashboard lets put the toggle in login.html," then "and also make it work only for Robert" (already true in §137, reconfirmed here since the control moved to a page that lists every customer).

**Why this needed more than moving a `<div>`**: `login.html`'s `selectCustomer(id)` calls `defaultSession()` on every login, rebuilding the session from scratch — so the toggle can no longer live as in-session dashboard state the way it did in §137; it has to be read at the moment of login and written into the freshly-built session before `saveSession()`, or it would just get reset to `false` immediately.

**Fix, `login.html`**: added a checkbox (`id="robertBrazeToggle"`, unchecked by default) rendered as its own row directly under Robert's card only — not nested inside the card's own `<a>` (which would make the checkbox trigger the card's navigation too) — with `onclick="event.stopPropagation()"` so clicking it doesn't also click through to the dashboard. In `selectCustomer(id)`, added `if (id === PRIYA_CUSTOMER_ID) { session.robertBrazeEnabled = !!document.getElementById("robertBrazeToggle")?.checked; }` right before `saveSession(session)` — explicitly scoped to his id specifically (not `isLoanCardJourneyCustomer(id)`, which also covers Maya when her own toggle points her at the same journey), so this can never leak onto her.

**Fix, `dashboard.html`**: removed the in-dashboard checkbox (`robertBrazeToggleHtml`) and its now-unused `handleToggleRobertBraze` handler entirely — the toggle is no longer settable once already on the dashboard, only at login. Everything else from §137 (the `loadOffers()` branch on `session.robertBrazeEnabled`, the "Braze"/"Braze Banner" badges, the footer note) is untouched, since `session.robertBrazeEnabled` still drives the exact same behavior, just set from a different page now.

**Verified live**: login page shows exactly one checkbox on the entire page, positioned directly under Robert's card only. Checked it, logged in as Robert → dashboard immediately loaded in Braze mode (no dashboard control needed to turn it on) — same "Braze"/"Braze Banner" badges and simulated Canvas-trigger/Banners-refresh ledger entries as §137. Fresh login with the checkbox left unchecked → normal CDH mode, unaffected (`22003ms`, a genuine fresh live call — session was fully reset by the intervening `localStorage.clear()`, not the cache from a prior run). Confirmed the dashboard's Personalized Offers panel no longer has any Braze-related control at all. Zero console errors throughout.

The screenshot showed a customer journey named **"Application Abandonment Recovery"** (Awareness → Consideration → Decision, 7 actions total) — a different name and a much richer action set than the placeholder "New Customer Acquisition" journey Sam had been using since §130 (3 stages, 1 action each, largely invented pending real Pega config).

Confirmed: replace.

Rather than ship uncorrected guesses, this was checked against Sam's own `ensureSamContainerOffers` live call right after being wired up — which (same as Elena's throttling case in §127 and the global-catalog-not-filtered-by-SubjectID behavior noted in §131) happened to return Pega's full, un-truncated action catalog. That live response confirmed **"Drop Off Reason Diagnostic Alert"** and **"High Value Application Callback Task"** as the real titles — not "...Diagnostic Action" or "...Callback" as first guessed — so both were corrected before shipping. `"Application Drop-Off SMS Nudge"` did not appear in that live response at all, the same situation as Elena's not-yet-live `Equipment Financing Nudge` (§134/§133): left as inferred, and safely excused from stage-advancement by the existing "never actually shown" rule rather than blocking Sam's Awareness stage forever.

Dismissed it → advanced to Consideration (`currentStageIndex` 0 → 1), which correctly showed all 3 of its actions (`Mortgage Rate Lock Reminder`, `Drop Off Reason Diagnostic Alert`, `High Value Application Callback Task`), each rendering its own illustration correctly. Dismissed all 3 → advanced to Decision (`currentStageIndex` → 2), which correctly showed both `Application Resume Deep Link` and `Stalled Step Email Follow Up`. Confirmed `mobile-app.html` (adopt-only, no changes needed there beyond the mirrored data) picked up the same shared session state and rendered the same Decision-stage card with no console errors. Zero console errors throughout on either surface (aside from pre-existing, unrelated 401s from other third-party calls already known to fail in this sandbox).

## 139.1. Fix: "Exit-Intent Save Prompt" Illustration Wasn't Rendering (Unescaped `&` Broke the SVG)

User report, with a screenshot: the "Exit-Intent Save Prompt" card showed a broken-image icon instead of its illustration.

**Root cause**: the new illustration's SVG (added in §139) had a raw `<text>...>Save & exit</text>` — a bare `&` is invalid inside XML text content (must be `&amp;`). `buildSceneIllustration` feeds this content into `svgDataUri`, which just URL-encodes the string as-is (`encodeURIComponent`) without validating it's well-formed XML first, so the malformed SVG was embedded directly into the `<img src="data:image/svg+xml;...">` — the browser's SVG parser rejected the whole document, not just that one element, so nothing in the illustration rendered at all.

**Fix, `js/data.js` and `mobile-app.html`**: changed `Save & exit` to `Save &amp; exit` inside the SVG `<text>` element only — the plain-JS `cta: "Save & exit"` field elsewhere on the same offer is a normal JS string (not XML), so it was already correct and untouched.

**Verified live**: fresh login as Sam (`CUST44`), live container call succeeded — the Awareness stage's "Exit-Intent Save Prompt" card now renders its full illustration (bookmark icon, checkmark badge, "Save & exit" button) instead of a broken-image icon. Checked the rest of §139's 6 other new illustrations for the same mistake (any bare `&` in SVG text content) — none found.

- Gave Leo the same "auto-enter journey on login, stage-scoped offers only, auto-advance once stage complete, call Container API once per session and cache, retry only on a genuine failure" treatment used for Diego/Robert/Aria/Maya/the SME customers/Sam — built in as the *starting* state this time (not upgraded later), since that per-stage-view throttling risk (§127) is now a known cost of skipping it for any new stage-scoped journey.

**Fix, `js/session.js`**: added `"in-market-card-intent"` to `maybeAdvanceJourneyStage`'s journey gate — falls into the existing generic "every action in this stage must be responded to" rule (no special-cased advancement logic needed, same as the SME journeys).

**Fix, `login.html`**: added the `CARD_INTENT_CUSTOMER_ID` branch to `selectCustomer(id)`, landing Leo directly on Awareness at login, same reasoning as Sam's own branch.

Not added to `describeClause` (the Explainability panel's eligibility-clause labeler) — confirmed the SME customers aren't in there either, so an unmatched clause already falls through to a sensible generic label; no special-casing needed.

The journey card on the dashboard correctly lists both stages with all 5 actions in the right groups. **Neither stage currently shows a live offer** — none of these 5 invented action titles exist yet in the connected Pega environment's response (confirmed by dumping the live response's 31 returned titles: all belong to Sam's and the SME customers' *already-configured* actions, none of Leo's). ## 145. New: Calculators (Balance Transfer, Rewards Estimator, Travel/Miles, EMI) — Real Tool, Signal Flows to Tealium

Direct request: "Let's add the below calculators to our banking app, Balance Transfer Calculator, Rewards Estimator, Travel/Miles Calculator and EMI/installment Calculator." These are the same four trigger names already described (but never actually built) in Leo Marchetti's "In-Market Card Intent" journey (§141/§142) — e.g. "a prospect enters an outstanding balance in the BT calculator... CDH responds with a balance transfer offer."

**Clarified before building** (`AskUserQuestion`): whether using a calculator should locally fake-simulate the CDH trigger, or be a standalone tool. The actual answer given was a third option — **the calculator's inputs/outputs should flow to Pega via Tealium**, same as every other intent signal in this app, and Pega's own journey logic (not a local simulation) is what would act on it from there. Also confirmed: a shared "Calculators" hub, open to any visitor (not gated to Leo or any one customer), matching how a real bank's calculator tools work.

**New file, `calculators.html`**: a single page, `?calc=<key>` query param selects one of four calculators (`balance-transfer`, `rewards-estimator`, `travel-miles`, `emi-installment`) out of a `CALCULATORS` config object; no param shows a hub grid linking to each. Each calculator does real math (simple-interest BT savings estimate; tiered-rate cashback by spend category with a dominant-category readout; miles-per-dollar travel estimate; standard amortizing-loan EMI formula) and, on "Calculate," fires a Tealium event carrying the calculator's own inputs/outputs (e.g. `outstanding_balance`, `current_apr`, `estimated_interest_saved` for the BT calculator) via the same `utag.js` (`tags.tiqcdn.com/utag/instellars-sandbox/...`) integration used everywhere else in this app — `utag.view()` for the page view, then `utag.link()` with `event_name` set to a calculator-specific value (`balance_transfer_calculator_used`, `rewards_estimator_used`, `travel_calculator_used`, `emi_calculator_used`) plus `customer_id` and `calculator_type`. **No local "offer just appeared" simulation was added anywhere** — the existing, already-verified Container API path (live-first with cached fallback, wired for Leo since §141) remains the only way an offer can actually surface in this app; a calculator's job here ends at sending the signal.

**Customer identity resolution**: a logged-in visitor's real `session.customerId` is used, and their own session ledger gets a "Calculator used" system log entry after each calculation. An anonymous visitor (no session) falls back to Leo's id (`CARD_INTENT_CUSTOMER_ID`) in the Tealium payload only — same precedent as `new-customer-application.html`'s use of Sam's id as an anonymous-prospect stand-in — but nothing is written to Leo's actual persisted session/ledger from that fallback path, so a different physical visitor's anonymous calculator use can never pollute Leo's own demo state.

**Entry points**: a light card on `browse.html` ("Plan it out first," under the existing "New to Instellars?" banner) and a `bnk-application-card` on `dashboard.html` (next to, not replacing, the existing New Application card) — both link to `calculators.html`, both visible regardless of which customer is browsing/logged in.

**Bug caught and fixed during live verification**: the first version loaded `utag.js` *before* setting `window.utag_data`, unlike every other Tealium integration in this app (which always sets `utag_data` fields first, then loads/fires). This silently broke Tealium's own automatic field population — `utag.view()`/`utag.link()` still ran with no errors, but `window.utag_data` only ever held the 3 fields this code set itself, never the ~47 `ut.*`/`dom.*`/`tealium_*`/`cp.*` fields the real library normally merges in (confirmed by direct comparison: Robert's already-working login-time Tealium call filled 47 keys in the same browser tab where the calculator's call filled only 3). Root cause: utag.js reads whatever's already on `window.utag_data` as part of its own load-time init, so setting fields *after* it loads misses that. Fixed by reordering to set `utag_data` first, then check `window.utag` and either fire immediately or load-then-fire from `onload` — matching `enableTealiumAnalytics`/`enableLoginTealiumAnalytics`'s structure exactly.

**Verified live**: all 4 calculators produce correct math (checked by hand — e.g. $5,000 @ 22% APR over 15 months → $1,375.00 saved; $3,000 @ 14% APR over 12 months → $269.36/month EMI; category-weighted cashback → $349.20/year on a $1,220/month spend mix). After the ordering fix, a real `utag.view()`/`utag.link()` call correctly populated 50 fields on `window.utag_data`, including `qp.calc` (confirming the query param was read) and `ut.event: "view"`. Confirmed the anonymous-visitor path shows the Leo-stand-in caveat and does **not** touch Leo's real session (checked his ledger was empty immediately after). Confirmed a logged-in customer (Robert) gets their real id in the payload and a ledger entry, with no anonymous caveat shown. Confirmed both entry-point cards render and link correctly from `browse.html` and `dashboard.html`. Zero console errors throughout (aside from the pre-existing, unrelated 401 already known to occur in this sandbox). Not mirrored into `mobile-app.html`/`agent-desktop.html` — same as `new-customer-application.html`, this is a standalone flow page, not part of the shared customer/journey data model those files duplicate.

---

## 145.1. Fix: Balance Transfer Calculator (and All 4 Calculators) Appeared to Do Nothing on "Calculate"

User report: clicked Calculate on the Balance Transfer Calculator and nothing happened.

**Root cause**: the number fields showed `4200` / `24.99` as gray HTML `placeholder` text — visually indistinguishable at a glance from a real pre-filled value, but a placeholder contributes nothing to `input.value`. A user who saw those numbers and clicked Calculate without typing anything got `parseFloat("") || 0` for every field, so the calculation silently ran against all-zero inputs and produced an unremarkable **$0.00** result — which reads exactly like nothing happened, especially since the result box also renders below the fold on a normal window size. Reproduced directly: a real mouse click on Calculate with untouched fields returned `$0.00`.

**Fix, `calculators.html`**: every calculator field now also gets `value="${f.placeholder}"` (not just `placeholder=`), so all four calculators start pre-filled with real, working example numbers — clicking Calculate immediately produces a meaningful result even before the customer types anything, and the numbers render in normal black text (not placeholder gray) so it's visually clear they're real, editable values.

**Verified live**: confirmed via `input.value` (not just visual inspection) that fields are genuinely populated, not just showing placeholder text. Clicked Calculate with untouched default values on both the Balance Transfer Calculator ($4,200 @ 24.99% → correctly $1,311.97 saved over 15 months) and the Rewards Estimator ($349.20/year, dominant category "groceries") — both produced immediate, correct, non-zero results. Zero console errors (aside from the pre-existing, unrelated 401 already known in this sandbox). Note for future verification in this environment: the preview browser tool cached an earlier fetch of `calculators.html?calc=...` across "fresh" navigations to the same URL during this fix — a cache-busting query param (or a genuinely new tab) was needed to confirm the edit had actually taken effect; not a real-world issue, but worth remembering next time a fix to an already-visited URL doesn't seem to show up.

---

## 145.2. Calculators: `utag.js` Now Loads on Page Load, Not Lazily on First "Calculate" Click

Direct request, after confirming with the user that `utag.js` was only loading lazily on the first "Calculate" click: "let's load it on page load."

**Fix, `calculators.html`**: moved the script-insertion logic out of `fireCalculatorTealiumEvent` (previously called only from `handleCalculate`) to the top-level page script, firing immediately once `customer`/`calcType` are known — same "load early" shape as `enableTealiumAnalytics`/`enableLoginTealiumAnalytics` elsewhere in this app, which load on page load rather than waiting for a user action. `window.utag_data` is still set *before* the script tag is inserted (the ordering fix from §145.1 — utag.js reads whatever's already on `window.utag_data` as part of its own init). An initial `utag.view()` page view fires once the script is ready, for the hub (`calculator_type: null`) or whichever specific calculator's page. `fireCalculatorTealiumEvent` is now much smaller — it just awaits a `utagReady` promise (already resolved in the common case) and calls `utag.link()` with the calculator-specific payload on "Calculate."

**Verified live**, directly against the user's own running server (`localhost:8000`), not just a temporary preview: `window.utag` is `object` immediately after page load, before any click — both on a specific calculator's page and on the hub. `window.utag_data` already has all 50 real Tealium-populated fields (confirming a genuine load, not a stub) before "Calculate" is ever clicked. Clicking Calculate afterward still works correctly (verified via the visible result + Tealium signal note). Zero console errors (aside from the pre-existing, unrelated 401 already known in this sandbox).

## 145.3. Calculators' `utag.link()` Payload Changed to Match `new-customer-application.html`'s Exactly

Direct request: "make the utag.link call for calculator exactly same as the one for Application only the event name will be different."

**Fix, `calculators.html`**: `fireCalculatorTealiumEvent`'s `.link()` payload no longer carries the per-calculator computed fields (`outstanding_balance`, `current_apr`, `estimated_interest_saved`, etc.) — it now sends exactly the same 5 fields as `new-customer-application.html`'s `fireTealiumViewAndLink`: `event_name`, `product_type`, `customer_id`, `page_active_time: 30`, `PercentageProgress: 60`. Only `event_name` (one of the four calculator-specific values) and the natural values of `product_type` (the calculator's own title, e.g. "Balance Transfer Calculator") and `customer_id` differ per calculator — everything else is byte-for-byte identical to the Application flow's call. Each calculator's `calculate()` function still returns its own `linkFields` (kept for a possible future use) but they're no longer read by the Tealium call itself.

**Verified live**, against the user's own running server (`localhost:8000`), by monkey-patching `window.utag.link` to capture the real argument passed on a genuine "Calculate" click:
```json
{"event_name":"balance_transfer_calculator_used","product_type":"Balance Transfer Calculator","customer_id":"CUST55","page_active_time":30,"PercentageProgress":60}
```
Matches the requested shape exactly. Calculator result and signal note still render correctly; zero new console errors.

Personal Loan Calculator: $15,000 @ 11.5%/36mo → $494.64/month, also correctly resolved to Naomi. Captured the real `utag.link()` payload via monkey-patching on both new calculators — both matched the required 5-field shape exactly, e.g. Home Loan: `{"event_name":"home_loan_calculator_used","product_type":"Home Loan Calculator","customer_id":"CUST56","page_active_time":30,"PercentageProgress":60}`.
