## 150. New "Gap Scan" Page (iREA Gap Scan Report) in the Explore-Other-Views Menu

Direct request: add a page, reachable from the menu, that shows a report like the shared "iREA Gap Scan: Missed CDH Opportunities" one; then "use the attached [irea_gap_scan_sample_bank.html] and create the page."

**Background:** the shared claude.ai artifact is a signed-in Dashboard-type page whose data wasn't readable without signing in as the user, so the attached HTML file is the source of truth. It is a self-contained report (KPI cards; value-by-journey, coverage-by-journey and coverage-by-tier charts; Top-10 and All-actions tables with filters/sort/CSV export; "data needed to price the rest"; bank-only actions; Print/PDF; a "Partial counts as" control; and a **Load scan CSV** button that swaps in a real scan produced by `apply_review.py`'s `dashboard_gap_actions.csv`). Its data is synthetic ("Sample Bank", 52 iREA actions across 11 journeys, 250,000 customers) and the page shows a banner saying so.

**Fix:**
- `gap-scan-report.html` (new): the attached file, byte-for-byte. Kept as its own file because it defines global element styles (`body`, `h1`, `button`, `table`, `th`, `td`, `section`) and its own light/dark theme that would clash with `css/app.css` if inlined.
- `gap-scan.html` (new): the site shell (same `bnk-header`, "Back to dashboard" link, `icon("search")` pill as `data-sources.html`) with the report in a same-origin iframe. The iframe is sized to the report's `body` height via `ResizeObserver` (plus `change`/`input` listeners), so the page scrolls once. Do NOT measure `documentElement.scrollHeight` for this, it never drops below the frame's own height, so the frame can grow but never shrink. The frame needs `flex: none`; `flex: 1` makes the flex-basis 0 and ignores the JS-set height.
- `dashboard.html`: "Gap Scan" item added to the "Explore other views" dropdown, after Data Sources (opens in a new tab like its siblings). It is not added to `mobile-app.html` or `agent-desktop.html`.

**Follow-up:** the scan that produces this report was built next, see §151.

**Verified live:** `gap-scan.html` renders in the shell: 4 KPI cards, 3 charts, 10 rows in Top 10, 52 in All actions, no console errors, lede reads "16 of the 52 iREA actions in full and 8 in part... about $1.9M a year". Filtering coverage to Missing drops the table to 28 rows and the frame shrinks from 11,548px to 9,179px, restoring when cleared; no nested scrollbar. Logged in as Sam (CUST44), the dashboard dropdown lists Gap Scan -> `gap-scan.html`.

## 151. Gap Scan Engine: Two-Dataset Comparison Built Into the Codebase

Direct request: "We need to also have the validation that produces this scan in this code base", then clarified: "there will be two datasets, one what we are comparing and the other what we are comparing against."

**Model:** REFERENCE (compared against) vs BANK (compared). Reference defaults to this site's own Pega CDH application (IREA): every journey/action in `js/data.js`, built live (`JOURNEYS` + `OFFER_CATALOG`, 101 actions across 14 journeys at time of writing), and can be swapped for an uploaded file. Bank is an uploaded CSV/JSON of the other bank's CDH actions. Everything runs in the browser; nothing is uploaded anywhere.

**Files:**
- `js/gap-scan-engine.js` (new, pure functions, `window.GapScan`): `buildSiteReference(JOURNEYS, OFFER_CATALOG)` (the two consts must be passed in; top-level `const` in data.js is not a window property), `parseUpload` (CSV or JSON), `normaliseBank` / `normaliseReference` (header aliases, so Pega-style pyName/pyIssue/pyGroup work; duplicates and unnamed rows warned/skipped; no usable rows is an error), `runScan`, `bankTemplateCsv`, `referenceCsv`.
- `gap-scan.html`: now has a "Run a scan" panel above the report iframe: step 1 reference (this site / upload, download as CSV), step 2 bank file (upload, "Use the sample file", template download), step 3 settings (bank name, customer count, optional data-signals list) and an editable valuation-assumptions table. Result is handed to the report via `iframe.contentWindow.loadScanData(result)`; the panel collapses with the outcome in its title bar.
- `gap-scan-report.html`: added `window.loadScanData(d)` and a "compared against ..." note in the meta line; the "How these numbers are made" text rewritten to describe this engine (the original claimed a CDH specialist confirms every match, which is not true here).
- `gap-scan-sample-bank-cdh.csv` (new): 28-action sample bank file, served as the "Use the sample file" button's source.

**How the scan works:**
- **Match:** names are lower-cased, banking synonyms folded (FD/CD/time deposit -> one token; OLB/online banking; eStatements/paperless; heloc; 2FA; ...), stop-words dropped, light stemming. Score = 0.65 x recall of the reference action's words + 0.35 x precision, with generic words (card, loan, account, reward, credit, savings...) weighted 0.4 and a requirement to share at least one distinctive word. +0.1 when issue/group overlaps the reference's group/journey/stage; a small raw-word bonus as tie-break. Threshold 0.5 to match, below 0.65 marked Partial ("name only loosely matches"). Names only: bank descriptions are deliberately not scored (they diluted scores). Inactive/retired bank actions never count as coverage.
- **Coverage:** Covered / Partial / Missing. Partial reasons: missing channels, no suitability check, narrower audience (<90% of the reference's reach), loose name. Channels, suitability and audience are judged ONLY when both datasets provide them (the site-derived reference has no channels, and only one action has a suitability description, so those checks mostly stay silent unless the reference is uploaded with them).
- **Value:** per action, customers x reach% x response rate x annual value per converted customer; the two ranges are sampled triangularly 2,000 times (seeded by action id, so reruns are identical) for P10/P50/P90. Value basis and tier (Service/Wellness/Retention/Sales) for site actions are keyword heuristics (`deriveBasis`, `deriveTier`), overridable via the `value_basis` / `pillar` columns of an uploaded reference. **The assumptions (`DEFAULT_ASSUMPTIONS`) are made-up placeholders**, editable on the page; do not quote the dollar figures as real.
- **Needs data:** only if the user lists the signals the bank has. Missing actions whose rule needs an unlisted signal are shown unpriced. Site-derived required signals come from a small keyword map (`deriveSignals`: loans/cards -> CreditScore, insurance -> HasInsurancePolicy, QR/merchant -> HasMerchantServices) and are an assumption, not read from Pega rules.
- **Bank-only actions:** bank actions not matched to any reference action.

**Known limits:** matching is lexical, not semantic; one bank action can cover several reference actions (many-to-one, same as the original sample); no Interaction History audience overlap (only an optional `audience_pct` column); no live Pega Container API call for the reference (it reads the app's own journey definitions, so actions Pega does not currently return still count as reference actions).

**Verified live** (served over http, in the browser pane): reference 101 actions / 14 journeys; sample bank -> 9 covered, 4 partial, 88 missing, 18 bank-only, P50 about $6.6M; the matches read sensibly after tightening (an earlier version matched "Lost Card Replacement" to "Cash Rewards Card Offer" on the shared word "card"; fixed with generic-word weighting). Also checked: scan is deterministic across runs; channel + suitability + audience gaps produce the expected Partial note; Pega-style JSON headers load; a file with no name column gives a clear error; the reference CSV round-trips (101 -> 101); the signals list produces "needs data" rows and feeds; no console errors; no horizontal overflow at 320px.

## 152. IREA Master Dataset (Pega Action Catalog Export) Becomes the Gap Scan Reference

Direct request: attached `IREA_ActionCatalog_20261117T111407.427 GMT.xlsx`: "the IREA Master Dataset which is what other bank data will be compared against."

**What the file is:** a real Pega CDH action catalog export with four sheets: `Action Properties` (Action, Field, Value, Type; one action after another, no instance column), `Treatment Details` (Action, Channel Group, Channel ID, Treatment, Field, Value), `Engagement Policies` (Action, Type, Logic, Conditions) and `Contact Policies` (2 rows). It holds **110 actions** under **80 action paths** (a path such as `/Acquire/Lending/PersonalLoanOffer` holds several actions: Credit Hacks, Easy Credit, Flexi Loan, Personal Loan Offer; Scratch & Win's path holds 17), 109 active (Lifestyle Card Recommendation has `pyIsPropositionActive = Never`).

**Parsing, `GapScan.parseActionCatalog(raw, {journeyByTitle})`** in `js/gap-scan-engine.js` (one parser for the embedded copy and for an uploaded .xlsx):
- Property rows are split into one block per action: a field repeating under the same path means the next action started. (Do not key on `pxIdentifier`; it is the same for every action under a path.)
- Issue/group come from `pyIssue`/`pyGroup`; tier is exact from the issue (Acquire=Sales, Engage=Service, Nurture=Wellness, Retain=Retention); name is `pyLabel`.
- Channels = `InboundChannels` + `OutboundChannels` + channel IDs of that action's treatments (matched by treatment name/label).
- Eligibility / suitability / applicability come from `Engagement Policies`, shortened to e.g. `IsFinanciallyVulnerable is false; ComplianceHoldFlag is false`. The universal "All Issue All Group Customer Eligibility" row is ignored (it applies to every action).
- **Required signals are real**: every `Customer.X` field read by an action's policies (71 distinct, e.g. CreditScore, FSSummaryMLP.AbandonedApplication) with its label. This replaced the earlier keyword guess (`deriveSignals`, now only used by the legacy site-derived reference). A "needs data" decision compares on the last path segment, case-insensitively.
- Journey: where an action's title matches an action in this site's `JOURNEYS`, it is grouped under that site journey and stage; otherwise under "Issue / Group". The report's journey filter therefore mixes both naming styles.
- Value basis is still a keyword heuristic, but the action's own name decides first, then its group, then its benefit text (the group alone mis-priced Scratch & Win mission actions as wealth advisory).

**Files / changes:**
- `js/irea-master-catalog.js` (new): the export trimmed to the fields the scan reads (1,272 property rows, 531 treatment rows, 224 policy rows); operator/system fields are left out. Exposes `window.IREA_MASTER_RAW` (with `source`, `exportedAt`). Works from file:// as well as http.
- `gap-scan.html`: **IREA master dataset is now the default reference** (the site-derived reference option was removed from the UI; `buildSiteReference` remains, used only to map titles to this site's journeys). Reference options: master / upload a newer catalog export (.xlsx) / upload CSV or JSON. The bank upload also accepts a Pega catalog export (.xlsx), converted with `catalogToBankRows`. Excel reading uses SheetJS 0.18.5, loaded on demand from cdnjs, so an .xlsx upload needs internet access.
- New setting **Channel check**: "Partial only if no channel in common" (default), strict "all reference channels", or off. Strict left only 1 of 109 covered on the sample bank, because a reference action lists every channel it can use.
- Match ranking uses the uncapped score (capping at 1.0 let a near-name win a tie against an exact name); only the displayed `match_score` is capped.

**Verified live (browser pane):** master parses to 110 actions (109 active, 23 issue/groups, 80 paths, 110 unique ids, 34 with suitability rules, 110 with channels); the "1 inactive action left out" notice shows; the real .xlsx uploaded both as reference and as bank parses to the same 110/109 and loads SheetJS; **self-comparison (catalog against itself) = 109 of 109 covered, 0 partial, 0 missing**; sample bank CSV vs master = 4 covered, 17 partial, 88 missing (1 / 20 / 88 with the strict channel rule); no console errors.

**Open items:** value assumptions remain placeholders (see §151); `Contact Policies` are exported but not used; no per-action reach % from the catalog, so audience checks need an `audience_pct` column in an uploaded CSV; the master snapshot is dated 2026-11-17 and goes stale as the Pega app changes (upload a fresh export on the page to refresh without a code change, or regenerate `js/irea-master-catalog.js`).

## 153. Gap Scan: Treatment, Engagement Policy and Contact Policy Comparison (When Rules Not Available)

Direct request: "I think we should build in a Treatment, Engagement Policies, Contact Policies and When rule comparison also."

**What the catalog export actually contains (checked before building):** Treatment Details (167 treatments attach to the 110 actions; fields incl. channel, active flag, weight, dates, placement, push/SMS/email message, email subject, click-through and image URL, treatment eligibility conditions, all mostly empty in the master); Engagement Policies (Action Eligibility / Suitability / Applicability plus group-level eligibility, 281 distinct conditions, every one of the form "From Customer <field> <operator> <value>"); Contact Policies (2 rows: "If there are 1 Rejects for treatments, suppress the action for 7 days"). **There is no When rule in the export**: no sheet, no field, and no policy references one. So When rules are NOT compared (the report says so). The condition model carries a `source` per condition so a When-rule source can be added without changing the comparison, but the data has to come from somewhere (a separate export) first.

**Engine (`js/gap-scan-engine.js`):**
- `parseCondition` / `readConditions`: structured conditions `{field, label, op, value, text, source}` with operators = != > >= < <= in null. `parseContactPolicy`: `{trigger, suppress, key, text}`.
- `parseActionCatalog` now also returns `row.detail = {treatments[], conditions{eligibility, suitability, applicability}, contactPolicies[]}`. Treatments are segmented from the Treatment Details rows (a repeated field under the same action/channel/treatment starts the next) and attached to an action by treatment name or label. **The export has one policy row per action instance under a path, so a path that holds several actions (e.g. PersonalLoanOffer: 4 actions) repeats identical rows; conditions and contact policies are de-duplicated per path and shared by every action on that path** (rows carry no instance id, so per-instance attribution is not possible). 10 of the 177 treatment records do not attach to any action (name mismatch on bundle actions) and are ignored.
- `compareDetails(ref, bank)` compares a matched pair: **Treatments** per channel (missing channel / inactive / no message / no subject / no link / no image / expired end date / extra channel), **Eligibility, Suitability, Applicability** by customer field (then operator and value: Same / Different threshold / Missing in bank / Extra in bank), **Contact policy** by trigger and suppression. Result statuses: Same, Different, Missing in bank, Extra in bank.
- `runScan` returns `details[]` (one row per check) and `detailSummary` (`available`, `comparedPairs`, counts per area and status); each action row gets `detail_gaps` (Different + Missing count). Detail only exists when BOTH sides carry it, i.e. the bank file is a catalog export (.xlsx); a CSV/JSON bank gives `available: false` and the report says so. Coverage (Covered/Partial/Missing) is unchanged; the rule/treatment differences are a separate layer.
- Same Pega path id between reference and bank adds a +0.02 match tie-break (the master has same-named actions, e.g. two "Pre-Approved Loan Offer").
- `js/irea-master-catalog.js` was regenerated with the treatment fields the comparison reads (now about 418 KB).

**Report (`gap-scan-report.html`):** new section "Treatments, policies and contact rules" (stacked bar per area, filters for area / result / search, default "Needs attention", CSV export, first 400 rows shown), a "Rule gaps" column in All actions, and a methodology bullet. **Page (`gap-scan.html`):** "Use the sample catalog (.xlsx)" button. **`gap-scan-sample-bank-catalog.xlsx`** (new) is a made-up "other bank" catalog in the export layout: about a third of the actions removed, no Push treatments, thresholds loosened (DTI 36 to 45, 180 to 90 days), every 4th policy missing its last condition, one contact policy altered and one removed, 3 bank-only actions. It is generated from the master by a script, not a real bank's data.

**Verified live:** the master compared with itself gives 447 checks, 0 differences (after the id tie-break); deliberately altered copies produce exactly the expected findings (inactive Web treatment, missing Push treatment, dropped IsProspect condition, DTI 36 vs 45 as "Different", missing and extra contact policies); a CSV bank shows the "not compared" message and hides the table; the real .xlsx loaded through the page as bank (self-comparison 361 checks after de-duplication, all Same); the sample catalog gives 89 covered / 9 partial / 11 missing and 347 checks (53 needing attention); no console errors.

**Open items:** When rules (need a data source); treatment text is compared for presence only, not wording; weights, dates and placement are read but only an expired end date is flagged; per-instance policy attribution on multi-action paths; group-level eligibility is folded into each action's Eligibility.

## 154. Gap Scan: Financial Impact From the iREA for Banks Value Calculator

Direct request: "Can we use the attached [iREA_for_Banks_Value_Calculator.xlsx] to determine the financial impact determined using Gap Scan."

**What the workbook is:** 82 MVP actions (Action Value sheet: base Retail/SME, eligible %, primary channel, uplift, value per outcome, contacts per year, incentive per outcome, value type) plus an Assumptions sheet (Retail 2,000,000 and SME 100,000 base, Low/Base/High multiplier 0.5/1/1.5, 25% overlap discount, 40 actions in Wave 1, Year-1 realisation 60% / 20%, package price USD 234,000, licence and run cost 0, channel reach and cost per contact) and a Business Case sheet (3-year view, ROI, payback). Every figure in it is an illustrative default, not a bank's data.

**New file `js/gap-scan-calculator.js` (`window.GapScanCalc`):** the 82 actions and defaults embedded as data, plus the workbook's own maths:
- `economics(action, model, mult)`: eligible = base x eligible %; reached = eligible x channel reach; outcomes = reached x uplift x multiplier; net = outcomes x value - reached x contacts x cost per contact - outcomes x incentive.
- `plan(model)`: Low/Base/High net per action, rank by Base net, Wave 1 = top N (or an explicit wave override), totals per wave.
- `threeYear(w1, w2, model)`: Year 1 = (W1 x 60% + W2 x 20%) x (1 - overlap); Years 2-3 = (W1 + W2) x (1 - overlap); costs = package + licence + run; ROI = net / cost; payback.
- `parseWorkbook(sheets)`: reads a bank's filled-in copy of the calculator (Action Value and Assumptions, found by label so row positions can move) and returns a model.
- Verified: defaults reproduce the workbook exactly (all-action net 7,493,481.4; Wave 1 6,449,215; Wave 2 1,044,266.4; Year 1 3,058,786.71; run-rate after overlap 5,620,111.05; ROI 60.1; payback 0.92 months), and parsing the real workbook gives the same totals. The source workbook is kept in the project root as `iREA_for_Banks_Value_Calculator.xlsx` (it is the file to upload in the Valuation model panel, and the source the embedded data in `js/gap-scan-calculator.js` was generated from).

**Engine (`js/gap-scan-engine.js`):** `runScan(..., {calculator: model})` values each reference action with the calculator instead of the placeholder ranges.
- `mapCalculator` pairs calculator actions with reference actions by name (exact name, else the existing banking-synonym similarity at the loose 0.65 threshold), one to one, best score first, so an action is never valued twice. 63 of the calculator's 82 actions map to the 109-action master; 19 do not (they come from the iREA deck and are not in the Pega catalog, e.g. Kill Switch Awareness, Money Mule Warning) and cannot be a gap. 46 master actions are not in the calculator and are shown as "Not in calculator" (not valued).
- A valued row gets P10/P50/P90 = Low/Base/High scenario net value, `eligible_pct`, `customers_to_treat` (eligible customers), and extra columns `calc_action`, `calc_value_type`, `calc_channel`, `calc_wave`, `calc_rank`. `value_basis` is `not-in-calculator` when unmatched.
- `result.financial` carries the settings, the matched / unmatched counts and the all-action Low/Base/High totals. The placeholder model is still available (and is used when no calculator is passed).

**Page (`gap-scan.html`):** "Valuation model" panel: radio iREA Value Calculator (default) or Simple placeholder ranges; for the calculator, upload a filled-in `.xlsx`, download the inputs as CSV, reset to defaults, edit the settings, scenario multipliers, channel reach/cost and every action's inputs. The "customers" field now applies to the placeholder model only. The scan summary says how many actions were valued.

**Report (`gap-scan-report.html`):** new "Financial impact of the gaps" section (Low / Base / High): gap run-rate before overlap split by Wave 1 and 2, overlap discount, run-rate after overlap, Year 1/2/3 benefit, 3-year benefit, cost, net benefit, ROI, payback, with notes on what is and is not valued. A fifth KPI "Net run-rate after overlap"; a Wave column in All actions; the meta line and the Value methodology bullet switch to the calculator wording. Partial actions use the existing share control. Missing and (share of) Partial actions feed the wave totals.

**Verified live:** sample catalog vs master = 89/9/11, 18 gap actions valued (2 not in the calculator), Base gap run-rate $1.20M, after 25% overlap $900K, 3-year net $2.08M, ROI 8.9x, payback 5.5 months; editing overlap to 30% and licence to 50,000 changes the table as the formulas predict (3-year cost 234,000 + 3 x 50,000 = 384,000); switching to placeholder mode hides the section and restores the old KPIs and text; no console errors; the page itself does not overflow at 375 px.

**Caveats / open items:** all calculator inputs are illustrative until the bank's own figures replace them; the package price is the cost of the whole iREA scope, so the ROI is a size-of-gap guide, not a quote for closing only these gaps; calculator actions that have no equivalent in the master (19) are left out; wave assignment uses the calculator's ranking of all 82 actions, not only the gaps; the report iframe's inner document measures about 6 px wider than the page at 375 px (clipped, not visible, not investigated); When-rule comparison is still open (see section 153).


## 155. Value Calculator: Rows Added to the Action Value Sheet So Every Master-Catalog Action Has a Value

Direct requests: after being told 46 of the 109 master actions showed "Not in calculator", "Add rows to the calculator"; then, after a first version put them on a new sheet, "no need for the new tab we can add it to the Action Value tab itself."

**What was added:** 46 rows (numbered 83 to 128, sheet rows 87 to 132) at the bottom of the **Action Value** sheet, one for each master-catalog action that is not among the 82 MVP actions. They have the same columns and formulas as the existing rows (checked cell by cell against the row-5 pattern for all 128 rows). Two columns were added at the right: **Z = Pega action id** the row values (this is how the code recognises an added row) and **AA = basis** for the starting values. The inputs are yellow. Row 3 of the sheet now carries a grey formula guide above the calculated columns (Outcomes = K x L x M x scenario, Gross = N x O, Contact cost = K x L x Q x R, Incentive = N x T, Net = P - S - U; text only, no formulas changed). To make the sheet one table, the workbook was changed as follows: the TOTAL row moved from row 87 to row 133 and sums rows 5 to 132; every range that ended at row 86 (the rank formulas, the Wave-override validation, Business Case C8:C10, all Journey Summary roll-ups: 232 formulas) now ends at row 132; the two notes moved below the total and a third note explains the added rows; the "82" wording on Read Me, Business Case B8, Assumptions D10, the sheet title and the Journey Summary heading now says 82 MVP + 46 added. The 18 Scratch & Win / investment-account rows sit under the existing journey "Wealth Management & Advisory" so the 20 journeys of Journey Summary still cover every row.

**Consequence:** the workbook's totals now cover 128 actions. Base net value of all actions is 8,376,411 (7,493,481.4 for the 82 plus 882,930 for the added rows); Wave 1 (top 40 by net value) is 6,457,715 instead of 6,449,215, because the added "Time Deposit" row (62,400) outranks the 40th MVP action (54,000) and pushes it into Wave 2. The Business Case, Journey Summary and ROI figures in the workbook therefore differ from the original 82-action file, which is still in the user's Downloads folder.

**The starting values are mine, not the bank's.** They are chosen by analogy with the closest MVP action (personal-loan variants use the Pre-Approved Loan margin of USD 400 with a smaller uplift; wealth items follow the advisory actions; the 21 Scratch & Win steps are low-value engagement messages with a few referral and voucher steps carrying a value and an incentive; SME items use the SME base). The rows live in the workbook and in `ADDED_RAW` in `js/gap-scan-calculator.js` (keep the two in step). They must be replaced with the bank's figures (priority: eligible % and value per outcome). The workbook was written with openpyxl, so it has no cached values until Excel opens it (calculation on load is switched on); LibreOffice was not available here to recalculate it.

**Code:** `js/gap-scan-calculator.js` embeds the 46 rows (`ADDED_RAW`, each with `added: true` and `refId`). `plan()` ranks all 128 actions together for the Wave 1 cut, as the workbook does. `parseWorkbook` reads the added rows from the Action Value sheet and marks a row as added when column Z holds an id. `mapCalculator` in the engine pins an added row to its Pega action id first (this keeps the two same-named "Pre-Approved Loan Offer" actions apart), then falls back to the name match. New result column `calc_added`; `financial.mvpActions`.

**Page and report wording:** the Valuation model text and the report's notes say the calculator has 82 MVP actions plus added rows, how many valued gaps use added rows (estimates to confirm with the bank), and that waves follow the ranking of all actions.

**Verified live:** with the defaults all 109 master actions map and are valued, none "Not in calculator", no negative values. Uploading the project workbook through the page gives 128 actions, 46 of them added, with zero field differences against the embedded data and the same totals. The sample catalog scan values all 20 gap actions (2 from added rows): gap Base 1.23M, after overlap 923K, 3-year net 2.12M, ROI 9.1x (before the added rows: 18 valued, 1.20M, 8.9x). No console errors.

**Open items:** the 46 sets of inputs need bank sign-off; the 19 calculator actions that are not in the master are still never a gap; a bank workbook without the added rows leaves master actions outside its 82 unvalued.

## 156. Sample Bank in the Catalog Layout, With Treatment, Engagement Policy and Contact Policy Tabs

Direct request: "Can you update the sample bank file with treatment, engagement-policy, contact-policy detail other tabs as the catalog file."

**What changed:** the 28-action sample bank (`gap-scan-sample-bank-cdh.csv`, a flat file that cannot carry tabs) now also exists as `gap-scan-sample-bank-cdh.xlsx`, laid out like a Pega action catalog export: **Action Properties** (label, name, issue, group, id, Always/Never active flag from the CSV status, description, inbound/outbound channels from the CSV channels), **Treatment Details** (one treatment per channel the bank uses, 50 treatments, with message text, email subject, click-through link and image where a channel carries them; a few deliberately inactive, without text, without an image, or expired), **Engagement Policies** (27 conditions across Action Eligibility, Suitability and Applicability) and **Contact Policies** (3). The CSV is kept (the Gap Scan page still has "Use the sample CSV (actions only)").

**How the detail was made (it is made up, like the rest of the sample):** for each of the 12 bank actions that the scan matches to a master action, the bank's eligibility, suitability and applicability conditions are copied from that master action and then changed in a repeatable way: every third action loses its last check, thresholds are loosened, the CSV's own suitability ("Credit score above 650") is added as a condition, and Personal Loan Pre-Approved gets a debt-to-income limit of 45 against the reference's 36. Bank-only actions get one simple eligibility condition. Contact policies: NTB Welcome Series suppresses for 30 days where the reference says 7; Birthday Message and Direct Deposit Bonus have policies the reference does not. Note the Pega export numbers every policy line "E1, E2..." whatever the type (a line starting "S1" is not read), and the generator follows that. The CSV's `audience_pct` has no place in the catalog layout, so it is not carried over. The generator is a throwaway script, not part of the project; edit the workbook directly to change the sample.

**Page (`gap-scan.html`):** new button "Use the sample bank (.xlsx, with policies)", next to "Use the sample CSV (actions only)" and "Use the sample catalog (.xlsx)" (the master-derived one).

**Verified live:** the sample bank loads as 28 actions (1 retired, left out) with 50 treatments, 27 conditions and 3 contact policies; the scan compares 21 matched actions on 144 checks (30 Same, 4 Different, 46 Missing in bank, 64 Extra in bank): for example DTI 36 versus 45, an applicability operator difference, and the 7 versus 30 day suppression. Coverage is 5 covered, 16 partial, 88 missing. The calculator valuation and financial section still show. No console errors.

**Note:** most reference treatments in the master carry no message text, link or image, so treatment differences are mainly missing or extra channels rather than missing content.

## 157. Gap Scan Review Screen (Confirm or Change Each Automatic Match)

**Asked:** "Lets build the review screen." The matcher is automatic, so a CDH specialist needs to confirm or correct each match, and every figure should follow their decision.

**What was added**

- `js/gap-scan-engine.js`: each action row now carries `candidates` (top 3 `[bank action name, score]`, score ≥ 0.3) and the result carries `bankActions` (the bank's catalog, used to fill the re-pair list).
- `gap-scan-report.html`: new section **Review matches** (`#sec-review`, between the top-actions and treatments/policies sections). One row per reference action with the automatic result, a Coverage selector (Covered / Partial / Missing), a Bank action selector (re-pair), a free-text note and a Confirmed checkbox. Row edge: orange = changed, green = confirmed.
  - Filter: "Worth a check" (default: the Top 10 actions to build first (`TOPSET`, taken from `M.top`, because their "not built" claim carries the most value and most have no close candidate), partial matches, covered with match score < 0.8, missing with a candidate ≥ 0.4; 36 of 109 in the sample run), All, Not reviewed, Reviewed, Changed; plus a search box. The list is capped at 150 rows.
  - `model()` applies decisions (`DEC[action_id|action]`) over coverage, bank action and gap note, so the KPIs, charts, value, financial impact and the action tables all follow. Setting Missing clears the bank action; choosing a bank action on a Missing row makes it Covered; clearing the bank action makes it Missing. Extra row fields: `auto_coverage`, `auto_bank`, `changed`, `review`, `reviewer_note`.
  - A "Review" column in All actions and a "N of M matches reviewed, K changed" chip in the header line.
  - Decisions are kept in `localStorage` under `irea-gapscan-review:<bank>|<reference>|<action count>` (try/catch, so blocked storage only loses persistence). Export review (CSV) writes `irea_gap_scan_review_<bank>.csv` (action_id, action, auto and final coverage and bank action, review_status, reviewer_note, values); Import review reads it back (needs `action_id` and `review_status`); Clear review asks for confirmation.
  - Rule (treatment / policy) checks were compared against the automatic match, so actions whose bank action was changed (`REPAIRED`) are left out of that section and a note says how many.
  - Methodology gets a "Review" bullet.

**Verified (local server, sample bank workbook, built-in browser):** 27 of 109 actions listed as "Worth a check"; re-pairing one missing action to a bank action moved the headline from Missing 88 to 87 and Covered 5 to 6, cut Missing value from $6.7M to $6.4M, showed "1 of 109 matches reviewed, 1 changed" in the header, and set the row to Covered with the orange edge. Export contained the changed row with `Changed`. Re-running the scan restored the decision from storage; Clear review returned to 0 changed. No console errors. In a very narrow pane (253 px) two existing filter selects (`#f-journey`, `#f-dstatus`) overflow; the Review section itself does not.

**Not covered:** the review is per browser (no shared store); When rules still have no data source; a re-paired action has no rule comparison.
